Scope: only the blog files above were provided. `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, `BlogJsonLd.tsx`, `RelatedArticles` and any API/MCP/CLI surface were not shown. Findings about those are marked as inferred and need a quick check.

## 1. Blog layout declares hreflang for locales that have no translation
`fe-next/app/[locale]/blog/layout.tsx:46-72`

The layout's `alternates` is inherited by every blog article unless `generateBlogMetadata` overrides it. It points all 6 locales, plus about 17 region variants, at `/{loc}/blog`. Most articles exist in only 1–2 languages. `milat-hayom-habit` is Hebrew-only, `leaderboard-elo-explained` is EN-only, and `juegos-palabras-gratis` is ES-only.

Where Next merges metadata, the child's `alternates` replaces the parent's, so this may be harmless. Hreflang cluster inconsistencies between the index and the articles still waste crawl budget. The region variants (`en-JP`, `en-SE`, `es-AR`…) have no region-specific content, which is noise. Google ignores these or flags them as conflicting, and AI crawlers gain nothing from them.

```diff
-        'en-IL': ..., 'he-IL': ..., 'en-US': ..., 'es-US': ..., 'en-GB': ..., 'en-SE': ...,
-        'sv-SE': ..., 'en-JP': ..., 'ja-JP': ..., 'en-ES': ..., 'es-ES': ..., 'en-MX': ...,
-        'es-MX': ..., 'en-AU': ..., 'es-AR': ..., 'es-CO': ...
```

Keep only the 6 language codes plus `x-default`.

## 2. The blog-wide ItemList lists every article in every locale
`fe-next/app/[locale]/blog/layout.tsx:109-153`

- The list claims `/he/blog/free-word-games-online` and `/ja/blog/leaderboard-elo-explained`, which are noindex fallbacks. Structured data pointing at noindex URLs is a contradictory signal.
- `name` is hardcoded, and it is truncated at line 126 (`'Netflix Just Dropped a Word Game — And It'`).
- The array duplicates the content-registry data, so it will drift. The `RelatedArticles` lists in the PageClient files already show this drift.

```diff
-    { slug: 'netflix-word-game-2026-rise', name: 'Netflix Just Dropped a Word Game — And It' },
+    { slug: 'netflix-word-game-2026-rise', name: 'Netflix Just Dropped a Word Game — And It Is Changing Word Games' },
```

Filter the list by the locales each slug is actually published in. Add a `locales: string[]` field to each entry and use `blogArticles.filter(a => a.locales.includes(locale))`.

## 3. Mismatched `dateModified`: future-dated values and a visible date that disagrees with the schema
- `hebrew-word-games-guide/page.tsx:15` has `DATE_MODIFIED = '2026-09-29'`.
- `leaderboard-elo-explained/page.tsx:14` has `DATE_PUBLISHED = '2026-08-03'`.
- `hebrew-word-games-guide/PageClient.tsx:107` shows `2025-10-24`.
- `improve-word-game-skills/PageClient.tsx:107` shows `2025-09-15`, while `page.tsx:15` says modified `2026-05-19`.

Dates later than the crawl date, or that disagree with the visible byline, make freshness signals untrustworthy. AI answer engines weight visible dates. Compare these dates against the current date and the real commit history.

Fix: export the dates from one module and use them in both `page.tsx` and `PageClient`, and show "Updated {dateModified}" in the UI.

```diff
-const DATE_MODIFIED = '2026-09-29';
+const DATE_MODIFIED = '2026-05-11'; // set to the real last-edit date
```

## 4. `hebrew-word-games-guide` ships JSON-LD without FAQ and cross-language content gaps
`hebrew-word-games-guide/page.tsx:53-60`

Unlike the sibling articles, this page has no `faqItems`, `keywords` or `articleSection`. It is the best-fit page for the "משחק מילים בעברית" query, which is the product's niche. It also carries visible quality problems:
- The English H1 and subtitle are fine.
- The `he` text includes machine-sounding Hebrew ("טאיל", "הצבא תורם מאוד…").
- The `he` article stays in `contentByLocale` even though its content is low quality, which hurts citation worthiness.

```diff
+import { extractFaqFromSections } from '@/utils/seo/parseFaqProse';
 ...
+  const faqItems = extractFaqFromSections(content.sections);
 ...
         dateModified={DATE_MODIFIED}
+        faqItems={faqItems}
+        keywords="Hebrew word games, משחק מילים בעברית, שורש, RTL word game"
+        articleSection="Language"
       />
```

Also add a question-style H2, for example "What is the shoresh system?", because `extractFaqFromSections` presumably keys on question-shaped headings. The current H2s are statements, so the FAQ extraction on the other pages likely returns little. Check this in `parseFaqProse`.

## 5. `ru` locale: metadata present, but content is only partly localized and hreflang is gated oddly
`free-word-games-online/page.tsx:18-42`, `content.ts:133`

`ruTranslated: 'ru' in metaTitles` is always true here. Russian metadata exists, but the Russian body contains errors: "на пяти языках" at line 168, while the English version says six languages. "Их них" is a typo at line 150. Contradictory facts between locales weaken entity consistency for AI citation. Fix the body to say six languages.

```diff
-...на пяти языках, включая иврит
+...на шести языках, включая иврит
```

## 6. Heavy `'use client'` article pages: the body depends on hydrated client components
`*/PageClient.tsx:1` and `:18-25` in each article

Each article's body is rendered inside a `'use client'` component that reads `useParams`, `useLanguage` and `useTheme`. Next does SSR client components, so the HTML usually contains the text. The risk is `useLanguage()` and `useTheme()` resolving differently on the server, which gives a hydration flash. The H1, 11 sections and ad slots also sit behind client JS. Crawlers that do not execute JS, including many LLM fetchers, only see what the SSR pass produced.

Fix: render the article body (H1, sections) in a server component and keep only the theme and ad wrappers client-side. Pass `locale` from `params`, not `useParams`:

```diff
-export default function HebrewGuidePageClient(): React.ReactElement {
-  const params = useParams();
-  const locale = (params.locale as string) || language;
+export default function HebrewGuidePageClient({ locale }: { locale: string }): React.ReactElement {
```
```diff
-      <HebrewGuidePageClient />
+      <HebrewGuidePageClient locale={locale} />
```

## 7. Related-article links are English-titled, hardcoded and non-localized
- `juegos-palabras-gratis/PageClient.tsx:34-44` shows English titles to Spanish readers.
- `milat-hayom-habit/PageClient.tsx:34-44` and `mishachke-milim-chinuch/PageClient.tsx:34-44` show English titles on Hebrew pages.
- The links often point at pages that are noindex for that locale, because the article is EN-only. That spends internal link equity on noindex targets.

Anchor text language mismatch weakens topical relevance, and the links lead to dead ends. Filter related articles to slugs that exist in the current locale.

```diff
-  const relatedArticlesData = [ ...all en... ];
+  const relatedArticlesData = allRelated.filter(a => a.locales.includes(locale));
```

## 8. Contradictory claims for citation: "no affiliate links" and ELO article tone
`free-word-games-online/content.ts:32`, and similar claims in `milat-hayom-habit/content.ts:30`

Unverifiable first-person claims fail E-E-A-T and fact-checking by AI engines:
- "several hundred thousand Israelis" (`milat-hayom-habit/content.ts:32`)
- "streak: 247 days"
- "Vanderbilt 2023 meta-analysis of 67 studies" (`mishachke-milim-chinuch/content.ts:50`)
- "40% of 5th graders…" (`mishachke-milim-chinuch/content.ts:40`)

These look fabricated or unsourced. If an LLM cites a bad stat, trust in the domain drops. The strongest cite-worthy piece is the ELO article, which is grounded in the real code. Replace uncited statistics with cited ones or remove them. Add a `citations` prop to `BlogPostingJsonLd`, as `improve-word-game-skills/page.tsx:62` already does.

## 9. Missing `Organization`, `WebSite` and `SoftwareApplication`/`VideoGame` entities on article pages, so agents cannot reach the product
All `page.tsx` files, for example `leaderboard-elo-explained/page.tsx:45`

The articles' CTAs ("Play now") link to `/${locale}/daily`. There is no `publisher`/`about` entity linking to a `VideoGame` or `WebApplication` schema (not visible here; check `BlogJsonLd.tsx`). Nothing in the audited files exposes an API, an MCP server or `llms.txt` for agents. The product is playable only through a human UI. For first revenue, add:
- `/llms.txt` that lists the daily puzzle URL, the blog index and the leaderboard.
- A read-only JSON endpoint for the daily puzzle and leaderboard (`/api/public/daily`, `/api/public/leaderboard`).
- An `about` entity in the article JSON-LD:

```diff
+        about={{ '@type': 'VideoGame', name: 'LexiClash', url: `${siteUrl}/${locale}`, gamePlatform: 'Web', applicationCategory: 'Game' }}
```

## 10. Titles over length and keyword-stuffed metadata
- `hebrew-word-games-guide/page.tsx:18` mixes English and Hebrew in one title ("Hebrew Word Games Guide - Free Online משחק מילים בעברית"). It is about 55 characters but reads as spam, and the mixed-script string is likely to be rewritten by Google.
- `improve-word-game-skills/page.tsx:27` is a 270-character description. It gets truncated at about 155 characters, and "Free tips from experienced players" is lost.
- `free-word-games-online/page.tsx:19` is 63 characters and gets truncated. The brand is missing from the title.

```diff
-  en: 'Hebrew Word Games Guide - Free Online משחק מילים בעברית',
+  en: 'Hebrew Word Games: Roots, Vowels & RTL Explained | LexiClash',
-  en: 'What is the best way to find words in word hunt? Proven strategies ... Free tips from experienced players.',
+  en: 'Find more words in Word Hunt and Boggle: chunking, pattern recognition and vocabulary tips used by competitive players.',
-  en: 'Free Word Games Online: The Honest Guide (No Pay-to-Win, 2026)',
+  en: 'Free Word Games Online: Honest Guide (No Pay-to-Win)',
```

## Priority for first revenue
Fix items 6, 9 and 1 first. They determine whether pages are crawled correctly, whether agents can reach the game, and whether Google trusts the hreflang cluster. Then fix items 2, 3 and 7 as cleanup. Item 8 protects trust before you push more content.

## Shard 13

# SEO / GEO audit: blog slice (LexiClash)

**Scope.** I only had the blog post files and the blog index. I did not see `BlogJsonLd.tsx`, `generateBlogMetadata`, `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP/CLI code. Findings on those are marked unverified, and I can't write an exact diff for what I can't see. Everything below is cited from the files shown.

Almost all of the content is in the server-rendered `page.tsx` + `content.ts` bundles and passed to a client component that is itself SSR'd. That means it is crawlable, and I'm not flagging "client-only rendering" as a blocker. The real problems are inconsistencies and missed conversion paths.

## 1. Future-dated article published in the JSON-LD and on the page
`fe-next/app/[locale]/blog/multiplayer-strategy-guide/page.tsx:14-15` and `PageClient.tsx:105`

**Impact.** `datePublished` and `dateModified` are `2026-08-03`. The other posts are dated 2026-05-19 or earlier, so either this date is in the future or it is a typo. A future date can get the article ignored or flagged in Google's Article rich result. It also weakens freshness signals for AI citation. Fix this first because it is a one-line change.

```diff
-const DATE_PUBLISHED = '2026-08-03';
-const DATE_MODIFIED = '2026-08-03';
+const DATE_PUBLISHED = '2026-05-20'; // real publish date
+const DATE_MODIFIED = '2026-05-20';
```
Also change `new Date('2026-08-03')` at `PageClient.tsx:105` to the same value, and better, export the constants and import them in both files so the visible date can't drift from the JSON-LD.

## 2. Visible date is hardcoded separately from the schema date in every post
`most-popular-word-games-2026/PageClient.tsx:85`, `multilingual-word-learning/PageClient.tsx:109`, `multiplayer-word-games-social/PageClient.tsx:111`, `netflix-word-game-2026-rise/PageClient.tsx:82`, `ordspel-familjer/PageClient.tsx:97`

**Impact.** The visible "date" and `datePublished` come from two independent literals. Google expects them to match, and a mismatch undermines trust in the page. The visible date also ignores `dateModified`, even though every post was updated 2026-05-19. Freshness is a ranking and citation signal that readers can't see.

```diff
-{safeToLocaleDateString(new Date('2026-05-15'), language, {...})}
+{safeToLocaleDateString(new Date(DATE_PUBLISHED), language, {...})}
```
Export `DATE_PUBLISHED` and `DATE_MODIFIED` from a small `meta.ts` per post, or pass them as props from `page.tsx`. Add `<time dateTime={DATE_PUBLISHED}>` around the date.

## 3. Hreflang/canonical claims for locales with no translation
`multiplayer-strategy-guide/page.tsx:17-19, 30`, `ordspel-familjer/page.tsx:18-20, 38`

**Impact.** `metaTitles` has only `en` (strategy guide) or only `sv` (ordspel-familjer), yet `/he/`, `/ja/` and the other locales render the same fallback content. `hasTranslation: locale in metaTitles` is evidently meant to noindex or canonicalize those, which is correct. `content.ts:3` of ordspel-familjer says "Non-SV locales fall back + noindex". I couldn't verify `generateBlogMetadata` does this. If it doesn't, you get duplicate content across 5+ URLs, and Google will pick its own canonical. Check that `/ja/blog/multiplayer-strategy-guide` emits `noindex` or canonicalizes to `/en/…` and is excluded from hreflang and the sitemap.

## 4. Ordspel-familjer: dead-end internal linking and the weakest conversion path
`ordspel-familjer/PageClient.tsx:34-44, 151-160`

**Impact.**
- `relatedArticlesData` passes `excerpt: ''` for every item, and titles are English on a Swedish page. Anchor text and context are weak and mismatched for the language.
- Slugs such as `boggle-vs-scrabble`, `word-games-for-kids-education` and `free-word-games-online` aren't in any file I can see, so some may 404. Verify each. Broken internal links waste crawl budget and hurt trust.
- The CTAs go to `/daily` and `/multiplayer`. The article's argument is "the daily puzzle for Swedish families", so the `/sv/` landing page is the better target. Links to `/${locale}/sv-specific landing` already exist elsewhere (`/sv/swedish-multiplayer-word-game` is used in the other posts).

```diff
+<Link href="/sv/swedish-multiplayer-word-game">…</Link>
```

## 5. Post with no FAQ/citation schema, while the one with FAQs is the exception
`multilingual-word-learning/page.tsx:55-62`, `multiplayer-strategy-guide/page.tsx:45-54`, `multiplayer-word-games-social/page.tsx:55-62`

**Impact.** Only `most-popular-word-games-2026` passes `faqItems`, `citations`, `wordCount` and `keywords`. The other three omit them. The multilingual and social posts omit `keywords`, `articleSection` and `wordCount`. The strategy guide is the best evergreen "how to win" content, and its Q&A-shaped sections ("Bank the obvious words immediately") are ideal for AI answer extraction. Generic Article schema without these fields gives LLMs and Google less to cite.

```diff
 <BlogPostingJsonLd ...
+  wordCount={wordCount}
+  keywords="word game, multilingual word games, learn languages with games"
+  articleSection="Language"
```
Reuse the `wordCount` reducer from `netflix-word-game-2026-rise/page.tsx:54-57`. Move it into a shared helper rather than copying it.

## 6. FAQPage JSON-LD in only one locale-aware post, and the FAQ answers are partially unsourced
`most-popular-word-games-2026/faq.ts:14, 18`, `content.ts:99`

**Impact.** The FAQ answers describe a competitor-heavy listicle but each embeds your product. That is good for citation. The weakness is that the 19,000-person study (`content.ts:99`) is cited vaguely, and the Netflix post names the paper correctly at `netflix-word-game-2026-rise/content.ts:174` ("Brooker et al. 2019") only in he/sv/ja/es, not in `en` (`:75`). For LLM trust, add the source to the English text and the `citations` array.

```diff
-A 2019 study tracked over 19,000 adults aged 50+ ...
+A 2019 study (Brooker et al., International Journal of Geriatric Psychiatry) tracked over 19,000 adults aged 50+ ...
```

## 7. Titles and descriptions: length and keyword problems
- `most-popular-word-games-2026/page.tsx:20` and `netflix-word-game-2026-rise/page.tsx:19` are 65+ characters and get truncated in SERPs. Lead with the query: "Most Popular Online Word Games of 2026 (Wordle, Connections & More)".
- `multiplayer-word-games-social/page.tsx:20` has a Swedish title with ASCII-stripped diacritics ("Varfor Spela Med Vanner Ar Battre"), and the Spanish description at `:31` has the same problem ("por que", "mas"). These look like typos to users, and they won't match queries typed with diacritics. Restore å/ä/ö/é/á, and fix the matching description at `:29`.
- `multiplayer-word-games-social/page.tsx:27` is about 250 characters and will be cut at roughly 155.

```diff
-sv: '... Varfor Spela Med Vanner Ar Battre',
+sv: 'Multiplayer Ordspel Online Gratis - Varför Spela Med Vänner Är Bättre',
```

## 8. Blog index has no collection schema and no list of posts for crawlers
`blog/page.tsx:19-31`

**Impact.** The only structured data is a 2-item BreadcrumbList, and the listing is rendered by `BlogIndexPageClient`, which I couldn't see. Add `CollectionPage` or `ItemList` JSON-LD listing every post URL. That gives AI crawlers and Google an explicit index of the content. Also, `SAFE_LOCALES` at `:14` excludes `ru`, while individual posts list `ru` metadata, which creates a locale inconsistency on the index and breadcrumbs for Russian.

```diff
-const SAFE_LOCALES = new Set(['en', 'he', 'sv', 'ja', 'es']);
+const SAFE_LOCALES = new Set(['en', 'he', 'sv', 'ja', 'es', 'ru']);
```
Add an `ItemList` script next to the breadcrumb, populated from a shared posts registry. The `blogIndexContent` literal is currently duplicated in three `PageClient`s (`multilingual:36-48`, `strategy:36-43`, `social:36-49`). Centralizing it also fixes the related-links drift.

## 9. Related-article blocks are English-only and inconsistent across posts
`multilingual-word-learning/PageClient.tsx:49`, `multiplayer-strategy-guide/PageClient.tsx:44`, `multiplayer-word-games-social/PageClient.tsx:50`

**Impact.** `articleMap = blogIndexContent.en` is used for every locale, so he/ja/sv/es readers see English anchor text. Anchor text in the page language is an internal-linking relevance signal. These lists also leave out the newest posts (`most-popular-word-games-2026`, `netflix-word-game-2026-rise`), so the newest, most citable content gets no internal links from older posts. Add them to a single shared registry.

## 10. Not verifiable from this slice: llms.txt, sitemap/robots, agent access
Unverified because the files weren't included:
- **`llms.txt` / `llms-full.txt`:** none shown. At minimum, publish one that lists the blog posts, `/daily`, and the multiplayer landing pages with one-line descriptions.
- **Sitemap:** confirm each post emits `<xhtml:link rel="alternate" hreflang>` only for locales where `hasTranslation` is true, and that the 2026-08-03 date from #1 isn't published as `lastmod`.
- **Agent reachability:** nothing in this slice shows an API, MCP server or CLI. For a game, the highest-value agent surface would be a read-only endpoint for today's daily puzzle and leaderboard (`/api/daily`), plus an OpenAPI doc linked from `llms.txt`. This is a product decision, not a fix I can write a diff for.

## Shard 14

# SEO/GEO audit: blog post pages (LexiClash)

**Scope:** I can only see six blog post folders (page.tsx, PageClient.tsx, content.ts). I can't see `BlogJsonLd.tsx`, the sitemap, robots, llms.txt, or any API/MCP surface. Findings on those are inferred from usage and marked as such. Server-rendered content, metadata and JSON-LD are mostly in good shape. The real gaps are broken locale handling, duplicate content, thin structured data, and no visible route into the product.

## 1. Locale fallback serves English under non-English URLs, with no signal to crawlers
`vocabulary-building-strategies/page.tsx:45`, same pattern at `science-behind-word-games/page.tsx:45`, `top-player-secrets/page.tsx:45`, `why-word-games-are-addictive/page.tsx:45` and `spelling-bee-science-vocabulary/page.tsx:51`.

- `contentByLocale[locale] || contentByLocale.en` returns English body text for any other locale, including `ru` on the spelling-bee post.
- The `hasTranslation` flag (e.g. `vocabulary-building-strategies/page.tsx:40`) covers only metadata. The article body can still be English.
- The `science-behind-word-games` and `top-player-secrets` metadata includes `ru`, but I can't confirm their `content.ts` files do.
- Impact: duplicate English pages under `/ru/`, `/he/` and others dilute rankings and can get hreflang or canonical pairs ignored. Titles and descriptions in a different language from the body also hurt CTR.
- Diff: keep `hasTranslation` tied to the content map, not the metadata map.
```diff
- hasTranslation: locale in metaTitles, ruTranslated: 'ru' in metaTitles
+ hasTranslation: locale in contentByLocale, ruTranslated: 'ru' in contentByLocale
```
Apply this in all six `page.tsx` files, and in `generateBlogMetadata` make `hasTranslation === false` emit `robots: {index:false}` and canonical to `/en/...`. I can't see that file, so verify it.

## 2. `spelling-bee-science-vocabulary` has no `ru` metadata or content
`spelling-bee-science-vocabulary/page.tsx:17-31` and `content.ts:18-213`.

- This page, unlike the others, has no Russian entry. It also doesn't pass `ruTranslated`, so the hreflang set is inconsistent across posts.
- Impact: the newest and most citable post has the weakest international footprint.
- Diff: either add a `ru` entry to both maps (preferred), or confirm hreflang for `ru` is omitted. Right now the `/ru/` URL renders English at `page.tsx:51`.

## 3. Mojibake and typos in `sv`/`es` metadata, plus mismatched read times
- `vocabulary-building-strategies/page.tsx:20,29` has ASCII-stripped Swedish, "Jag larde mig … pa … Strategier for ordforrad". It reads as broken in the SERP. `why-word-games-are-addictive/page.tsx:20,29` has the same problem ("Varfor", "Upptack", "Lar dig", "ar sa").
- Spanish titles lack accents (`vocabulary-building-strategies/page.tsx:22`, `why-word-games-are-addictive/page.tsx:22`: "Por Que", "Psicologia"). Spanish *body* text is also unaccented (`vocabulary-building-strategies/content.ts:488-608`).
- `spelling-bee-science-vocabulary/page.tsx:28` has a typo, "vokabylärstrategier".
- Impact: lower CTR, and Google may treat the text as low quality in sv/es.
- Diff (example):
```diff
- sv: 'Jag larde mig 500 nya ord pa 30 dagar - Strategier for ordforrad',
+ sv: 'Jag lärde mig 500 nya ord på 30 dagar – Strategier för ordförråd',
```

`top-player-secrets/page.tsx:55-62`, `vocabulary-building-strategies/page.tsx:55-62` and `why-word-games-are-addictive/page.tsx:55-62` pass no `citations`. Only `science-behind-word-games` and `spelling-bee-science-vocabulary` do.

```diff
+<time dateTime="2025-07-03">{safeToLocaleDateString(new Date('2025-07-03'), language, {...})}</time>
```

## 7. Related-articles lists are hard-coded and stale, and they link to English-only titles
`science-behind-word-games/PageClient.tsx:36-54` and the identical blocks in `top-player-secrets`, `vocabulary-building-strategies` and `why-word-games-are-addictive` (lines 36-55).

- The same 9-10 slug list is copy-pasted per page. It omits newer posts: `vocabulary-building-strategies`, `spelling-bee-science-vocabulary`, and in some files `multiplayer-word-games-social`. This is poor internal linking, with new posts receiving no link equity.
- `why-word-games-are-addictive/PageClient.tsx` doesn't render `RelatedArticles` at all (import missing, lines 14-15). It is a dead-end page with only two CTAs.
- Titles and anchor text are always English (`articleMap = blogIndexContent.en`), so `/he/`, `/sv/` and other pages link with English anchors.
- Diff: move the list to `lib/blogIndex.ts` (single source, localized), and add to `why-word-games-are-addictive/PageClient.tsx` after line 133:
```diff
+<RelatedArticles currentSlug="why-word-games-are-addictive" locale={locale} heading={relatedHeading} articles={relatedArticlesData} />
```

## 8. Client-only components wrap all body text
Every `PageClient.tsx` is `'use client'` and picks content through `useParams`/`useLanguage` (e.g. `science-behind-word-games/PageClient.tsx:1,19-25`).

- Next.js still server-renders client components, so HTML should contain the text. But the locale is derived from `params.locale || language` (a context). If `language` ever disagrees with the URL, SSR output and hydration differ. The `page.tsx` files already resolve `content` on the server.
- Impact: the risk is mostly about stability, but the body is also rendered via `section.content.split('\n\n')` into `<p>` tags, which is fine for crawlers.
- The `isDarkMode` ternary changes classes on hydration, with no crawl impact.
- Diff: pass `content` and `locale` as props from the server page, and drop `useParams`.
```diff
-<SciencePageClient />
+<SciencePageClient content={content} locale={locale} />
```

## 9. Heading structure and image alt are weak for extraction
- `vocabulary-building-strategies/content.ts:30` (and the first section of every post) has an untitled intro, so there is no H2 summarizing the answer. LLMs lift "definition + answer" blocks under question-style headings.
- `spelling-bee-science-vocabulary/PageClient.tsx:92` and the other hero images use a single English `alt` regardless of locale. `vocabulary-building-strategies/PageClient.tsx:123` is the same.
- Diff: add a short answer-first TL;DR object per locale and render it before the sections, e.g. `<p className="font-semibold">TL;DR: …</p>` in each `PageClient`. Move `alt` strings into `content.ts` as `heroAlt`.

## 10. No visible path for agents or users to reach the product from these pages
Across all `PageClient.tsx` files (e.g. `science-behind-word-games/PageClient.tsx:166-179`).

- The only calls to action are `/daily` and `/singleplayer`. Nothing offers a structured way to reach the product, such as an API endpoint, MCP server, or llms.txt entry. I can't see those files; verify `public/llms.txt` exists and lists the blog posts, `/daily` and the education pages. If it doesn't, add one.
- The BlogPosting JSON-LD has no `publisher` or `about` visible here. Check `BlogJsonLd.tsx` for `publisher` with `Organization` and `logo`, and add `isPartOf` pointing to the `WebSite` node.
- Revenue note: there is no `SoftwareApplication`/`VideoGame` JSON-LD tied to the posts. Adding `mentions` of the LexiClash `VideoGame` entity lets answer engines connect research content with the product.
- Diff (suggested, in `BlogJsonLd.tsx`, not visible):
```diff
+ about: { '@type': 'VideoGame', name: 'LexiClash', url: 'https://www.lexiclash.live' },
+ publisher: { '@type': 'Organization', name: 'LexiClash', url: 'https://www.lexiclash.live', logo: {...} },
```

## Priority order for first revenue
1. Items 1-3: stop duplicate and broken-locale pages from diluting rankings. These are quick to fix.
2. Item 7: restore internal links, especially `why-word-games-are-addictive`, which has no related links.
3. Items 4-6: strengthen the signals AI engines use to decide whether to cite you.
4. Items 8-10: structural and agent-access work.

## Shard 15

# SEO / GEO audit: blog posts (word-game-history, word-games-and-mental-health, word-games-for-brain-training)

I could only see three blog post directories. I did not see `BlogJsonLd`, `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, `layout.tsx`, or any API/MCP/CLI code. Findings below are limited to what these files show. Where an issue depends on code I couldn't see, I say so.

Worth knowing first: the pages are server components (`page.tsx`) that render a `'use client'` child. Next.js still server-renders client components, so the article text is probably in the initial HTML. The content is not hidden behind client-only rendering. The problems are elsewhere.

## 1. Locale fallback serves the wrong language under a mismatched canonical/hreflang (all three posts)
`word-game-history/page.tsx:37-40`, `word-games-and-mental-health/page.tsx:37-40`, `word-games-for-brain-training/page.tsx:37-40`

- Every `metaTitles` map includes `ru`, so `hasTranslation: locale in metaTitles` is true for all six locales.
- `word-game-history/content.ts:20-587` ends at `ru`, but its Russian text is partly mechanical and some paragraphs are truncated. Compare `he:150-157` with `en`: Hebrew drops entire sections.
- In `word-game-history`, `sv`, `ja`, `es` have 8 or fewer sections against 7 in `en`, and are noticeably thinner.
- Impact: thin, near-duplicate translated pages with hreflang declared for them. Google may treat them as low quality, and AI engines may cite the weaker translation.
- Fix: make `hasTranslation` reflect real completeness, and noindex or omit hreflang for partial locales.

```diff
-  return generateBlogMetadata({ ..., hasTranslation: locale in metaTitles, ruTranslated: 'ru' in metaTitles });
+  const FULL_LOCALES = ['en', 'he', 'es'];
+  return generateBlogMetadata({ ..., hasTranslation: FULL_LOCALES.includes(locale), ruTranslated: false });
```

I couldn't see `generateBlogMetadata`, so confirm it emits `robots: noindex` when `hasTranslation` is false.

## 2. Author persona is inconsistent, which weakens E-E-A-T and JSON-LD author data
`word-games-and-mental-health/content.ts:158` (he `authorName: 'חנון המילים'`), `word-games-for-brain-training/content.ts:146` (same), against `'Ohad Fisher'` everywhere else.

- The mental-health article is written in first person as a woman in the he/es/ru versions, but the `en` bio says "she" (`content.ts:27`) under the name "Ohad Fisher".
- The brain-training article makes medical claims about a "neurologist" and cites studies, but the byline is a persona.
- Impact: Google and LLMs weigh author identity on health content (YMYL). An inconsistent or fabricated author lowers trust and the odds of being cited.
- Fix: use one real author name and a stable `Person` with `sameAs` links. I couldn't see `BlogPostingJsonLd`, so verify it receives the author. None of the three `page.tsx` files pass an `author` prop (`:55-62`).

```diff
 <BlogPostingJsonLd
   title={content.title}
   ...
+  authorName="Ohad Fisher"
+  authorUrl={`${siteUrl}/${locale}/about`}
```

## 3. Dates are hardcoded and inconsistent between the page body and the JSON-LD
`word-game-history/PageClient.tsx:110` shows `2026-01-08`. `page.tsx:14` has `DATE_PUBLISHED = '2026-01-08'`, but `DATE_MODIFIED = '2026-05-19'` appears identically on all three posts (`page.tsx:15`).

- The same modified date on three unrelated posts looks like a bulk bump.
- `mental-health` and `brain-training` have no visible "updated" date.
- Impact: Google discounts fake freshness, and LLMs prefer a visibly dated, genuinely updated source.
- Fix: set `dateModified` per post only when content actually changes. Move the dates into one constant that both the visible date and the JSON-LD read, so they can't drift.

## 4. Brain-training meta description makes unsupported comparative claims
`word-games-for-brain-training/page.tsx:27`

- "A 19,000-person study shows word games beat brain training apps" misstates the ACTIVE trial. The article body itself (`content.ts:42-50`) says gains were domain-specific and that the study did not test word games against apps.
- "better than Lumosity" invites a legal and credibility problem.
- Impact: the SERP snippet contradicts the page, which hurts CTR and trust. An AI engine quoting the snippet would repeat the false claim.
- The title (`:18`) reads "Word Making Games", an awkward keyword stuff.

```diff
-  en: 'Benefits of Playing Word Making Games for Brain Training (Research)',
+  en: 'Do Word Games Help Your Brain? What the Research Says',
-  en: 'What are the benefits of playing word making games? A 19,000-person study shows word games beat brain training apps ... better than Lumosity for brain training.',
+  en: 'What do 19,000-person studies say about word games and brain health? Evidence on cognitive reserve, what word games train, and what brain-training claims to ignore.',
```

## 5. Titles are likely too long and the mental-health title is generic
`word-games-and-mental-health/page.tsx:18`, `word-game-history/page.tsx:22`

- `es` titles run to about 80 characters ("Historia de los Juegos de Palabras - De Azulejos Antiguos a Cuadrículas Digitales"). Google truncates at about 60.
- If `generateBlogMetadata` also appends a site suffix, they will be longer still.
- Impact: a truncated title loses the keyword and brand at the end.
- Fix: keep titles to 60 characters or fewer, for example `es: 'Historia de los Juegos de Palabras: De Azulejos a Cuadrículas'`.

## 6. No `FAQPage` or `Article` structure for list-style and answer-style sections
`word-games-for-brain-training/content.ts:105-118` and `word-games-and-mental-health/content.ts:57-68`

- The content has clear question-and-answer structure ("What word games actually train", "What you should actually do") but is rendered as plain `<p>` tags, split from a string on `\n\n` (`PageClient.tsx:118-128`).
- Lists such as "Lexical retrieval…, Working memory…" become anonymous paragraphs instead of `<ul>/<li>`.
- Impact: LLMs and Google extract list and definition structures far more reliably than run-on paragraphs. This costs featured-snippet and AI-citation eligibility.
- Fix: parse lines with a `Term: description` pattern into a `<ul>`, and add `FAQPage` JSON-LD for the 3 to 4 questions per article.

```diff
-  {section.content.split('\n\n').map((paragraph, pIndex) => (
-    <p key=...>{paragraph}</p>
-  ))}
+  {renderBlocks(section.content)}  {/* emits <ul><li><strong>Term</strong>: …</li></ul> when lines match /^[^:]{3,40}: /, else <p> */}
```

## 7. Related articles and "see also" links are English-only and image-less
`word-game-history/PageClient.tsx:36-54`, `word-games-and-mental-health/PageClient.tsx:36-51`

- `blogIndexContent` only has `en`, and `articleMap = blogIndexContent.en` is used for every locale. Hebrew, Spanish, and other readers get English link text.
- `image: ''` is passed for every related article.
- `word-games-for-brain-training/PageClient.tsx` has no `RelatedArticles` at all. It is an orphan-ish page with no outbound internal links except the two CTAs (`:140-149`).
- Impact: weak internal linking and mismatched anchor-text language. The `RelatedArticles` link set is also inconsistent: `word-game-history` links to `best-boggle-alternatives-2026`, but the other two do not.
- Fix: add `RelatedArticles` to the brain-training page, and localize titles and excerpts per locale. The localized titles already exist in each post's `content.ts`.

## 8. CTA links use generic anchors and the product is never linked contextually
`PageClient.tsx` in all three posts (`word-game-history:170-179`, `mental-health:165-174`, `brain-training:140-149`)

- The only links to the product are two buttons: "Daily Challenge" and "Practice".
- The body text mentions "LexiClash" once, in `word-game-history/content.ts:82`, and it is not a link. The mental-health and brain-training articles never link to the game in prose.
- Impact: for first revenue, these are the highest-intent pages. They send traffic out through weak internal anchors, and AI engines that cite the article have no product link to attach.
- Fix: add an in-prose link such as `<Link href={`/${locale}/daily`}>play a daily word grid</Link>` after the "What you should actually do" section, and link "LexiClash" at its first mention.

## 9. Missing `alt` text depth and image dimensions for social/AI previews
`word-game-history/PageClient.tsx:120-127` (same in the other two)

- The alt text is a generic description ("Word games and brain training research visualization"), and the images are `fill` with no explicit `width`/`height`.
- I could not see whether `generateBlogMetadata` sets `openGraph.images`. If it does not, shares and AI previews have no image.
- Fix: confirm `generateBlogMetadata` returns `openGraph.images: [{ url, width: 1200, height: 630, alt }]` per slug.

## 10. Unused import and persona text leaking into indexable content
`word-game-history/PageClient.tsx:10` imports `User` (unused), and the Hebrew content has machine-translation artifacts that will be indexed, for example `word-game-history/content.ts:157` ("ממציא = תמלוגים, בן זוג = עושק") and `:129`.

- Impact: broken Hebrew reads as low quality to readers and to quality classifiers, and it feeds problem 1.
- Fix: remove the unused import. Have a native speaker review or regenerate the `he`, `ru`, and `sv` copy before keeping those locales indexable.

## Not auditable from these files
- `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `llms.txt`, and hreflang `alternates` live outside what I was shown.
- Agent access: I saw no API, MCP, or CLI surface in these files, so I can't judge whether an agent could reach the product. Check separately whether `/daily` has a stable, documented URL and whether `llms.txt` lists it.

## Priority for first revenue
Fix 8 first (product links on informational pages), then 4 and 5 (CTR and title/description accuracy), then 1 and 2 (indexing quality and trust on health content).

# SEO/GEO audit: lexiclash.live (files provided only)

Scope note: I only saw page-level files under `fe-next/app/[locale]/`. I did not see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, `generatePageMetadata`, `BlogJsonLd`, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. I make no claims about those. Each finding below is grounded in a line I can cite.

## 1. `/boggle-word-shake-free` is `force-dynamic`, so every non-`en` URL is a thin duplicate and the page is never cached
`fe-next/app/[locale]/boggle-word-shake-free/page.tsx:6,16-17,23,38-44`
- **Impact:** The page has no `generateStaticParams`, so all locales render per request. Non-`en` locales get `noindex` (line 23) but serve the same English body. Canonical and hreflang are hard-coded to `/en/`. Rendering per request also hurts crawl budget and TTFB for the page that targets a high-intent query ("boggle word shake free").
- **Diff:**
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 3600;
+export function generateStaticParams() {
+  return [{ locale: 'en' }];
+}
+export const dynamicParams = true;
```
Nothing on this page reads request data. The client game is already `ssr:false`.

## 2. `/brain-training-word-games` advertises hreflang for 6 locales with untranslated or missing content
`fe-next/app/[locale]/brain-training-word-games/page.tsx:11,38-41,13-19`
- **Impact:** `OG_IMAGE` and `OG_LOCALE` have no `ru` entry (lines 13-27), yet `ru` is in `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` and gets hreflang. If `getBrainLandingCopy('ru')` falls back to English, you are declaring an English page as Russian. Google then ignores or flags the hreflang cluster, which suppresses ranking in all six locales.
- **Diff:**
```diff
 const OG_LOCALE: Record<string, string> = {
   en: 'en_US',
   he: 'he_IL',
   sv: 'sv_SE',
   ja: 'ja_JP',
   es: 'es_ES',
+  ru: 'ru_RU',
 };
```
Also emit hreflang only for locales with real copy. Confirm that `getBrainLandingCopy` returns Russian.

## 3. The `/brain-training-word-games` VideoGame JSON-LD points at `/brain`, and the claims don't match the page
`fe-next/app/[locale]/brain-training-word-games/page.tsx:94,103,98`
- **Impact:** The entity `url` and `offers.url` are `/brain`, not the page that carries the markup. `gamePlatform` lists iOS and Android without anything in view backing that up. Mismatched or unverifiable structured data costs rich-result eligibility and AI-citation trust.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-    url: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}/brain`,
+    url: pageUrl, // define pageUrl at module/function scope
...
-    gamePlatform: ['Web Browser', 'iOS', 'Android', 'PWA'],
+    gamePlatform: ['Web Browser', 'PWA'],
```
Also, `/brain` has no `inLanguage`, which weakens the pairing.

## 4. `/brain/page.tsx` has the SEO copy but never renders it, and the visible page is client-only behind an auth loader
`fe-next/app/[locale]/brain/page.tsx:31-132,154-159`, `brain/PageClient.tsx:98-100`
- **Impact:** `seoContent` (FAQ, features) feeds only JSON-LD. There is no FAQ in the DOM, and the comment at lines 154-158 confirms the visible card was removed. Google may treat structured data with no matching visible content as spam. The client returns `<PageLoader>` while `authLoading` is true (line 98), so the SSR HTML is a spinner with no H1. Crawlers see an empty page.
- **Diff:** render a server-side `<h1>`/summary regardless of auth state, and move the loader under it:
```diff
-  if (authLoading || (isAuthenticated && isLoading)) {
-    return <PageLoader .../>;
-  }
+  const showLoader = authLoading || (isAuthenticated && isLoading);
...
-      <div className="px-4 py-6 ...">
-        <QuickDrillsSection .../>
+      <div className="px-4 py-6 ...">
+        {showLoader ? <PageLoader .../> : <QuickDrillsSection .../>}
```
Keep the header and `<h1>` outside the conditional.

## 5. The `/brain` metadata lists 5 drills including the retired "Pattern Switcher", and `ru` is missing from `seoContent`
`brain/page.tsx:37,64,99,116` vs `brain/drills/pattern-switcher/page.tsx:3-8`
- **Impact:** The copy and JSON-LD (via `features`) advertise a drill that now redirects to the hub. That is a stale-claim risk for AI citations and a mismatch for users. The `/brain-training-word-games` page's `DRILL_IDS` lists 4 drills while its title says "5".

`blog/word-games-for-kids-education/page.tsx:15-16,41,58`, `PageClient.tsx:27-34`
- **Impact:** `relatedHeadingByLocale` has no `ru`, so Russian users get an English heading. `blogIndexContent` only has `en` (line 49), so the "Related" cards are always English even on translated pages. Related links are internal links for crawlers. `hasTranslation: locale in metaTitles` is true for all six locales, but the `he`/`sv`/`ja`/`es` bodies are noticeably shorter than `en` (for example `sv` has 7 sections vs 9 in `en`).
- **Diff:**
```diff
     es: 'También te puede interesar',
+    ru: 'Вам также может понравиться',
```

## 7. Facts in the article body disagree across locales, hurting E-E-A-T and citation
`blog/word-games-for-kids-education/content.ts:36 vs 157,294`; `:116 vs 255 vs 682`
- **Impact:** The `en` text says "Marcus is in college now" with no time reference, while `he`/`sv`/`ja`/`es`/`ru` say "eight years ago". The `ru` text lists "six languages" but names five (line 682). The `he` read time says 12 minutes vs 9 in `en`. Inconsistent claims are filtered out by answer engines, and Google's QRG penalizes contradictory facts.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-    он поддерживает шесть языков: русский, английский, шведский, японский и испанский.
+    он поддерживает шесть языков: иврит, английский, шведский, японский, испанский и русский.
```
Align `readTime` values too.

## 8. Brain drill pages are `noindex` but linked from the hub's JSON-LD ItemList
`brain/drills/*/page.tsx:9`, `brain-training-word-games/page.tsx:114-120`
- **Impact:** The ItemList on an indexed page lists URLs that are `noindex`. This sends a contradictory signal and wastes crawl budget.
- **Diff:** either remove `url:` from ItemList items or drop `noIndex: true` in those four `generatePageMetadata` calls (`brainLightningRound`, `brainMemoryHunt`, `brainComboMaster`, `brainRareGems`). The second option is better if the drills are playable without signup.

## 9. Community board pages share one generic metadata key, so every board has an identical title and description
`community/[boardCode]/page.tsx:10`
- **Impact:** All boards use `seoKey: 'communityBoard', path: '/community'`. That path is the hub's own canonical, so every board URL canonicalizes to `/community`. Board pages are never indexed individually, and the shared UGC pages lose the long-tail value. The page also fetches client-side only (`PageClient.tsx:24-36`), so the board title and grid are invisible to crawlers.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-  const { locale } = await params;
-  return generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'communityBoard', path: '/community', locale });
+  const { locale, boardCode } = await params;
+  return generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'communityBoard', path: `/community/${boardCode}`, locale });
```
Better still, fetch the board on the server and pass it as props so the title and grid are in the HTML. That also lets you set a board-specific `<title>`.

## 10. `/community` renders its SEO content after the client shell, with `force-dynamic` and non-localized or missing FAQ content
`community/page.tsx:6,13-83`
- **Impact:** `force-dynamic` prevents caching for a page that is entirely static copy plus a client shell. `ja` and `sv` have `faq: []` (lines 56, 65), so no FAQ schema is emitted for those locales. `ru` has no entry at all, so Russian visitors fall back to English copy under a Russian URL.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 3600;
+export function generateStaticParams() {
+  return ['en','he','sv','ja','es','ru'].map((locale) => ({ locale }));
+}
```
Add a `ru` entry, or `noindex` and omit hreflang for locales without real copy.

Sitemap, robots, `llms.txt`, MCP/API/CLI access for agents, and the root `[locale]` layout hreflang were not in the provided files. I would check those next, since they are likely the biggest levers for AI-agent reachability.

# SEO / GEO audit — LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]/…` slice)

I could only see the route files above, not `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP/CLI code. Items on those are flagged as unverified, not findings.

## 1. Contact page: 23 hreflang entries, 12 pointing at the wrong language
`fe-next/app/[locale]/contact/layout.tsx:56-71` and `contact/page.tsx:14`

The layout emits `en-IL`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `en-SE`, `en-AU` and similar, all pointing at `/en/…`. It also emits `es-AR` and `es-CO`. Google treats this as noise, and it hands the CTR-relevant regions no better signal. Also, `page.tsx:14` already calls `generatePageMetadata`, so two metadata sources compete. Next merges them, and the layout wins on `alternates`. Impact: wasted crawl, a risk of ignored hreflang clusters, and no `ru` x-default conflict.
```diff
-        'en-IL': ... 'es-CO': ... (lines 56-71)
+        // keep only the six real locales + x-default
```
Delete lines 56-71 and let `generatePageMetadata` own the metadata. Remove the layout's `generateMetadata` entirely.

## 2. Competitive page: hreflang and index for en only, but the route serves every locale
`competitive-word-games/page.tsx:21-28, 43-49`

For `/he/competitive-word-games`, the canonical is hardcoded to `/en/…` and the page is `noindex`. That is fine. But the visible copy (lines 118-245) is English and the links use `/${locale}`, so non-English visitors get English content. Make it explicit and cheaper to crawl:
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 3600;
+export function generateStaticParams() { return [{ locale: 'en' }]; }
```
And `notFound()` for non-en locales, which removes the thin duplicates. `force-dynamic` also means no CDN caching on a page whose content is static, which slows TTFB for crawlers.

## 3. Money pages are `force-dynamic`, which defeats caching
`daily-word-wheel/page.tsx:7`, `competitive-word-games/page.tsx:5`, `custom/page.tsx:12`

Daily-word-wheel is the page targeting "word wheel daily record". Nothing in it reads cookies or headers. Compare `connections/page.tsx:10-25`, which already did this fix.
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 3600;
+export function generateStaticParams() { return LOCALES.map((locale) => ({ locale })); }
```

`daily-word-wheel/page.tsx:190, 189`

- Line 190 says "5 language support" but `LOCALES` has 6, and `inLanguage` is set to 6.

- The `HowTo` rich result is deprecated, so it adds bytes but no SERP feature.

`daily-word-wheel/page.tsx:145`

The GEO FAQ is only injected for `en`, so this is fine. The weakness is that the page's best citation hook (scoring table, BLEND = 60) sits in a collapsed `<details>` (`AnimatedLanding.tsx:230`) and in framer-motion `whileInView` wrappers that start at `opacity: 0`. The server HTML contains it, but it is hidden until hydration. Expose the answer in plain text:
```diff
-<m.section className="mb-12" initial="hidden" whileInView="visible" ...>
+<section className="mb-12">
```
for the FAQ section at `AnimatedLanding.tsx:218`, and add `open` on the first `<details>`.

## 6. Connections landing: JSON-LD claims platforms that may not exist
`connections/page.tsx:127`

`gamePlatform: ['Web Browser','iOS','Android','PWA']`. The competitive page lists only Web and Android (`competitive-word-games/page.tsx:98`). A mismatch like this erodes trust in structured data. Align the two, and drop iOS if there is no app.
```diff
-gamePlatform: ['Web Browser', 'iOS', 'Android', 'PWA'],
+gamePlatform: ['Web Browser', 'Android'],
```

## 7. Connections English FAQ example is wrong
`connections/content.ts:121`

"TRAFFIC + STORM → JAM (traffic jam, jam storm)". "Jam storm" is not a phrase. This is the text an LLM will quote as the definition, so a bad example hurts citations. The demo `FIRE + ENGINE → TRUCK` is also wrong in format: "fire truck" works, but "truck engine" is fine and "fire engine" is itself already a phrase. Use a clean example:
```diff
-Example: TRAFFIC + STORM → JAM (traffic jam, jam storm).
+Example: FIRE + ENGINE → TRUCK is not it; use SUN + BURN → … 
```
Better: `BIRTH + NIGHT → DAY (birthday, daynight)` is also weak. Pick a verified pair such as `HOUSE … BOAT → …` from the actual puzzle pool.

## 8. Community, daily, and pyramid pages are `noindex` with a bare title and no content
`connections/community/page.tsx:15-18`, `daily/page.tsx:15-18`, `pyramid/page.tsx:15-18`

Correct to noindex, but `/connections/daily` is the viral entry point that gets shared. It has no `openGraph`/`twitter`, so shared links render as plain text.
```diff
+    openGraph: { title: 'Word Bridge Daily Challenge — LexiClash', description: "Today's Word Bridge puzzle.", url: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}/connections/daily`, images: [`${BASE_URL}/og-image-en.webp`] },
```
Apply the same to `pyramid/page.tsx`.

## 9. Crossword is a client-only shell, correctly noindexed; no path to ranking
`crossword/page.tsx:8-13`

~37 crawlable words, as the comment says. Not an error, but it is an unmonetised opportunity: the daily crossword is a high-intent query. Skip until there is a landing page, as the comment already says. A server-rendered intro plus today's clue count under the `CrosswordPageClient` would be enough to lift the `noindex`.

## 10. Custom and custom/[puzzleCode] metadata drops `ru` and has no canonical
`custom/page.tsx:16`, `custom/[puzzleCode]/page.tsx:34`

`supportedLocales = ['en','he','sv','ja','es']` omits `ru`, so Russian users get English titles. `/custom` is indexable (no robots) but has no canonical or hreflang, so it will dedupe across locales unpredictably.
```diff
-const supportedLocales = ['en', 'he', 'sv', 'ja', 'es'];
+const supportedLocales = ['en', 'he', 'sv', 'ja', 'es', 'ru'];
```
and in `custom/page.tsx` add `alternates: { canonical: \`https://www.lexiclash.live/${validLocale}/custom\` }`.

## Not verifiable from this slice
- `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`: not provided. The content test mentions `app/sitemap.ts` derives from `SUPPORTED_LANDING_LOCALES`, which is good.
- Agent access (API/MCP/CLI): nothing visible. For revenue, an `llms.txt` pointing at the daily-puzzle and leaderboard endpoints, plus a read-only public JSON of the daily record, would give AI answers something to cite directly.

## Shard 18

# SEO / GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]/…`)

**Scope.** I only saw the daily, download-android, editorial-policy, dev and education files. I did not see `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP/CLI code. Those parts of the brief are marked "not assessable" at the end rather than guessed at.

Findings are ordered by impact on impressions or AI citations.

---

### 1. `/daily/word-hunt` is noindexed but its JSON-LD and hreflang still point at it
- **`daily/layout.tsx:142-149`**: the ItemList sends "Word Hunt Survival" to `/daily`, but the "Daily Word Wheel" item points at `/daily/word-wheel`. `word-wheel/page.tsx:174` noindexes that route. Its own comment says `/daily-word-wheel` is the indexable landing page.
- **Impact:** structured data advertises a URL that Google is told to drop. Entity signals are split between two URLs for the same mode, which weakens citation of the Word Wheel page.
- **Diff** (`layout.tsx:149`):
```diff
-        url: `https://www.lexiclash.live${localePath}/daily/word-wheel`,
+        url: `https://www.lexiclash.live${localePath}/daily-word-wheel`,
```
- Apply the same change to `word-wheel/page.tsx:196-197` and `:209` (breadcrumb and Event `VirtualLocation`). Better still, don't emit Event or FAQ JSON-LD on a noindex shell. Delete the `schemas` block there, since `/daily-word-wheel` should carry it.

### 2. `/daily` hreflang is incomplete and disagrees between layout and page
- **`daily/page.tsx:251-261`** returns `alternates` with no `x-default`. `layout.tsx:44-71` has the full set. In Next, the page's `alternates` replaces the layout's, so the shipped output loses `x-default` and the regional variants.
- Hreflang also omits `ru` in `ogImageMap` (`page.tsx:239-245`), so `/ru/daily` falls back to the English OG image.
- **Impact:** weaker locale targeting for a six-locale site, and wrong share cards for Russian.
- **Diff** (`page.tsx:253`):
```diff
       languages: {
+        'x-default': `${baseUrl}/en/daily`,
         en: `${baseUrl}/en/daily`,
```
```diff
+    ru: `${baseUrl}/og-image-en.webp`, // or a real og-image-ru.webp
```

### 3. Share-param URLs return a different `<title>` and no canonical
- **`daily/page.tsx:152-176` and `206-230`**: when `wh*` or `share` params are present, the function returns early with no `alternates.canonical` and no robots directive. Each shared result URL, such as `/daily?whName=…`, is therefore a distinct indexable page with a thin title.
- **Impact:** crawl budget goes to near-duplicates, and the hub's ranking signals are split.
- **Diff** (add to both early returns):
```diff
+          alternates: { canonical: `${baseUrl}${localePath}/daily` },
+          robots: { index: false, follow: true },
           openGraph: {
```
- Keep `openGraph.url` as is so previews still work.

### 4. Hard-coded dates in the Event JSON-LD make a stale, invalid rich result
- **`layout.tsx:154-161, 260-261`** and **`word-wheel/page.tsx:184-206`**: `startDate` and `endDate` are bare dates computed at render time, and `tomorrow.setHours` uses server-local time. The layout is not forced dynamic, so the value can be cached for days.
- Google's Event guidelines also don't apply to a permanent online game. The Event, plus `HowTo` (deprecated for rich results) and `VideoGame`/`WebApplication`/`SoftwareApplication` triple-typing, adds noise without giving eligibility.
- **Impact:** possible structured-data warnings, and misleading "dated" signals to AI crawlers.
- **Fix:** delete `wordHuntEventSchema` and its entry at `layout.tsx:298`. Keep the VideoGame/WebApplication node and the FAQ.

### 5. The `/daily` FAQ and definition copy is never rendered, and no FAQPage JSON-LD is emitted
- **`daily/page.tsx:297-304`**: the page deliberately renders only `<DailyRedirect />`. The FAQ in `dailySeo.data.ts:30-35` (and its Hebrew version, `:49-56`, which the test calls an "AI-Overview eligibility" asset) feeds only meta and HowTo. `layout.tsx:293-300` has no FAQPage entry.
- **Impact:** the best citable "what is Word Hunt / Word Wheel / when does it reset" answers are not visible to crawlers or LLMs. That defeats the point of writing them.
- **Diff** (`layout.tsx`, after `howToSchema`):
```diff
+  const faqSchema = {
+    '@context': 'https://schema.org',
+    '@type': 'FAQPage',
+    mainEntity: localeSeo.faq.map((qa) => ({
+      '@type': 'Question',
+      name: qa.question,
+      acceptedAnswer: { '@type': 'Answer', text: qa.answer },
+    })),
+  };
```
```diff
-            howToSchema,
+            howToSchema,
+            faqSchema,
```
- Google may ignore FAQ markup for text that isn't on the page. So also render the FAQ as a collapsed `<details>` block below the fold, outside the flex game container. That avoids the layout problem described in `page.tsx:297-302`.

### 6. The Android landing page's OG images and language map are wrong for sv/es/ru
- **`download-word-game-android/page.tsx:18-24`** maps `sv` and `es` to the English image. `ru` has no entry and `OG_LOCALE` has no `ru`.
- `page.tsx:68` uses `robots.index: isSupported`, and the supported list does include `ru`.

```diff
+  ru: 'ru_RU',

```

### 7. The archive date pages declare 18 regional hreflang entries on noindex pages
- **`daily/archive/[date]/page.tsx:81-103`**: the full hreflang cluster (including `en-IL`, `es-AR`, `es-CO`, and others) is emitted while `robots` is `noindex` (`:108`).
- **Impact:** Google ignores hreflang on noindexed URLs, and it wastes bytes in every response across about 780 URLs. The self-canonical on a noindex page is fine.
- **Diff** (`:83-102`): remove the `languages` block entirely.
- The same page has a hard-coded English `<h1>` ("Puzzle #…") and English labels at `:131, 201, 216, 224, 255`, so the localized pages are English-only despite the locale meta.

- **`daily/archive/page.tsx:50-56`** falls back to English strings when `t.daily.archive` is missing. **`:137-155`** emits one link per day. The pages behind these links are noindex, so their only value to search is link equity, which `follow: true` preserves.
- **Impact:** the archive is a low-value hub for AdSense purposes. Either noindex it as well or add a short intro paragraph.
- **Diff** (`:43`):
```diff
-  return generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'dailyArchive', path: '/daily/archive', locale });
+  return generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'dailyArchive', path: '/daily/archive', locale, noIndex: true });
```
- Drop the Breadcrumb item named "Archive" if you noindex it.

### 9. The editorial policy has weak metadata and misses E-E-A-T markup
- **`editorial-policy/page.tsx:15-31`**: English-only title and description for all six locales (the `languages` map lists only five). There is no `openGraph`, and no JSON-LD (`Organization`, `WebPage`, or `AboutPage`).

```diff
         es: `${SITE_URL}/es/editorial-policy`,
+        ru: `${SITE_URL}/ru/editorial-policy`,
       },
     },
+    openGraph: { title: 'Editorial Policy & Standards - LexiClash', url, siteName: 'LexiClash', type: 'website' },

```

- **`education/PageClient.tsx:1, 25-70`**: `'use client'`, and teachers are redirected away on the client (`:33-41`). The FAQ and GEO answer arrive via server-supplied props (`geo`, `faq`), which is the right pattern.

I did not see the server `page.tsx` or `generateMetadata`, so I cannot confirm that the teacher landing has a title, canonical, hreflang, or Product/FAQ JSON-LD. Please verify those render server-side. This is the page most likely to produce first revenue (Teacher Pro).

## Shard 19

# SEO / GEO audit: LexiClash education pages

**Scope limit.** The only files I was given are test snapshot outputs (`__textsnapshots__/*.meta|jsonld|text.txt`) for two guide pages (`brain-breaks-word-games`, `early-finishers-activities`) and some component tests. I could not see `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, any MCP/API/CLI surface, or the code that generates this metadata. So:

- Each diff below shows the **output the generator should produce**. Apply it in the generator, then re-record the snapshots.
- I found nothing, good or bad, about llms.txt, sitemap, robots or agent access in what I was given. Treat those as unaudited, not clean. The agent-access gap is item 8.

What is already good, so you don't touch it:
- Canonicals are self-referencing.
- The marketing H1 is in the first server render (`EducationLanding.ssrMarketing.test.tsx:77`).
- Each page has FAQ, BreadcrumbList and LearningResource JSON-LD, with FAQ answers that are real extractable prose.

## 1. Localized pages link back into English funnel pages and show English CTA copy
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.es.text.txt:23,25,26`**, and the same pattern in the he, ja, sv and ru text snapshots (for example `…early-finishers-activities.ja.text.txt:23,24,25`).

- **Evidence:** the "Request teacher access" and "Contact for district pricing" hrefs are `/en/education/access` and `/en/education/for-schools`. The CTA block is untranslated English ("Bring LexiClash to your classroom… Request teacher access… Start 14-day free trial") on es, he, ja, sv and ru pages.
- **Impact:** a Spanish, Hebrew or Japanese teacher who is ready to convert lands on an English form. This is the conversion step on the pages that earn the impressions.
- **Impact on citations:** the localized pages carry a mixed-language tail, which weakens language signals for AI citation.
- **Fix:** translate the keys and use locale-prefixed links.

```diff
- /en/education/access
- /en/education/for-schools
+ /${locale}/education/access
+ /${locale}/education/for-schools
```

Also translate the keys behind "Bring LexiClash to your classroom", "Request teacher access", "Start 14-day free trial" and "For schools & districts" in the es, he, ja, sv and ru dictionaries.

## 2. No Offer or SoftwareApplication structured data for the one thing you sell
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:1-150`** (the same applies to every page's JSON-LD).

- **Evidence:** the pages repeat "Teacher Pro — $9/mo, unlimited classes, free plan 3 classes × 50 students" (`…en.text.txt:2`). The JSON-LD has WebPage, Breadcrumb, LearningResource and FAQ blocks, but no `SoftwareApplication` or `Offer`.
- **Impact:** LLMs and Google cannot extract price or plan. Questions like "free classroom word game app with pricing" cannot cite you with a number.
- **Fix:** add this as a fifth block on every guide page. It is the same `$9` constant the code already uses.

```diff
+ ---
+ {
+   "@context": "https://schema.org",
+   "@type": "SoftwareApplication",
+   "@id": "https://www.lexiclash.live/en#app",
+   "name": "LexiClash",
+   "applicationCategory": "EducationalApplication",
+   "operatingSystem": "Web",
+   "offers": [
+     {"@type":"Offer","name":"Free","price":"0","priceCurrency":"USD"},
+     {"@type":"Offer","name":"Teacher Pro","price":"9","priceCurrency":"USD",
+      "url":"https://www.lexiclash.live/en/teacher/upgrade"}
+   ]
+ }
```

## 3. `isPartOf` and `provider` point to an `#org` node that is not defined on the page
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:9-11, 71-76`**

- **Evidence:** `isPartOf` is `{"@id": ".../en/education#org"}` and `provider` is an inline `EducationalOrganization`. Neither carries `sameAs`, a logo, or a defining node on this URL.
- **Impact:** crawlers that read one URL see a dangling reference. Entity linking for the brand is weak, and the brand is nearly unknown, so this matters.
- **Fix:** emit the full Organization once per page (or at least on `/education`), with `logo` and `sameAs`.

```diff
+ {"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"EducationalOrganization",
+  "@id":"https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education#org",
+  "name":"LexiClash Education","url":"https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education",
+  "logo":"https://www.lexiclash.live/images/logo.png",
+  "sameAs":["<your real social/Product Hunt/GitHub URLs>"]}
```

## 4. Titles on four of the six locales drop the brand
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.es.meta.txt:2`**, and likewise `he.meta.txt:2`, `ja.meta.txt:2`, `sv.meta.txt:2`.

- **Evidence:**
  - en has `| LexiClash`.
  - es has "…| Juegos de palabras", he has "…| משחקי מילים חינמיים", ja has "…| 無料オンライン単語ゲーム", and sv has "…| Ordspel online". None of these four contains the brand.
  - ru has a brand, but it is "Бесплатный класс LexiClash".
  - The `early-finishers` es, he and ja titles keep the brand, so the pattern is inconsistent.
- **Impact:** a lower branded CTR and no brand association in SERPs and AI answer snippets for these locales.
- **Fix, in the generator's title template, for the affected locales:**

```diff
- "title": "Pausas de concentración para aula | Juegos de palabras",
+ "title": "Pausas de concentración para aula | LexiClash",
```

Apply the same to the he, ja and sv brain-breaks titles.

## 5. Russian pages use the English hero image
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.ru.jsonld.txt:12`, `…ru.meta.txt:52,62`**, and the same in `early-finishers-activities.ru.jsonld.txt:12`.

- **Evidence:** `education-hero-en.webp` is used for `primaryImageOfPage`, `og:image` and `twitter:image` on `/ru/`. Every other locale has its own `education-hero-<locale>.webp`.
- **Impact:** English text appears in Russian social cards and image results. Either the localized asset is missing, or ru falls through to the en default.
- **Fix:** ship `education-hero-ru.webp`, then:

```diff
- "primaryImageOfPage": "https://www.lexiclash.live/images/education-hero-en.webp",
+ "primaryImageOfPage": "https://www.lexiclash.live/images/education-hero-ru.webp",
```

Make the same change for `ogImages` and `twImages`.

## 6. Machine-translated copy is garbled in text that AI engines will quote, and one claim is wrong
- **`…brain-breaks-word-games.he.jsonld.txt:114`:** the Hebrew answer contains the stray English fragment "בתDashboard" ("…קבע את שפת הכיתה בתDashboard…").
- **`…brain-breaks-word-games.he.jsonld.txt:106` and `…he.text.txt`:** other Hebrew fragments read unnaturally. FAQ answers are what get lifted verbatim into AI answers, so bad copy is quoted as-is.
- **`…brain-breaks-word-games.en.text.txt:2`:** "Works offline-first: Games run on any internet. No downloads, no logins." The product is online-only, so "offline-first" is a false claim that an LLM can repeat.
- **`…brain-breaks-word-games.sv.meta.txt:8`:** the keyword `" snabba pausningar"` has a leading space. Google ignores the keywords field, so the impact is cosmetic.
- **Fix:** have a native speaker review the he, ja, sv and ru FAQ strings. Then:

```diff
- Works offline-first: Games run on any internet. No downloads, no logins, no friction.
+ Works on any device with a browser: no downloads, no logins, no friction. Needs an internet connection.
```

## 7. Hreflang is bloated with invented region variants
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.en.meta.txt:27-43`** (identical in all locales).

- **Evidence:** 17 region codes (`en-JP`, `en-SE`, `en-ES`, `en-MX`, `es-AR`, `es-CO`, `en-AU`, `en-IL`…) all point to the same six URLs. There are only six real language versions, and `x-default` is already present.
- **Impact:** no ranking gain. It adds about 3 KB of head markup per page and raises the chance of a reciprocal-set mismatch. Google needs every URL in the set to list all the others, and any miss invalidates the pair.
- **Fix:** keep only these entries.

```diff
  "languages": {
    "x-default": ".../en/education/brain-breaks-word-games",
    "he": …, "en": …, "sv": …, "ja": …, "es": …, "ru": …
-   "en-IL": …, … "ru-RU": …
  }
```

Keep `en-US` only if you plan a distinct US version.

## 8. No agent-reachable product surface visible, and the "no signup" pitch is not machine-readable
**Not found in the files provided: no `llms.txt`, MCP, API or CLI reference.**

- **Evidence:**
  - The strongest differentiators are in the page text: "join with a class code, no email, no password", "$9/mo", and the free tier limits (`…en.text.txt:2`). They are expressed only as page copy.
  - The student join path (`/en/education/classroom-game`) and the checkout (`/en/teacher/upgrade`) are plain links, so an agent can find them, but nothing documents them for agents.
- **Impact:** AI assistants asked "what classroom word game can I run with a join code?" have no canonical short document to cite. That is lost GEO citation share at low cost.
- **Fix:** add `public/llms.txt` (route it through the non-localized path) with real URLs you control:

```diff
+ # LexiClash
+ > Live multiplayer word games for classrooms. Teachers host a game; students join with a class code, no account.
+ ## Key pages
+ - [Classroom game](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/classroom-game): join/host with a code
+ - [Teaching guides](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education): brain breaks, early finishers, icebreakers
+ - [Teacher Pro, $9/mo](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/teacher/upgrade): unlimited classes, analytics; free plan = 3 classes × 50 students
+ - [Word lists](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/lists)
```

Also confirm that `robots.txt` does not block GPTBot, ClaudeBot or PerplexityBot, and that the sitemap lists all six locales of every guide page. I could not verify either.

## 9. Thin internal-link coverage on the Russian pages
**`…brain-breaks-word-games.ru.text.txt:5-16`**

- **Evidence:** the ru hrefs jump from `/ru/education` straight to the guide links, with no `/ru/education/lists/...` links at all. The other locales link topic lists (`lists/topic/animals|emotions|food`) plus language lists. The same applies to `early-finishers-activities.ru`.
- **Impact:** the Russian list pages (if they exist) get no internal authority from guides, which weakens ru discovery and crawl depth.
- **Fix:** render the "Free word lists to play" block for ru. If the lists are not translated yet, link to `/ru/education/lists` only, rather than silently dropping the block.

```diff
+ /ru/education/lists/topic/animals
+ /ru/education/lists/russian
+ /ru/education/lists
```

## 10. Minor: speakable is declared but unverifiable, and breadcrumb labels drift between pages
- **`…brain-breaks-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:13-18`:** `speakable` with `cssSelector: ["[data-answer]"]`. Google supports speakable only for news publishers, so the impact is nil. Remove it, or at least confirm that `[data-answer]` exists in the DOM.
- **`…brain-breaks-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:35` vs `…early-finishers-activities.en.jsonld.txt:35`:** the position-2 breadcrumb label for the same URL (`/en/education`) is "Education" on one page and "Teaching guides" on the other. Pick one label.
- **Not an issue:** `robots` is `index,follow`, and every canonical is self-referencing.

3. Item 2: Offer schema.
