## Shard 20

# SEO/GEO audit: LexiClash education pages

**Scope caveat.** I only have the text/meta/JSON-LD snapshots for a few `/education/*` pages. I can't see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, the root layout, or any API/MCP/CLI code. Anything about those is flagged as unverified, not asserted. Line numbers below refer to the snapshot files, which are generated output. The fix has to land in the source generators (the `generateMetadata` and JSON-LD builders), which I can't see. I give the exact output change and name the likely source.

Paths are shortened to `…/__textsnapshots__/` (full prefix: `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/__tests__/__textsnapshots__/`).

---

## 1. ESL FAQ schema contradicts the page and the product ("accounts required")
**`…/esl-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:97`** (same in `.es.jsonld.txt:97`, `.he.jsonld.txt:97`)

- **Problem:** The FAQPage answer says "Yes, student accounts are required." The page body says "no student accounts", "no account, no email", and the join flow needs only a code. The en/he meta also says "Free student accounts" (`esl-word-games.en.meta.txt:37`), and the body lists "Free student accounts — quick one-time setup" (`esl-word-games.en.text.txt`, features list).
- **Impact:** This is the most damaging item for AI citations. LLMs and Google quote FAQ answers. The no-signup claim is your main differentiator against 7ESL-style libraries, and an engine that quotes "accounts required" will undercut it. Contradictory facts also lower trust in the whole entity.
- **Fix (source of the FAQ strings, en/es/he):**
```diff
-"text": "Yes, student accounts are required but always free. Teachers set up a classroom, and students join with a simple code. No payment needed, ever."
+"text": "No. Teachers create a classroom and students join from any browser with a six-character code and a display name. No email, password or student account. Free for 3 classes of 50 students."
```
Also change the og/tw descriptions ("Free student accounts, 6 dictionaries…") to "No student accounts".

## 2. Hebrew/Russian/Swedish pages have English-only or wrong-language schema and metadata
- **`…/esl-word-games.he.meta.txt:4`**: the keywords are the English and Spanish list ("juegos en ingles…") on the he page. Keywords carry little weight with Google, but this signals unlocalised content.
- **`…/end-of-year-classroom-activities.ru.meta.txt:50`, `…ru.jsonld.txt:12`, `…early-finishers-activities.ru.meta.txt:51`**: the ru pages use `education-hero-en.webp` instead of an `-ru` image. The share preview is English, and `primaryImageOfPage` is wrong for the locale.
- **`…esl-word-games.he.jsonld.txt:64`, `.es.jsonld.txt:64`**: `teaches` is English in the he/es JSON-LD, mixed with `inLanguage: he`/`es`.
- **Impact:** Lower CTR on social and Discover, and weaker locale relevance signals.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-"url": "https://www.lexiclash.live/images/education-hero-en.webp"
+"url": "https://www.lexiclash.live/images/education-hero-ru.webp"
```
Create the asset, or fall back deliberately. Localise `teaches` per locale.

## 3. hreflang cluster is bloated with fake regional variants
**`…/end-of-year-classroom-activities.en.meta.txt:18-43`** (all pages)

- **Problem:** The `languages` map has 24 entries. Locale-regional entries such as `en-JP`, `en-SE`, `en-ES`, `en-MX`, `es-AR`, `es-CO`, `en-IL` and `he-IL` all point at the same URLs as the base language. Nothing breaks, but each page carries about 24 `<link>` tags, and the regions are invented, e.g. `en-JP` is not a real audience.
- **Impact:** Crawl and HTML bloat, no ranking gain, and more surface for reciprocity errors in Search Console. Also, `x-default` points to `/en/…` while `en` points to the same URL, which is acceptable.
- **Fix:** Keep the 6 languages plus `x-default`, and add regional variants only where the content really differs (e.g. `en-GB` spelling):
```diff
-    "en-IL": …, "he-IL": …, "en-US": …, "es-US": …, "en-GB": …, "en-SE": …, "sv-SE": …, "en-JP": …, "ja-JP": …, "en-ES": …, "es-ES": …, "en-MX": …, "es-MX": …, "en-AU": …, "es-AR": …, "es-CO": …, "ru-RU": …
```

## 4. Metadata titles are inconsistent, and some are weak or untranslated
- **`…/esl-word-games.es.meta.txt:2`**: "ESL Word Games para el aula" is a mixed-language title. Spanish searchers type "juegos de vocabulario en inglés", which the page's own FAQ and body target, but the title does not contain it.
- **`…/end-of-year-classroom-activities.en.meta.txt:2`**: "End of Year Classroom Activities — Word Games" has no brand suffix, while the ru, sv and early-finishers pages end in `| LexiClash`. The en title is also missing the "no prep" hook.
- **Impact:** Lost long-tail impressions on the es page, and an inconsistent brand in SERPs.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-"title": "ESL Word Games para el aula — en vivo | LexiClash",
+"title": "Juegos de vocabulario en inglés para el aula — gratis | LexiClash",
-"title": "End of Year Classroom Activities — Word Games",
+"title": "End of Year Classroom Activities: Word Games, No Prep | LexiClash",
```

## 5. JSON-LD entities don't resolve, and `isPartOf` points at an org that is never defined
**`…/end-of-year-classroom-activities.en.jsonld.txt:9-11`, `:71-76`**

- **Problem:** `isPartOf: {"@id": ".../en/education#org"}` and `provider` use a per-locale `#org` id on the `/education` URL, but no standalone `EducationalOrganization` node is in these snapshots. In the ESL Course node (`esl-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:142-146`) the provider is a different, unidentified `Organization` ("LexiClash"). So you have one entity under three ids with no `sameAs`, `logo` or `@id` unification.
- **Impact:** Knowledge-graph and AI entity resolution is fragmented, so the brand is never consolidated. `isPartOf` should point to a `WebSite`, not an org.
- **Fix:** Emit one site-wide graph (root layout) and reference it everywhere:
```diff
-"provider": {"@type":"Organization","name":"LexiClash","url":"https://www.lexiclash.live"}
+"provider": {"@id":"https://www.lexiclash.live/#org"}
```
```json
{"@type":"Organization","@id":"https://www.lexiclash.live/#org","name":"LexiClash","url":"https://www.lexiclash.live","logo":"https://www.lexiclash.live/images/logo.png","sameAs":[]}
```
Change `isPartOf` to `{"@id":"https://www.lexiclash.live/#website"}`.

## 6. Invalid or low-value structured data that risks rich-result eligibility
- **`…/esl-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:135-163`**: `Course` requires `hasCourseInstance` with an instructor or schedule, and Google's Course rich result expects a real course. A 5-minute "Online" game is not one. The `Offer` also sits on a `Course` that duplicates the `LearningResource` above it.
- **`…esl-word-games.en.jsonld.txt:66-69`**: `audience.educationalRole: "student"` is wrong for a teacher-led product. The end-of-year page uses "teacher".
- **`…/end-of-year…jsonld.txt:13-18`**: `speakable` with `[data-answer]` is for news. Only add it if that attribute exists in the DOM, which I can't confirm.
- **Impact:** Possible structured-data warnings, and no rich-result upside.
- **Fix:** Drop the `Course` node. Set `educationalRole` to `teacher`. Replace the Course with a `SoftwareApplication`/`WebApplication` node (see #8).

## 7. Several FAQ claims are internally inconsistent, which hurts AI answer quality
- **`…/end-of-year…en.jsonld.txt:114`** says there's no "finishing early", while `…early-finishers-activities.*.text.txt` says "Four finished; twenty still working" and "a student who finishes 10 minutes early plays one round". The product claims contradict each other across sibling pages.
- **`…early-finishers-activities.ru.text.txt` and `.sv.text.txt`**: the trailing CTA block is in English ("Bring LexiClash to your classroom… Request teacher access"), as are the ES/HE "Are you a teacher?" blocks. Localised pages ending in English dilute language signals.
- **Impact:** Citation confusion, and an incoherent page for a model that retrieves both.
- **Fix:** Pick one truth (solo and live rounds both exist) and reword the end-of-year FAQ: "In a live game everyone plays the same board until time runs out; early finishers can start a solo round."

## 8. Agent reachability: no evidence of an agent surface, and the product is gated behind forms
**`…/esl-word-games.en.text.txt`, CTA "Request teacher access" → `/en/education/access`** (the `/en/education/access` and `/en/teacher/upgrade` links appear in every page's HREFS)

- **Observation:** The only machine-visible path to the product is a human form (`/en/education/access`) and a "Join with class code" button. I see no `/llms.txt`, MCP server, public API or CLI referenced in these snapshots (unverified: I can't see the repo root).
- **Impact:** An agent asked "set up a ESL vocab round for my class" can't act. It can only describe the product.
- **Fix (minimal, cheap):** Add `fe-next/public/llms.txt`:
```
# LexiClash
> Teacher-led multiplayer word games (Boggle-style) for classrooms. Students join with a 6-character code, no accounts. Free for 3 classes × 50 students; Teacher Pro $9/mo.
## Key pages
- [ESL word games](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/esl-word-games)
- [Classroom game](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/classroom-game)
- [Teacher access](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/access)
- [Pricing / Teacher Pro](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/teacher/upgrade)
```
Also check that `robots.txt` does not block GPTBot, ClaudeBot or PerplexityBot, and that the sitemap lists all six locales with `alternates`.

## 9. Internal links: price and CTA pages are locale-leaking, and the money pages are thin on inbound links
- **`…/early-finishers-activities.ru.text.txt` HREFS lines 17-19** and the other locales: `/en/education/access`, `/en/teacher/upgrade` and `/en/education/for-schools` link to English from ru/sv/es/he/ja pages. The `/{locale}/teacher/upgrade` link is present but the second CTA block drops to `/en/`. This sends non-English visitors to English conversion pages and passes link equity to `/en/`.
- **Impact:** Conversion leak at the revenue step, plus a mixed-language funnel.
- **Fix:** Build those hrefs with the active locale, or add localised versions of `access` and `for-schools`:
```diff
-href="/en/education/access"
+href={`/${locale}/education/access`}
```

## 10. Crawlable content: interactive pieces and anchors that carry the answer
**`…/esl-word-games.en.text.txt`, "Try a 60-second ESL round" block**

- **Observation:** The demo (A1/A2/B1 list tabs, "Start the round") renders as text in the snapshot, so it is server-rendered, which is good. But the HREFS show the main CTA as `/en/education/classroom-game?cefr=A1`, a query-parameter variant.
- **Risk:** Parameter URLs can get crawled as duplicates, and I can't see whether `classroom-game` canonicalises to itself.
- **Fix:** Confirm that `classroom-game?cefr=…` returns `<link rel="canonical" href="https://www.lexiclash.live/{locale}/education/classroom-game">`, or add `rel="nofollow"` on the CTA links.

---

### Priority order for first revenue
1. Fix #1 (accounts-required contradiction) and #7 (early-finish contradiction). They are cheap and directly affect what AI engines say.
2. Fix #9 (locale leakage on the Request access and upgrade CTAs). It is the conversion path.
3. Add #8 (llms.txt), then #5 (one Organization graph).
4. Treat #2, #3, #4, #6 and #10 as cleanup.

# SEO/GEO audit: snapshots of `/education` pages

I could only see test snapshots (`__textsnapshots__`), not the source that generates them. Line numbers below point to the snapshot evidence, and each fix names the generator to change. I haven't verified where those generators live. I also haven't seen `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt` or any API/MCP/CLI, so I can't audit those.

## 1. Russian pages point social and structured-data images at the English hero


## 2. FAQ schema contradicts the page and the pricing (trust and citation risk)
- **Evidence:** `esl-word-games.ja.jsonld.txt:97`, `esl-word-games.ru.jsonld.txt:97`, `esl-word-games.sv.jsonld.txt:97`, and the he text at `esl-word-games.he.text.txt:2` ("כן, צריך חשבון").
- **Impact:** The answer to "do students need an account?" says "Yes, accounts are required" while the same page says "no student accounts". AI answer engines will quote the contradiction, and it undermines the main selling point.
- **Diff:** Change the i18n FAQ string in all six locales.
```diff
-"Yes, accounts are required but free. Teacher creates class, students join with a code."
+"No. Students join with a six-character class code. Only teachers sign up, and it's free."
```

## 3. ESL page `keywords` are Spanish and English strings on ja/ru/sv/he
- **Evidence:** `esl-word-games.ja.meta.txt:4`, `esl-word-games.ru.meta.txt:4`, `esl-word-games.sv.meta.txt:4`.
- **Impact:** Every locale carries the same "juegos en ingles…" string. It is ignored by Google and adds nothing, and the mismatch signals a template shortcut. The first-day pages already use localized keyword arrays.
- **Diff:** Delete the key or localize it.
```diff
-"keywords": "esl word games online, ..., juegos en ingles, ..."
+"keywords": ["<localized terms per locale>"]
```

## 4. Hreflang cluster: `en-IL`, `en-JP`, `en-SE` and similar entries
- **Evidence:** `esl-word-games.ja.meta.txt:18-34` and the same block in every meta file.
- **Impact:** 23 alternates point at only 6 URLs. Region variants like `en-JP`, `en-SE` and `en-ES` add little, and `x-default` equals `/en`. This is valid but noisy, and it sends no real geo signal.
- **Diff:** Keep the six language codes plus `x-default`, and drop the speculative `en-XX` regions.
```diff
-"en-IL": ..., "en-JP": ..., "en-SE": ..., "en-ES": ..., "en-MX": ..., "en-AU": ...
```

## 5. Missing `speakable` and offers on the ESL page, plus a thin Course schema
- **Evidence:** `esl-word-games.ja.jsonld.txt:1-13` has no `speakable`, while `first-day…jsonld.txt:13-18` does. `esl-word-games.ja.jsonld.txt:135-163` has `Course` with `courseWorkload` "PT5M".
- **Impact:** Course markup without `hasCourseInstance.instructor`, and with a `provider` that duplicates the `EducationalOrganization` node, risks rich-result validation errors. A `Course` for a game is a questionable type.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-"@type": "Course"
+"@type": "LearningResource"   # already emitted at line 43, so drop the Course block
```
Also add `"speakable"` as on the first-day page.

## 6. Untranslated strings leak into localized pages
- **Evidence:** `first-day…es.text.txt:2`, `first-day…he.text.txt:2`, `first-day…ja.text.txt:2`, `first-day…sv.text.txt:2` and `first-day…ru.text.txt:2` end with "Bring LexiClash to your classroom / Request teacher access / Start 14-day free trial…". The same "Are you a teacher? / For schools & districts" block is English on the ESL pages, e.g. `esl-word-games.ja.text.txt:2`.

## 7. Localized pages link out to English conversion pages
- **Evidence:** `esl-word-games.he.text.txt:24-25` (`/en/education/access`, `/en/education/for-schools`), and the same in ja, ru and sv. Similar leaks at `first-day…es.text.txt:22-25`.


## 8. Inconsistent internal links (ru lacks list links)
- **Evidence:** `esl-word-games.ru.text.txt:10-18` has no `/ru/education/lists/...` links, while he, ja and sv link to lists at lines 10-15.

## 9. `for-schools` lacks `LearningResource`, the Offer is a hybrid, and the og image is generic
- **Evidence:** `for-schools.en.jsonld.txt:100-144`, `for-schools.en.meta.txt:43`.
- **Impact:** `Offer` has `price` plus `priceSpecification`. A "$49 per teacher / year" offer with `availability: InStock` and no checkout is mildly misleading to rich results.

```diff
+"availability": "https://schema.org/PreOrder"   # for invoice-only plan
```
Use a dedicated schools og image instead of the generic `og-image-en.webp`.

## 10. Mistranslated and machine-translated FAQ text is being published as structured data
- **Evidence:** `first-day…he.jsonld.txt:90,138` ("ילדים זז", "תפוס כמה שמות קולנו"), `first-day…sv.jsonld.txt:114` ("asynchron upptäck"), `esl-word-games.ru.jsonld.txt:105` ("быстрый вспомнил").
- **Impact:** Grammatical errors in FAQ answers reduce citation quality and trust in the quoted answers.
- **Diff:** Have a native speaker fix these strings in the catalogs. Prioritize he and ru.

## Not auditable from this input
sitemap, robots.txt, `llms.txt`, an MCP server or CLI, client-only rendering, and the `/en` root page. For revenue, an agent-facing entry point is missing from everything I can see. A short `llms.txt` stating "free: 3 classes × 50 students; Pro $9/mo; join by 6-char code" would help AI citation, since the pricing facts already exist in the `for-schools` FAQ.

The snapshot files are test fixtures, so the real fix lives in the page's `generateMetadata` and JSON-LD builders under `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/`. Ask for those files if you want exact source diffs. **I could not audit sitemap, robots, llms.txt or agent access (API/MCP/CLI) from this input. Check them separately.**

## 1. Cross-locale hrefs jump to /en on games-for-teachers (all non-en locales)
- **Where:** `games-for-teachers.es.text.txt:23-26` (same lines in he, ja, sv; `ru.text.txt:17-20`)
- **Problem:** the trial CTA and the "For schools & districts" links go to `/en/education/access`, `/en/teacher/upgrade` and `/en/education/for-schools`. The CTA copy is also untranslated English ("Bring LexiClash to your classroom", "Request teacher access").
- **Impact:** it leaks crawl equity and users to English, and it creates a conversion path in the wrong language on the page aimed at paying teachers. Localized pages also get weaker internal-link signals.

```diff
- href="/en/education/access"
+ href={`/${locale}/education/access`}
- href="/en/teacher/upgrade"
+ href={`/${locale}/teacher/upgrade`}
- href="/en/education/for-schools"
+ href={`/${locale}/education/for-schools`}
```

## 2. x-default and canonical inconsistency; for-schools x-default points to /en while some pages are not translated
- **Where:** `for-schools.*.meta.txt:11` (`x-default` is /en, which is correct)
- **Problem:** the hreflang set has 24 entries, including region variants such as `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `en-MX` and `es-AR`, all pointing to the same URL. Many of them (`en-IL`, `en-SE`, `en-JP`, `en-AU`) are invented targeting.
- **Impact:** it adds noise, and some validators flag duplicates. It brings little ranking benefit.
- **Diff:** trim to the 6 languages plus `x-default`.
```diff
- "en-IL": ..., "he-IL": ..., "en-US": ..., (all region keys)
```

## 3. OG image is English-only on translated pages and mislabeled

For ru in games-for-teachers: `education-hero-ru.webp`.

## 4. Structured data is untranslated English inside localized pages

The larger risk is that `x-default` varies between pages (see #2). Noise like this raises the chance of Google ignoring the whole hreflang cluster.
- **Diff:** keep only the 6 language codes plus x-default.
```diff
-    "en-IL": ..., "he-IL": ..., "en-US": ..., "es-US": ..., "en-GB": ..., "en-SE": ...,
-    "sv-SE": ..., "en-JP": ..., "ja-JP": ..., "en-ES": ..., "es-ES": ..., "en-MX": ...,
-    "es-MX": ..., "en-AU": ..., "es-AR": ..., "es-CO": ..., "ru-RU": ...
```

## 2. Inconsistent `x-default`
`indoor-recess-games.en.meta.txt:20` points to `/en/...`. The `.ru.meta.txt:20` file also points to `/en/...`. All of these are the same in this snapshot. Confirm that every page type uses the same rule, as page-level drift is common. Low risk here, so I list it only as a check.

## 3. Russian pages use the English hero image
`indoor-recess-games.ru.meta.txt:52,62` and `indoor-recess-games.ru.jsonld.txt:12`; also `middle-school-word-games.ru.meta.txt:52,62` and `middle-school-word-games.ru.jsonld.txt:12`.
- **Impact:** English-text social/OG previews on Russian pages lower CTR. They also send a mixed-language signal.
- **Diff:** `education-hero-en.webp` → `education-hero-ru.webp` (generate the asset, or fall back on purpose).

## 4. Untranslated English CTA block on every non-English page
`indoor-recess-games.es.text.txt:2` (and he/ja/ru/sv). Example: "Bring LexiClash to your classroom… Request teacher access… Contact for district pricing". The same block is in `middle-school-word-games.*.text.txt`.
- **Impact:** this is the revenue CTA, and it is in English on non-English pages. That hurts conversion and weakens language signals for indexing. On the es/he/ja/ru/sv pages the three upgrade/access/for-schools hrefs go to `/en/...` (`indoor-recess-games.es.text.txt:22-25`; for `ru`, `indoor-recess-games.ru.text.txt:16-19`).
- **Diff:** localise the strings and use locale-prefixed hrefs.
```diff
-/en/education/access
-/en/teacher/upgrade
-/en/education/for-schools
+/{locale}/education/access
+/{locale}/teacher/upgrade
+/{locale}/education/for-schools
```
Make the same change in the component that builds these links.

## 5. Structured data fields left in English in localised pages
`middle-school-word-games.es.jsonld.txt:57-58,64` (also he/ja/ru/sv), and every `indoor-recess-games.*.jsonld.txt:57-58`. `educationalUse`, `teaches` and `learningResourceType` stay English (`"Classroom Activity"`, `"Vocabulary acquisition…"`) while `inLanguage` says es/he/ja/ru/sv.
- **Impact:** mild. It mixes languages in the entity data that AI systems read.
- **Diff:** localise `teaches`; keep enumerated values like `educationalUse` in English, as they are controlled terms.

## 6. Duplicate and contradictory FAQ content, plus FAQPage mismatch
`middle-school-word-games.es.text.txt` (and he/ja/ru/sv) contains **two** FAQ sections with the same questions and different answers. For example, the first says "No… Sin registro" and the second adds the sign-in option. The JSON-LD (`middle-school-word-games.es.jsonld.txt:84-148`) matches only the second one.
- **Impact:** visible FAQ ≠ schema FAQ, which risks FAQ rich-result ineligibility. The duplicated content also dilutes the passage that an AI would cite. The English page has only one FAQ, so this is a locale-specific regression.
- **Diff:** delete the older "Preguntas Frecuentes" block from the non-English middle-school templates (keep the one that matches the JSON-LD).

## 7. Garbled machine translation on indexed pages
- `middle-school-word-games.he.text.txt:2`: "הימורים שקול", "תלמידי חטיבה יודעים—ויפילו—פדגוגיה".
- `indoor-recess-games.he.jsonld.txt:119-122`: "מה אם האינטרנט נמוך?" ("what if the internet is low").
- `indoor-recess-games.he.text.txt:2`: "יחידיפאזל יומי ב־one" (a stray English "one").
- `indoor-recess-games.ja.jsonld.txt:87`: "学生はログイン…".
- **Impact:** poor quality signals, and these text blocks are what generative engines quote. A bad quote hurts brand trust and teacher conversion.
- **Fix:** have a native speaker review, or drop the page for locales you cannot maintain. A noindex, or removal from the hreflang set, is better than low-quality text.

## 8. Titles and descriptions are weak or have issues
- `middle-school-word-games.en.meta.txt:2` ("Word Games for Middle School | Vocabulary Building") has no brand and no free/no-signup hook. The indoor-recess title does include the brand.
- `middle-school-word-games.ja.meta.txt:3` mixes full-width and half-width punctuation.
- `middle-school-word-games.he.meta.txt:3`: "תחרות אמיתיות" is ungrammatical.
- `indoor-recess-games.en.meta.txt:3`: the description is ~150 characters, which is fine.
- **Diff (EN):**
```diff
-"title": "Word Games for Middle School | Vocabulary Building",
+"title": "Free Word Games for Middle School | No Sign-up | LexiClash",
```

## 9. `keywords` meta and `speakable` are not worth the markup
`…meta.txt:4-13` (`keywords`) is ignored by Google. `speakable` (`…jsonld.txt:13-18`) only helps news publishers, and its `[data-answer]` selector needs to exist in the DOM.
- **Impact:** none. It is just maintenance cost and a potential invalid-markup flag.
- **Diff:** remove `keywords` and `speakable`, or keep them but verify `data-answer` renders.

## 10. Entity gaps for AI citation
The schema defines an `EducationalOrganization` with `@id` `…/education#org` (`indoor-recess-games.en.jsonld.txt:73`), but that node is only referenced and never defined on these pages. `isPartOf` in the WebPage node (line 9-11) points to an org id, not a `WebSite`. No `Organization`/`SoftwareApplication` node carries `sameAs`, price, or `offers`. The Teacher Pro price ($9/mo) appears only in body text.
- **Impact:** AI engines cannot resolve the entity or its offer, so product and pricing citations are less likely.
- **Diff:** emit once, sitewide:
```json
{"@type":"SoftwareApplication","@id":".../#app","name":"LexiClash","applicationCategory":"EducationalApplication","operatingSystem":"Web","offers":[{"@type":"Offer","price":"0","priceCurrency":"USD"},{"@type":"Offer","name":"Teacher Pro","price":"9","priceCurrency":"USD"}]}
```
and define the `#org` node it references.

## Positives seen
- Canonicals are self-referencing and correct.
- BreadcrumbList is present and valid.
- Content is server-rendered: the `.text.txt` snapshots contain full body text, so it is crawlable.
- Internal links are locale-prefixed, except the CTA links in #4.
- Each page has a good answer-first passage ("What are good indoor recess games…"), which suits AI citation.

# SEO and GEO audit: LexiClash education pages

**Scope.** I only had the test snapshots for `sight-words-practice` and `spelling-bee-practice` (meta, JSON-LD, text and hrefs, across locales). I did not see `robots.ts`, `sitemap.ts`, `llms.txt`, the root layout, or any API/MCP/CLI code. Item 9 covers what I could not audit.

Diffs below show the **expected snapshot output**. The metadata generator in `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/**` must change to produce them, and I couldn't see it. Update the snapshots after the generator change.

---

## 1. Titles and descriptions are truncated in search results
- **Where:** `sight-words-practice.en.meta.txt:2-3` (title 69 chars, description 241), `spelling-bee-practice.en.meta.txt:2-3` (title 76, description 243).
- **Impact:** Google cuts titles at about 60 chars and descriptions at about 155. The brand suffix and the "no signup" hook get cut, so click-through rate on your two best-fit pages drops. The same strings are reused in `og:title`, `twitter:title` and the JSON-LD `name`, so the problem repeats everywhere.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-  "title": "Sight Words Practice Online — Free Dolch & Fry Word Games | LexiClash",
-  "description": "Free sight words practice with real games. Paste any Dolch or Fry list into a custom word list, then drill it with flashcards, word matching, spelling challenges, and whole-class word games. No student accounts, no downloads — just a browser.",
+  "title": "Sight Words Practice: Free Dolch & Fry Games | LexiClash",
+  "description": "Paste any Dolch or Fry list and drill it with flashcards, matching and spelling games. Students join with a code: no accounts, no downloads.",
```
```diff
-  "title": "Spelling Bee Practice Online — Free Word Games for Spelling Champions | LexiClash",
-  "description": "Free online spelling bee practice through word games. Boggle-style grids, anagram drills, word wheels, and 1v1 spelling duels. No signup, no app — just open the browser and start practicing for Scripps, regional bees, or classroom spelling tests.",
+  "title": "Spelling Bee Practice Online, Free | LexiClash",
+  "description": "Free spelling bee practice: Boggle grids, word wheels, anagram drills and 1v1 duels. No signup. Use Scripps or custom word lists.",
```
Keep the long text for `og:description`. Also fix the JSON-LD `name` in `spelling-bee-practice.en.jsonld.txt:45` to the short page name, as the sight-words page already does at `sight-words-practice.en.jsonld.txt:45`.

## 2. Conflicting signals on the untranslated sight-words locales
- **Where:** `sight-words-practice.{es,he,ja,ru,sv}.meta.txt:6-9`. They are `noindex`, canonical to `/en/`, and `og:url` and JSON-LD `url` point to the locale's own URL.
- **Impact:**
  - The pages mix `noindex` with a cross-URL canonical and a self-referencing `og:url`. Google treats that as a mixed signal.
  - Indexed pages link into them. For example `spelling-bee-practice.es.text.txt:20` links to `/es/education/sight-words-practice`, so internal links and crawl budget go to a page you told Google to drop.
  - The body is English with some translated chrome. If it ever gets indexed, it duplicates the English page.
- **Fix:** Pick one strategy. Cheapest: keep `noindex`, self-canonical, no JSON-LD, and link to the `/en/` version from translated pages until translated.
```diff
-  "canonical": "https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/sight-words-practice",
+  "canonical": "https://www.lexiclash.live/es/education/sight-words-practice",
```
Do not emit the WebPage, FAQ or Organization nodes from `sight-words-practice.es.jsonld.txt:1-173` when `robots.index=false`. Change the link in `spelling-bee-practice.es.text.txt:20` to `/en/education/sight-words-practice`.

## 3. Revenue CTAs leave the locale
- **Where:** `sight-words-practice.es.text.txt:24-25` (and `he:24-25`, `ja:24-25`, `ru:18-19`, `sv:24-25`), `spelling-bee-practice.es.text.txt:24-25`. The hrefs `/en/education/access` and `/en/education/for-schools` are hardcoded to `/en`.
- **Impact:**
  - "Get teacher access" and "Contact for district pricing" are the school-revenue paths. A Hebrew, Spanish or Japanese visitor lands on an English page, and the locale-switch state is lost.
  - The CTA copy is also untranslated ("Are you a teacher?").
- **Diff (in the CTA component):**
```diff
-<Link href="/en/education/access">
+<Link href={`/${locale}/education/access`}>
-<Link href="/en/education/for-schools">
+<Link href={`/${locale}/education/for-schools`}>
```
If the localized routes don't exist yet, state that deliberately. Don't let it fall out of a string constant.

## 4. The FAQ contradicts the pricing in structured data (trust problem for AI citations)
- **Where:** `spelling-bee-practice.es.jsonld.txt:144` ("completamente gratis, sin nivel premium"), `he.jsonld.txt:144`, `ja.jsonld.txt:144`. The English version at `en.jsonld.txt:144` is correct ("solo practice at home is free"). Teacher Pro at $9/mo is advertised at `sight-words-practice.en.jsonld.txt:179`.
- **Impact:** LLMs quote FAQ answers verbatim. An answer saying "no premium tier" next to a $9 Pro plan reads as a false claim and lowers citation trust.
- **Diff (es):**
```diff
-"text": "Sí — completamente gratis, sin nivel premium, sin tarifa por usuario. Abre el navegador, ..."
+"text": "Sí — la práctica individual en casa es gratis, sin tarifa por usuario. Abre el navegador, ..."
```
Apply the equivalent change to the he and ja strings.

## 5. Hreflang bloat and nonstandard variants
- **Where:** `spelling-bee-practice.en.meta.txt:10-35` (24 entries).
- **Impact:**
  - Entries like `en-SE`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `en-MX`, `en-AU`, `es-AR` and `es-CO` all point to the same URL as the bare language code.
  - They add nothing for Google and make the set harder to maintain. Every page must also list every other, and any one that omits a variant breaks reciprocity.
  - Sight-words only declares `en` (`sight-words-practice.en.meta.txt:10-13`), so the two clusters are inconsistent.
- **Diff:** Keep the six language codes plus `x-default`.
```diff
   "languages": {
     "x-default": ".../en/education/spelling-bee-practice",
     "he": ..., "en": ..., "sv": ..., "ja": ..., "es": ..., "ru": ...,
-    "en-IL": ..., "he-IL": ..., "en-US": ..., "es-US": ..., "en-GB": ..., ... "ru-RU": ...
   },
```

## 6. Inconsistent and camelCase practice URLs
- **Where:** `spelling-bee-practice.en.text.txt:6,9,10` link to `/en/practice/wordHunt`, `/practice/wheelRush` and `/practice/classic`. The sight-words page links to `/en/daily/word-hunt` and `/en/daily/word-wheel` (`sight-words-practice.en.text.txt:6,9`).
- **Impact:**
  - Two URL schemes exist for the same game modes ("word hunt" via `/practice/wordHunt` and `/daily/word-hunt`).
  - camelCase paths are case-sensitive and easy to duplicate. The rename `wheelRush` vs `word-wheel` suggests the same mode under two names.
  - Anchor text and inbound links split across two URLs.
- **Fix:** Pick kebab-case, 301 the old path (`/practice/wordHunt` to `/practice/word-hunt`), and link consistently from all education pages. Confirm in the sitemap that only canonical forms are listed.

## 7. JSON-LD graph is thin on the site entity
- **Where:** `sight-words-practice.en.jsonld.txt:9-11` (`isPartOf` points to the `EducationalOrganization`), and the org node at `:148-207`.
- **Impact:**
  - `isPartOf` should reference a `WebSite`, not an organization. The Organization is also re-emitted on every page with a locale-specific `@id` (`/en/education#org`, `/es/education#org`, ...), so entities fragment per locale and per section.
  - It has no `logo` or `sameAs`.
  - The `Offer`s are not linked to a product via `itemOffered`, so a model can't tell what the $9 buys.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-  "isPartOf": { "@id": "https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education#org" },
+  "isPartOf": { "@id": "https://www.lexiclash.live/#website" },
+  "publisher": { "@id": "https://www.lexiclash.live/#org" },
```
Emit one site-wide `Organization` and one `WebSite` in the root layout, and add `logo` and `sameAs` to the Organization. Give the Teacher Pro offer `"itemOffered": {"@type":"Service","name":"LexiClash Teacher Pro"}` and a `"url"` of `/{locale}/teacher/upgrade`.

## 8. Low-value structured data effort, and a small localization miss
- **Where:** HowTo nodes (`sight-words-practice.en.jsonld.txt:209-242`) and FAQPage nodes (`:75-146`). Breadcrumb leaf names are untranslated at `sight-words-practice.es.jsonld.txt:35`, `he:35`, `ja:35` and `ru:35`. The `ru` page's image falls back to English at `ru.jsonld.txt:12` and `ru.meta.txt:21`.
- **Impact:**
  - Google no longer shows HowTo rich results, and FAQ rich results are limited to government and health sites. This markup helps LLM readability only, so don't invest more here.
  - Breadcrumb leaf labels in other languages show English in the SERP trail.
- **Diff:** Localize the leaf name in the breadcrumb builder.
```diff
-"name": "Sight Words Practice Online",
+"name": t('education.sightWords.breadcrumb'),
```
Generate `education-hero-ru.webp`.

## 9. Not auditable from what you gave me: agent reachability
I could not confirm any of the following:
- `app/robots.ts` and `app/sitemap.ts`: whether `noindex` locale pages are excluded from the sitemap, and whether AI crawlers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, Google-Extended) are allowed.

What the snapshots show about agent access:

Minimum for first revenue:

3. Send me those files if you want exact diffs.

## 10. Meta `keywords` is dead weight and leaks English into localized pages
- **Where:** `spelling-bee-practice.es.meta.txt:4`, `he:4`, `ja:4` (English keyword strings on translated pages). Also `sight-words-practice.en.meta.txt:4`.
- **Impact:**
  - Google ignores the keywords meta tag.
  - It does expose the same list to competitors, and shows the localized pages were not keyword-researched in their own language.
  - The localized titles are real translations, but the query phrases in Spanish, Hebrew and Japanese (for example "práctica de ortografía") were not used as H1 or in the description.
- **Diff:** Remove the `keywords` field from the metadata builder.
```diff
-  keywords: t('seo.keywords'),
```
Spend the effort instead on localized descriptions with the head query, for example in es: `"Práctica de ortografía gratis: Boggle, anagramas y duelos 1v1. Sin registro."`

## Shard 25

# SEO/GEO audit: education pages (from the snapshots provided)

I only have test snapshots and tests for `/education/*`. I can't see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP surface, so I have no findings on those and have not guessed at them. All findings below come from rendered output.

## 1. Vocabulary page FAQ and HowTo claim "students must sign up", and the page says the opposite
`vocabulary-games-classroom.en.jsonld.txt:85-88`, `:151-152`, mirrored in all locales (es:85-88, he:85-88, ja:85-88, ru:85-88, sv:85-88).

The FAQ answers "Yes, but it's free… One email or Google account." The visible page (`…en.text.txt:2`) says "Join a class game right now — no account… Nothing asks for an email or a password." The HowTo step 2 says students "log in with their free LexiClash account." AI engines quote FAQ JSON-LD verbatim, so they will tell teachers the product needs student accounts. That is your main differentiator against Quizlet and Kahoot, and it is being misstated. The page copy also says "Student accounts free" and "Pro adds…" in places, which makes the contradiction worse.

Diff (en; apply the same change per locale):
```diff
-"text": "Yes, but it's free and takes 30 seconds. One email or Google account, that's it. No credit card, no upsell."
+"text": "No. Students join a class game with a six-character class code and a display name, with no email or password. Optional free accounts save XP and progress."
```
```diff
-"text": "Students log in with their free LexiClash account on any device. The teacher's session appears in their dashboard."
+"text": "Students open the join page on any device, enter the six-character class code and a display name, and they are in. No account needed."
```
Update the "under 60 seconds setup once students have their free accounts" description at line 139 the same way.

## 2. Spelling-bee pages state a different class-code format and size than the vocabulary page
`spelling-bee-practice.ru.jsonld.txt:136` says "up to 30 people join with a 4-digit code". The vocabulary page says a six-character code and 50 players (`depthSections.test.ts:73-75` pins 50). The ja text (`…ja.text.txt`) says a 6-character code. Citing engines will surface the conflicting figures. The test file in `depthSections.test.ts` only guards the `depth` blocks, not FAQs.

```diff
-"...(до 30 человек присоединяются по коду из 4 цифр, без личных аккаунтов)..."
+"...(до 50 человек присоединяются по коду из 6 символов, без личных аккаунтов)..."
```
Also add a test that scans the FAQ JSON-LD for "4-digit", "30 students", and "sign up".

## 3. Spelling-bee ru JSON-LD claims "no premium tier, no paid features" while the page sells Teacher Pro
`spelling-bee-practice.ru.jsonld.txt:144` ("никаких платных функций, никакой подписки"). Same on sv:144 ("ingen premium-nivå"). The same page's text advertises "Teacher Pro — $9/мес". This hurts trust when engines quote it, and it hurts revenue because the paid tier is denied in the answer. Scope the claim to individual students:
```diff
-"Да — полностью бесплатно, никаких платных функций, никакой подписки."
+"Да — для учеников, занимающихся дома, всё бесплатно, без подписки. Платный Teacher Pro ($9/мес) нужен только учителям для неограниченных классов."
```
The ja text already does this correctly, so copy that wording.

## 4. Russian pages use the English hero image
`vocabulary-games-classroom.ru.meta.txt:43,53`, `vocabulary-games-classroom.ru.jsonld.txt:12`, `spelling-bee-practice.ru.meta.txt:43,53`, `…ru.jsonld.txt:12` all point at `education-hero-en.webp`. Every other locale has its own image. Shares of ru pages show English text on the card, which lowers click-through. Either generate `education-hero-ru.webp` and switch the path, or accept English and drop the localized alt. Diff: `-education-hero-en.webp` / `+education-hero-ru.webp`.

## 5. Spelling-bee ja/sv text is untranslated or contains machine-translation artifacts in indexed copy and titles
- `spelling-bee-practice.ja.text.txt:2` includes "Sight Words Practice Online" and untranslated English blocks ("Are you a teacher?", "For schools & districts") on every locale page.
- `…sv.jsonld.txt:101`, `:125`, and the "Stavningspraktik Online" title use odd coinages such as "stavningstävlingsmonstrant" and "stavningsdelagare" (mistranslations of "participant"). Swedish searchers will not use these words, so you lose impressions on "stavning öva".
- The ru "Teacher Pro" block is also in English at the end of every page.

Fix: replace "stavningsdelagare" with "stavningstävlande" or "elever" throughout, and localize the three teacher-CTA strings.

## 6. Hreflang cluster is invalid in structure
`*.meta.txt` "languages" (for example `vocabulary-games-classroom.en.meta.txt:11-34`) emits 24 entries. Region variants (`en-IL`, `en-SE`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `en-MX`, `en-AU`, `es-AR`…) all point at the same URL as the bare language. These are redundant, and some are semantically wrong (`en-JP`, `en-SE` → English). That adds noise and gives Google's hreflang parser more room to ignore the cluster. Keep `x-default` plus the 6 bare languages, and add region codes only where the content differs.

```diff
-    "en-IL": ..., "he-IL": ..., "en-US": ..., "es-US": ..., "en-GB": ..., "en-SE": ...,
-    "sv-SE": ..., "en-JP": ..., "ja-JP": ..., "en-ES": ..., "es-ES": ..., "en-MX": ...,
-    "es-MX": ..., "en-AU": ..., "es-AR": ..., "es-CO": ..., "ru-RU": ...
```
Also, `ogLocale` for `es` is `es_ES` while the audience is mostly Latin American, so check that.

## 7. Spelling-bee ja and sv pages emit a broken breadcrumb/H1 and a reversed-meaning title
`spelling-bee-practice.ja.text.txt:2` begins "Backスペリング練習オンライン…" where "Back" is concatenated nav text. The first text node an agent reads is a "Back" link. The ja copy has "★スペリング 練習オンライン。" with a stray space, and the visible H1 is split into spans. Make the back link `aria-hidden` or move it after the `<h1>`, and render the H1 as a single text node.

## 8. Schema type mismatches and weak entities
- `vocabulary-games-classroom.*.jsonld.txt` emits `Course` with `Offer price 0`, plus `WebPage`, `LearningResource`, `FAQPage` and `HowTo`. Google restricts Course rich results to real courses, so there is little upside and a risk of a manual-action flag. Drop `Course` and keep `LearningResource`.
- `isPartOf.@id` is `…/education#org`, but the `EducationalOrganization` node is only defined inside `provider`, not at the hub. A `Course` provider is a separate unlinked `Organization`. Use one `@id` (`https://www.lexiclash.live/#org`) everywhere.
- `educationalUse`, `teaches` and `educationalLevel` stay in English in every locale (for example ru:51-63). Translate `teaches`.
- The `HowTo` node exists only on en (absent in es/he/ja/ru/sv). Parity matters if you keep the node.

## 9. Missing `Offer` for the thing you actually sell
No `SoftwareApplication`/`Product` with the Teacher Pro `$9/month` offer is in any JSON-LD. The price appears only in the visible text. Add to the education hub and vocab page:
```json
{"@type":"SoftwareApplication","name":"LexiClash Teacher Pro","applicationCategory":"EducationalApplication","operatingSystem":"Web","offers":[{"@type":"Offer","price":"0","priceCurrency":"USD","name":"Free (3 classes × 50 students)"},{"@type":"Offer","price":"9","priceCurrency":"USD","name":"Teacher Pro (monthly)"}]}
```
This is the main route for an engine to cite your pricing correctly. Pull the figures from `FREE_TIER_LIMITS` and `TEACHER_PRO_PRICE_USD` so they cannot drift.

## 10. Internal links: cross-locale leaks and missing hub links
- Every page links `/en/education/access` and `/en/education/for-schools` from ja/ru/sv/es/he (for example `spelling-bee-practice.ja.text.txt:24-25`). Non-English readers get dropped into English, and the English URLs are the targets of those locale pages' hreflang. Localize them to `/${locale}/…` (if those routes exist) or label them as English.
- Spelling-bee ru links to no list pages (`…ru.text.txt:5-19`), while ja/sv link to grade lists and a language list. Add `/ru/education/lists/english` and the `/ru/education/lists/russian` equivalent.
- The vocab ru page also lacks the list links that es, he, ja and sv have (`…ru.text.txt:5-17`). Add the topic and language list links.
- `/ja/practice/wordHunt`, `/ru/practice/wheelRush` use camelCase path segments; check that the canonical practice URLs are not lowercase-kebab elsewhere (`/daily/word-hunt` is kebab). Inconsistent casing risks duplicate URLs.

## Not assessed
`sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `llms.txt`, client-only rendering of the game itself, and agent access (API/MCP/CLI) are outside the files provided. A product with a code-based join flow and per-list drills would benefit from a documented list-import endpoint and an `llms.txt` pointing at the education hub. I can't recommend specific diffs without seeing the app routes.

# SEO / GEO audit: education surface only

The education work is already heavily guarded: JSON-LD locale, claim drift, orphan links, and server-rendered speakable and HowTo markup. I'm not re-litigating that. The remaining issues are below, highest impact first.

## 1. `/education/duels` has no `<h1>` in server HTML
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/__tests__/subpageSeoContent.test.tsx:91-94` (the test pins the gap), with the reason at lines 82-83 and 96-111.
- **Impact:** The crawler view of a page that already has HowTo JSON-LD has no primary heading. This weakens topical relevance and AI-snippet extraction. The h1 only appears after hydration, via `DuelHistory.tsx:127`, for signed-in students.
- **Diff** (file not shown, so adjust names):
```diff
// duels/page.tsx: emit the h1 server-side through the seo block

+ <GamePageSeoContent ... asH1 />

// components/education/duels/DuelHistory.tsx:127
- <h1 ...>{title}</h1>
+ <h2 ...>{title}</h2>
```
```diff
// subpageSeoContent.test.tsx:91-94
- it('/duels does NOT claim an h1 — its client half already renders one', ...
-   expect(container.querySelectorAll('h1')).toHaveLength(0);
+ it('/duels ships exactly one server-rendered h1', ...
```

Also change the guard at lines 96-111 to assert that `DuelHistory` has no `<h1`.

## 2. `sight-words-practice` may advertise hreflang to noindexed locales
- **Where:** `landingSchemaLocale.test.ts:119-123` and `:126-135` (all pages must use the shared ~24-entry hreflang map). `learningResourceLocale.test.ts:54-86` shows the non-en routes are `noindex, follow` with canonical to `/en`.
- **Impact:** The `en` page declares alternates that point at noindex URLs which canonicalize back to `/en`. Google discards the cluster, and its own docs call this a conflicting signal. Result: a lower chance the `/en` page is picked for the other locales' queries.
- **Diff:** In the page's `generateMetadata`, for the English-body page only:
```diff
- alternates: { canonical, languages: hreflangAlternates(path) },
+ alternates: { canonical: `https://www.lexiclash.live/en${path}` }, // no languages map
```
Also drop the page from the localized entries in `app/sitemap.ts`. I haven't seen either file, so please verify.

## 3. Noindex plus cross-canonical on the same URL
- **Where:** `learningResourceLocale.test.ts:5-8` (noindex, follow, with canonical to /en).
- **Impact:** Pairing noindex with a canonical to another URL is a mixed signal. Google may ignore one of them. Pick one signal. Keep `noindex` and set the canonical to self, or drop `noindex` and keep the cross-canonical.

## 4. `access/page.tsx` has no `ru` metadata
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/access/page.tsx:5-11`. `META` has en/he/sv/ja/es but no `ru`, although six locales ship.
- **Impact:** Low for search, since the page is noindex. `/ru/education/access` shows English titles in tabs, shared links, and previews, which hurts teacher conversion from Russian traffic.
- **Diff:**
```diff
   es: { ... },
+  ru: { title: 'Запрос доступа для учителя — LexiClash', description: 'Бесплатный доступ к LexiClash для учителей. Отправьте запрос по почте и начните использовать классные игры со словами.' },
 };
```

## 5. The metadata guards only cover five pages; the six teacher-moment landings are unchecked
- **Where:** `metaSelfConsistency.test.ts:100-106` lists 5 PAGES. The six `EducationLandingContent` pages, with `meta.*` objects from `teacherMomentContent.test.ts:18-58`, are not in the title/description/og consistency run. `eslTeacherIntent.test.ts:50-52` caps title length at 75 only for ESL.
- **Impact:** Titles above ~60 characters get truncated in the SERP, lowering CTR across 36 localized URLs. Nothing stops this.
- **Diff** (add to `teacherMomentContent.test.ts`, inside the locale loop):
```diff
+ expect(c.meta.title.length, `${slug}/${locale} title >65`).toBeLessThanOrEqual(65);
+ expect(c.meta.description.length, `${slug}/${locale} desc >160`).toBeLessThanOrEqual(160);
```
Use a CJK-aware limit for `ja` (about 35 characters).

## 6. Agent reachability: no evidence of an API, MCP server, or CLI
- **Where:** Nothing in the input. The only agent-facing artifacts visible are `public/llms.txt` and `public/llms-full.txt`, which `educationClaims.test.ts:168-172` guards for claim drift.
- **Impact:** An answer engine can quote the product but can't act on it. The no-account guest path (`/education/classroom-game`, per `noAccountCta.test.ts:112-116`) is the obvious thing to expose. Given the goal of first revenue, the most valuable exposure would be the teacher flow of creating a class, getting a six-character code, and linking to `/teacher/upgrade`.

```diff
+ ## Try it without an account
+ - [Start a classroom game](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/classroom-game): a teacher creates a game, students join with a 6-character code, no student account needed.
+ - [Teacher Pro, $9/month](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/teacher/upgrade): analytics, reports, pressure dials, unlimited classes.
```
I'd defer building an MCP server until the landing pages show conversions, and I haven't confirmed whether one already exists.

## Shard 28

# SEO / GEO audit: `education/classroom-game`, `education/duels`, `education/early-finishers-activities`

Scope: only the files shown. I can't see `generatePageMetadata`, `buildEducationLandingMetadata`, `educationSubpageJsonLd`, the sitemap, robots, `llms.txt` or any API/MCP surface. Claims about those are marked as unverified, and I list fewer than 10 issues because the rest of the shown code is fine.

## 1. Classroom-game SEO copy and `<h1>` sit in a 0×0 clipped box (highest impact)
**`fe-next/app/[locale]/education/classroom-game/LobbySeoTail.tsx:18-22`, used at `page.tsx:62-81`**

- **What's wrong:** `absolute h-0 w-0 overflow-hidden` hides the only `<h1>`, the HowTo steps and the for-schools CTA link.
- **Why it matters:** Google treats content that is invisible to users as low-value for ranking. The HowTo JSON-LD at `page.tsx:44` describes steps that no user ever sees, which is a structured-data guideline risk. The for-schools link, the only internal link to the revenue funnel on this page, is inside the clipped box too.
- **Impact:** the page is likely demoted, and it gets no rich result and no AI citation. `/education/for-schools` loses its internal-link equity from a page that, per `page.tsx:51-56`, is its primary CTA target.
- **Diff:** render the copy visibly for signed-out visitors. This is the audience crawlers match, since the guest demo renders when there is no user. Move the copy out of the clip wrapper and into `ClassroomGuestDemo`, or into a normal-flow section below the demo.

```diff
-      <LobbySeoTail>
-      <GamePageSeoContent ... asH1 />
-      <section ...>...</section>
-      </LobbySeoTail>
+      {/* Visible to guests; PageClient locks the body only for signed-in teachers */}
+      <GamePageSeoContent ... asH1 />
+      <section ...>...</section>
```

Then drop the `setIsInGame(true)` body lock for guests. It only needs to run inside `ClassroomGameInner`, which already calls it.

## 2. Duels: the SEO copy lives in a client-only, auth-dependent branch
**`duels/PageClient.tsx:120-139`, `duels/page.tsx:39-47`**

- **What's wrong:** the SEO copy is passed as `seoContent` into a `'use client'` tree. It is server-rendered only because the empty branch happens to render when `user` is null. Once a signed-in student with a classroom loads, the copy sits at the end of a locked scroller (`PageClient.tsx:244`).
- **Why it matters:** the page has no `<h1>` for signed-out visitors. `page.tsx:37-38` deliberately omits `asH1` because `DuelHistory` emits one, but `DuelHistory` is not mounted in the guest/crawler branch. The crawler therefore sees no heading, so the title and the on-page topic don't line up.
- **Diff:** the guest branch should include the h1.

```diff
 // page.tsx
-            <GamePageSeoContent title=... features=... />
+            <GamePageSeoContent title=... features=... asH1 />
```

and in `PageClient.tsx` pass a variant that has no h1 into the signed-in branch (line 244), so there is only one `<h1>` per state.

## 3. Hardcoded English strings for `FOR_SCHOOLS_*` fall back by locale
**`classroom-game/page.tsx:15-30, 71, 77`**

- **What's wrong:** unsupported locales get English copy under a localized URL. This is minor, but it adds to the invisible-block problem in #1.
- **Fix:** move these strings to the shared locale files so `generatePageMetadata` and the visible copy stay in sync.

## 4. `force-dynamic` on indexable marketing routes
**`classroom-game/page.tsx:11`, `duels/page.tsx:8`**

- **What's wrong:** every crawl and every AI-bot fetch hits the origin. The early-finishers page correctly uses `revalidate = 86400` (`early-finishers-activities/page.tsx:9`).
- **Why it matters:** this raises TTFB and cuts crawl budget. The cause is `useSearchParams` in the client tree, which Suspense already handles.
- **Diff:**

```diff

+export const revalidate = 86400;
```

Verify that `generatePageMetadata` doesn't read headers or cookies. If it does, keep `force-dynamic`.

## 5. Early-finishers content is thin on locale-specific structure; Hebrew/ru/sv `answer` and FAQ copy is weak for citation
**`early-finishers-activities/content.ts:50-54, 215-219, 875-879`**

**`content.ts:45-48`**

**`content.ts:18-19, 183, 348`**

# SEO/GEO audit: middle-school and vocabulary-topics landing pages

**Scope caveat.** This diff only shows the content layer of three education landings: middle-school, vocabulary-topics, and esl-word-games class games. I cannot see `buildEducationLandingMetadata`, `EducationLandingTemplate`, `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. I make no claims about hreflang, canonical, sitemap, robots, or agent reachability. Those need a separate pass over `lib/seo/*` and `app/`. Every issue below is verifiable in the files shown.

## 1. Hebrew/Swedish/Japanese/Russian metadata on middle-school and topics pages describes the wrong audience
**Impact:** The SERP snippet and every AI summary of the localized page say "primary class" or "first grade". That mismatches the title and H1, which cuts click-through. Because the content is machine-visible, it also makes AI answers misattribute the page's age group in 4 of 6 locales.

- `middle-school/copy.he-sv.ts:6` says "לכיתת יסוד" (primary class).
- `middle-school/copy.he-sv.ts:73` says "lågstadieklass" (lower primary).
- `middle-school/copy.ja-ru.ts:6` says "児童ごとに" (per elementary pupil).
- `middle-school/copy.ja-ru.ts:73` says "для начального класса".
- `vocabulary-topics/copy.he-sv.ts:6`, `:84` and `vocabulary-topics/copy.ja-ru.ts:84` carry the same kind of copy-paste.

## 4. The Course JSON-LD gets the meta title and description as its name and description
**Impact:** `landing.ts:19` (middle-school) and `landing.ts:19` (topics) pass `c.metaTitle` and `c.metaDescription` into `educationCourseJsonLd`. The structured-data `name` ends up as "…| LexiClash", with a brand suffix and an em-dash keyword string. Course rich-result validators and LLM extractors treat that as the course name. Because this is a game rather than a course, a `Course` type is also questionable. There is no `FAQPage` or `ItemList` for the six class games, which are the most extractable content on these pages. I can't confirm whether `buildEnglishLearnerLanding` adds FAQPage, so check that.


## 5. JSON-LD URL is hard-coded and bypasses locale and canonical rules
**Impact:** `jsonLd.ts:3-13` in both folders builds `https://www.lexiclash.live/${locale}${PAGE_PATH}`. If the canonical logic in `buildEducationLandingMetadata` omits the default-locale prefix or uses a different host, the structured-data URL conflicts with the canonical, and Google may ignore the entity. I can't verify this without `lib/seo`.

```diff
- const BASE_URL = 'https://www.lexiclash.live';
+ import { SITE_URL, localizedUrl } from '@/lib/seo/urls'; // reuse whatever buildEducationLandingMetadata uses
- url: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}${PAGE_PATH}`,
+ url: localizedUrl(locale, PAGE_PATH),
```

## 6. The topic word lists render in all locales but use an inconsistent English label pattern
**Impact:** `copy.en.ts:122-131` is a good crawlable list, with the words as plain text. In the other locales the labels mix "(food)" etc., which is fine. The impact is that the lists are only worth citing if rendered server-side. `revalidate = 86400` in `page.tsx:8` suggests static rendering, which is fine. Add `ItemList` or `DefinedTermSet` JSON-LD so AI engines can lift "English food vocabulary: bread, apple…" directly.

```diff
+ { '@type': 'ItemList', name: 'English vocabulary by topic', itemListElement: topics.groups.map((g, i) => ({ '@type': 'ListItem', position: i + 1, name: g.label, description: g.words.join(', ') })) },
```


# SEO/GEO audit: education landing pages and word-list routes

**Scope.** Only the files shown were reviewed. These are `games-for-teachers`, `indoor-recess-games`, `irregular-verbs-games`, the `help` pages and the `lists/[lang]` routes. The sitemap, robots.txt, llms.txt, `buildEducationLandingMetadata`, `JsonLd` and `EducationLandingTemplate` were not provided, so I make no claims about canonical or hreflang output, nor about what is in the sitemap. Item 10 covers what I could not verify.

## 1. The pages contradict each other on whether students need accounts (conversion and citation trust)
`games-for-teachers/landing.ts:13` is the quotable GEO answer. It says "no app or student account". The same page's `content.ts` says the opposite:
- `content.ts:60`: "Free account required".
- `content.ts:72`: "Free student accounts — one-time setup, tracks XP".
- `content.ts:94`: "Create a free account".

`indoor-recess-games/content.ts:135` says "no accounts, no login". `content.ts:170` says "No emails, no accounts".

**Impact.** An LLM that sees both on one URL either hedges or drops the citation. Visitors who read "no login" and then hit a signup wall bounce, which costs you first revenue.

**Fix.** Make one statement true everywhere. Check against the product, but the likely truth is that joining needs no account and per-student tracking needs one.
```diff
- content.ts:60   ctaSubLabel: 'Free account required • No credit card',
+ content.ts:60   ctaSubLabel: 'Teacher account free • Students join with a code • No credit card',
- content.ts:72   text: 'Free student accounts — one-time setup, tracks XP and progress across sessions'
+ content.ts:72   text: 'Students join with a code. Optional free accounts add XP and per-student progress'
```
Apply the equivalent change to the `he`, `es`, `sv`, `ja` and `ru` lines (151, 230, 309, 388, 467). Also fix the "Create a free account" FAQ answers at lines 94, 173, 252, 331, 410 and 489.

## 2. A false "works offline" claim contradicts the site's own FAQ
`games-for-teachers/content.ts:110` says "Works offline… via cached assets". `indoor-recess-games/content.ts:186` says "These games need internet". The same claim is repeated at lines 347 (sv), 426 (ja) and 505 (ru). Multiplayer cannot work offline.

**Impact.** This is a factual error an AI answer engine can repeat, and a teacher who relies on it gets a broken lesson.
```diff
- a: '... Works offline in classrooms with patchy WiFi via cached assets.',
+ a: '... Needs an internet connection; it copes with typical school WiFi.',
```

## 3. Word-list routes are the programmatic SEO surface but have no static generation or caching
- `lists/[lang]/page.tsx:6-27` and `lists/[lang]/[item]/page.tsx:7-31` export no `generateStaticParams`, no `revalidate` and no `dynamicParams`.
- Every other education page here uses `revalidate = 86400`.
- Each URL renders on first request, so crawlers get slower first-byte times.
- Nothing in these files guarantees the catalog is enumerated for the sitemap.
- Combinations the catalog does not list (a locale outside `list.locales`) return 404 from `notFound()` at lines 23 and 18.

```diff
+ export const revalidate = 86400;
+ export const dynamicParams = false;
+ export async function generateStaticParams() {
+   return LIST_ROUTES.map(({ locale, lang, item }) => ({ locale, lang, item })); // derive from lib/seo/wordLists/catalog
+ }
```
Add the equivalent `generateStaticParams` to the `[lang]` hub page.

## 4. Course JSON-LD on game pages is mislabelled and uses the title as the course name
`games-for-teachers/landing.ts:107-114` and `irregular-verbs-games/jsonLd.ts:10-15` emit a `Course` whose `name` is `metaTitle`. That includes "— Go, Went, Gone | LexiClash" and the brand suffix. These pages are tools and games, not courses with instances. Google's Course guidelines expect a real course, and the mismatch risks the markup being ignored or flagged.

**Impact.** There is no rich-result gain, and the `name` wastes the entity signal.

**Fix.** Use a clean name, and prefer `WebApplication` plus `LearningResource` (the `learning` block already feeds this).
```diff
- name,
+ name: name.split(' | ')[0],
```
Better, change `educationCourseJsonLd` to emit `WebApplication` with `applicationCategory: 'EducationalApplication'` and `offers.price: '0'`. Also update the `page.test.tsx:30` test that looks for `Course`.

## 5. Meta descriptions are far too long and will be truncated
- `irregular-verbs-games/copy.en.ts:7` is about 260 characters. The `es`, `he`, `sv`, `ja` and `ru` equivalents are similar.
- `games-for-teachers/content.ts:47` is about 190 characters.
- `games-for-teachers/content.ts:45` is a title of about 70 characters, which clips at roughly 60.

The Google snippet gets cut mid-sentence and the keyword-bearing tail ("free for 3 classes of 50") is lost.
```diff
- metaDescription: 'Irregular verb games for an ESL class: paste go/went/gone, eat/ate/eaten and see/saw/seen, students join with a six-character code, and the board goes on the projector. Support, Core or Challenge per student, six languages, free for 3 classes of 50.',
+ metaDescription: 'Irregular verb games for ESL class: paste go/went/gone lists, students join with a code, board on the projector. Free for 3 classes of 50.',
- metaTitle: 'Free Word Games for Teachers — Zero Prep, Class Analytics | LexiClash',
+ metaTitle: 'Free Word Games for Teachers — Zero Prep | LexiClash',
```

## 6. Thin FAQ questions in es/he weaken FAQPage and AI extraction
`games-for-teachers/content.ts` has full natural-language questions in `en`, `sv`, `ja` and `ru`. The `es` questions are fragments at lines 247, 251, 255, 259, 263 and 267. Examples are "¿Mis listas?", "¿Sigo progreso?" and "¿Maestro sustituto?". The `he` answers at lines 169-189 are terse as well. The `es` answers are short and drop the key claims, such as "hasta 30" at line 233 versus 50 everywhere else.

**Impact.** These fragments rarely match real queries, so they will not be cited.
```diff
- q: '¿Mis listas?',
+ q: '¿Puedo usar mis propias listas de vocabulario?',
- q: '¿Sigo progreso?',
+ q: '¿Cómo hago seguimiento del progreso de los estudiantes?',
- { icon: '👥', text: 'Multijugador — hasta 30; duelos de parejas' },
+ { icon: '👥', text: 'Multijugador para toda la clase (hasta 50); duelos por parejas' },
```

## 7. A competitor link sits on a conversion page
`irregular-verbs-games/copy.en.ts:68-73` (repeated in every locale) links to `https://7esl.com/word-games/` with the label "7ESL word games". It is a followed outbound link to a competitor from the page meant to convert teachers. It also gives them anchor text and a referral.

**Fix.** Remove it. If you keep it for honesty, add `rel="nofollow noopener"` in the renderer for `arcadeNote.href`. I would drop it until you have revenue.

## 8. Machine-translation errors in localized pages hurt hreflang value
- `indoor-recess-games/content.ts:1259`: Russian breadcrumb "Home" is `'Дом'` (house). The breadcrumb feeds `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD.
- `indoor-recess-games/content.ts:433`: Spanish keyword "ideas para recreo en casa".
- Hebrew at lines 369, 285 and 319 has garbled phrases such as 'פאזל יומי ב־one' and "רדימה".
- ES title (line 429) says "días lluviosos" while the answer (line 456) says "recreo cubierto".

**Impact.** Poor on-page language lowers rankings and citations in the non-English markets you are pitching.
```diff
- home: 'Дом',
+ home: 'Главная',
```
The rest need a native-speaker pass.

## 9. Help metadata is generated for locales that 404
`help/page.tsx:21-29` runs `buildHelpMetadata` for any locale, but the page calls `notFound()` for non-help locales (`ru`) at line 34. `help/[slug]/page.tsx:37-38` guards this correctly with `if (!found) return {}`.

**Fix.** Make it consistent.
```diff
  const { locale } = await params;
+ if (!isHelpLocale(locale)) return {};
  const t = helpT(locale);
```
Also confirm that `ru` is not advertised in the hreflang of sibling pages that link to help. The test at `helpPages.test.tsx:46` covers help only.

## 10. Not auditable from this diff: agent reachability, llms.txt, sitemap and robots
Nothing in these files exposes an API, MCP server or CLI, or references llms.txt, `sitemap.ts` or `robots.ts`. Check these before launch:

- **Sitemap:** it should include the `lists/...` URLs, with per-locale `alternates` restricted to locales that exist (help is five locales, the rest six).

Send those files and I will audit them.

# SEO/GEO audit: `education/lists`, `middle-school-word-games` and the miss-gap routes

**Scope:** The files I was given contain no `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, `lib/seo/*` helpers, API routes, MCP server or CLI. I cannot confirm any of those exist or are correct. The only evidence is the `seoMeta.test.ts` assertions (canonical, hreflang cluster and sitemap agreement for list pages), which I take as correct. The `education/lists` section is well built for search: server-rendered words, JSON-LD, reciprocal hreflang, and tests for all of it. The findings below are the gaps in this slice, ordered by impact.

## 1. Agents have no machine-readable way to reach the product or its word lists
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/lists/_views/ListView.tsx:137-156`. The word and definition data exists only as HTML and `listGraph` JSON-LD. No JSON, MCP or CLI surface is visible in this slice.
- **Impact:** This is your best AI-citation asset: a large catalogue of graded word lists with definitions, and an agent can only reach it by scraping. "Make a Grade 5 vocab game" has no agent-callable path, and that is the path to first revenue.
- **Fix:** I could not see `public/llms.txt` or an API route, so these are new files.
  ```diff
  + // fe-next/app/api/lists/[slug]/route.ts  (GET, public, cacheable)
  + import { getCatalog } from '@/lib/seo/wordLists/catalog';
  + export async function GET(_: Request, { params }: { params: Promise<{ slug: string }> }) {
  +   const { slug } = await params;
  +   const l = getCatalog().lists.find((x) => x.slug === slug);
  +   if (!l) return Response.json({ error: 'not_found' }, { status: 404 });
  +   return Response.json({ slug: l.slug, lang: l.lang, grade: l.grade, topic: l.topic, words: l.words });
  + }
  ```
  ```diff
  + # fe-next/public/llms.txt
  + # LexiClash
  + > Free live vocabulary games for classrooms. Teachers paste a word list; students join with a code.
  + - [Word list library](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/lists): graded lists with definitions, 6 locales
  + - [List data (JSON)](https://www.lexiclash.live/api/lists/{slug})
  + - [Create a classroom](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/education/classroom-game)
  ```

## 2. Lists without a grade hub can be orphaned from the hubs
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/education/lists/_views/HubViews.tsx:13,84`.
- **Impact:** Each grade group shows only 3 cards (`PREVIEW_PER_GRADE`). The "view all" link at lines 75-82 exists only when a grade hub `gh` exists. Lists with `grade === null`, or grades with no hub, are capped at 3 links. Every list beyond the third depends on topic hubs or `relatedLists` and the sitemap alone, so the page is weakly linked and crawled late.

  ```diff

  +            <CardGrid lists={gh ? lists.slice(0, PREVIEW_PER_GRADE) : lists} locale={locale} />
  ```

## Shard 34

# SEO/GEO audit: what the provided files support

I only have the education hub, its content files, a few landing pages and three noindex tool routes. I can't see `generatePageMetadata`, `buildEducationLandingMetadata`, the sitemap, robots, `llms.txt` or any API/MCP surface. So I list only issues evidenced in these files, plus gaps I can't verify, flagged as such.

## 1. The spelling-bee page is indexed in 6 locales, but 3 of them carry a wrong or broken claim
**`fe-next/app/[locale]/education/spelling-bee-practice/content.ts:330`**

The Russian FAQ says "до 30 человек присоединяются по коду из 4 цифр". The real join code is 6 characters and a room holds 50 (see `sight-words-practice/content.ts:183`). Because this is a FAQ answer, an AI engine citing the Russian page would repeat a wrong fact. The same copy block feeds `FAQPage` markup, so the error also sits in structured data.

Other localized copy here is machine-translated gibberish that undermines trust and citation quality:
- `:136`: "рекомендуемые для подготовки".
- `:167`: "Två deltare ansikte mot ansikte".
- `:234`: "スペリング暖房".
- `:193`: "Se Utbildningsmöte".

Diff for the verifiable factual error (`:330`):
```diff
-... (до 30 человек присоединяются по коду из 4 цифр, без личных аккаунтов) ...
+... (до 50 человек присоединяются по коду из 6 символов, без личных аккаунтов) ...
```
For the garbled locales, either translate properly or take the same approach as sight-words: serve EN only and noindex the rest.

## 2. The spelling-bee page promises "no signup", but the free tier needs a teacher account
**`spelling-bee-practice/content.ts:53, 61, 84, 91`**

- The meta description says "No signup, no app".
- `:91` says "No account required".
- The hub and sight-words FAQ say the teacher needs an account for classroom use.

This is mostly fine for solo play. The problem is `:90`, which mixes classroom use with "no student accounts" and doesn't say the teacher account is required.

The wider risk is a mismatch that increases bounce and trust loss. Add one clause to the meta description:
```diff
-... No signup, no app — just open the browser ...
+... Solo practice needs no signup; teachers create a free account for classes. No app — just open the browser ...
```

## 3. Spelling-bee page: the HowTo schema is EN-only, but the page is indexed in other locales (and the cited research is unsupported)
**`spelling-bee-practice/jsonLd.ts:13`, `content.ts:89`**

Non-EN locales get no HowTo. That is harmless on its own.

The bigger issue is that `hreflang`/`noindex` handling isn't set for this page. The sight-words landing sets `enOnlyHreflang: true` (`sight-words-practice/landing.ts:73`), but spelling-bee doesn't. Since the non-EN bodies are low-quality translations (see #1), they compete with the EN page and dilute it.

```diff
 // spelling-bee-practice/landing.ts:74
     extraJsonLd: spellingBeeExtraJsonLd(locale),
+    enOnlyHreflang: true,
```
This only works if the copy is genuinely EN-only. If you keep the translations, fix them first.

## 4. Hub: FAQ JSON-LD includes the geoAnswer, but the visible FAQ omits it
**`education/page.tsx:42, 64`**

The schema is built from `[geoAnswer, ...faq]` (`:42`). The visible `LandingFaq` receives only `content.faq` (`:64`). The geoAnswer is rendered elsewhere via `geo={content.geoAnswer}`, so it is probably visible. Please confirm that `EducationPageClient` renders it server-side. If it renders only after hydration, the answer is invisible to crawlers while the markup claims it. I couldn't verify this because `PageClient` isn't in the provided files.

## 5. Hub: all internal links sit inside a closed `<details>`
**`education/page.tsx:105`**

The comment says the links ship in the server HTML for crawlers. That's true, but Google may down-weight content that's hidden by default. There are about 19 links to the money pages. Hiding them also hurts click-through from the hub.

Fix: keep the details open on desktop, or move at least the top two cards (duels, classroom-game) outside the details.
```diff
-      <details data-testid="education-resources" className="group">
+      <details data-testid="education-resources" className="group" open>
```
This trades some mobile screen space for crawl and click reach. Decide with that cost in mind.

## 6. Hub copy markets prices that conflict across surfaces
**`education/seoContent.ts:20` vs `:40`, `:70`**

The EN description gives "Teacher Pro $9/month with a 14-day trial; Schools from $49 per teacher / year". The EN FAQ says "Free to start" and Teacher Pro $9/month, with no trial mentioned. The other locales' descriptions omit prices entirely.

Over 160 characters of the EN description will be truncated in the SERP, so the price information is cut off anyway. Shorten it so the pitch survives:
```diff
-description: 'Free word and vocabulary games for your classroom — no student accounts and no downloads. Bring your own word lists, run whole-class multiplayer in any browser, and teach vocabulary in 6 languages including Hebrew and Japanese. Free for teachers; Teacher Pro $9/month with a 14-day trial; Schools & departments from $49 per teacher / year (5+ teachers, invoice/PO).',
+description: 'Free vocabulary games for your classroom: no student accounts, no downloads, your own word lists, whole-class play in any browser. Free for teachers; Pro $9/mo.',
```
Check whether the 14-day trial is real, and make the other surfaces agree.

## 7. The hub is missing from the shared `Offer` schema
**`education/page.tsx:54-61`**

The sight-words page emits detailed `Offer` nodes (Free / Pro $9 / Schools $49) through `educationProviderNode`. The hub passes `webAppSchema`, but I can't see whether it includes offers. AI engines answering "how much does X cost" lean on this. Confirm `buildEducationWebApplicationJsonLd` carries the same three offers, and reuse the sight-words block if not. ## 8. The noindex tool routes lack a canonical or hreflang strategy
**`team-tiles-unplugged/page.tsx:33-36`, `unplugged-reteach/page.tsx:33-36`**

These are `noindex, follow` with no canonical, and they use `force-dynamic` with query params. This is correct for keeping them out of the index. The only cost is that `follow` lets crawlers wander parameterised URLs. Low impact. If crawl budget matters, use `follow: false` as `unplugged-grade-passback/page.tsx:108` does.
```diff
-    robots: { index: false, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: false, follow: false },
```

**`vocabulary-games-classroom/classGames.ts:29`**

The "10 vocabulary games" section is the right shape for list-style AI citations. The header comment records that it lost four comparisons to a printable listicle.
