## Shard 35

# SEO / GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]/…`)

This covers only the files provided. Sitemap, robots, llms.txt, and API/MCP/CLI files were not in scope, so I make no claims about them. Items are ordered by impact.

## 1. FAQ page emits two competing FAQPage JSON-LD blocks, and the layout block disagrees with the visible content
- **Where:** `faq/layout.tsx:81-84` and `faq/page.tsx:246`
- **Problem:** The layout injects a FAQPage built from translation keys. `page.tsx` injects a second FAQPage from `content.ts`. Google ignores or flags duplicate FAQPage on one URL, and the two have different `@id`s (`#faqpage` vs `#faq`), different question sets, and different answers. LLM extractors see contradictory data.
- **Impact:** FAQ rich-result eligibility is lost, and AI citations pull from inconsistent answers.
- **Diff:** keep the one that matches the visible text (page.tsx, 16 items), and keep the layout's breadcrumb and webpage nodes.
```diff
// faq/layout.tsx
-  const faqSchema = { ... };
-  return JSON.stringify([breadcrumbSchema, faqSchema, webPageSchema]);
+  return JSON.stringify([breadcrumbSchema, webPageSchema]);
```

## 2. Hreflang on `/faq` omits `ru`, uses a mess of region variants, and depends on page-local copy
- **Where:** `faq/page.tsx:73-96`
- **Problem:** Russian is a shipped locale (`ru` is in `validLocale`) but has no `ru` alternate. The `ru/faq` page is therefore not reciprocally linked. The 17 region codes (`en-IL`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, …) all point at the same few URLs and add no signal. Hreflang is also hand-written per page instead of shared.
- **Impact:** The Russian page may be treated as a duplicate or an unlinked alternate, which hurts the ru-RU impressions you are targeting with the Russian landing pages.
- **Diff:**
```diff
         es: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/es/faq',
+        ru: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/ru/faq',
-        'en-IL': ... through 'es-CO': ...   // drop all region variants
```

## 3. Russian landing pages declare hreflang to themselves only, and are noindexed on every other locale path
- **Where:** `erudit-onlayn/page.tsx:38-45`, `filvordy-onlayn/page.tsx:38-45`
- **Problem:** The canonical is hardcoded to `/ru/...`, which is correct. But `/en/erudit-onlayn` etc. render a full page with `index: false`, and the JSON-LD `@id` is fixed to `/ru`. Breadcrumb URLs use `${locale}`, so `/en/erudit-onlayn` emits breadcrumbs pointing to a noindexed URL. Also, `openGraph.images` uses `og-image-en.webp`, an English preview on the Russian pages.
- **Impact:** Minor crawl waste, and weaker social and AI preview cards for the pages most likely to earn ru-RU traffic.
- **Diff:** pin breadcrumb locale and use a ru image.
```diff
-      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 1, name: 'Главная', item: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}` },
-      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 2, name: 'Эрудит онлайн', item: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}${PAGE_PATH}` },
+      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 1, name: 'Главная', item: `${BASE_URL}/ru` },
+      { '@type': 'ListItem', position: 2, name: 'Эрудит онлайн', item: `${BASE_URL}/ru${PAGE_PATH}` },
```
Apply the same change at `filvordy-onlayn/page.tsx:98-99`. For non-ru locales, return 404 or `notFound()` instead of rendering a noindexed duplicate.

## 4. FAQ answers: stale or wrong language lists in sv/ja/es JSON-LD and visible text
- **Where:** `faq/content.ts:260`, `:290`, `:362`, `:392`, `:464`, `:494`
- **Problem:** The sv, ja, and es answers list only "Hebrew, English, Swedish, Japanese", while en, he, and ru list six languages. This contradicts the product and the English answer. These strings feed the page.tsx FAQPage JSON-LD, and AI engines quote them.
- **Impact:** AI answers about language support will be wrong for non-English queries, and Spanish-speaking players are told Spanish is unsupported.
- **Diff (sv example, same pattern for ja and es):**
```diff
-... realtid på hebreiska, engelska, svenska och japanska.
+... realtid på hebreiska, engelska, svenska, japanska, spanska och ryska.
```
```diff
-Ja! LexiClash stöder hebreiska, engelska, svenska och japanska.
+Ja! LexiClash stöder hebreiska, engelska, svenska, japanska, spanska och ryska.
```

## 5. FAQ accordion content: the "SEO" comment overstates what the page does
- **Where:** `faq/PageClient.tsx:114-120`
- **Problem:** Answers are in the DOM, which is good, but they sit in `max-h-0 opacity-0` containers. `GamePageSeoContent` also renders a second, shorter FAQ list on the same page with different wording. Google may discount hidden text, and the visible copy diverges from the schema.
- **Impact:** Moderate. Use the native `<details>` pattern that the Russian pages already use, and drop the duplicate short FAQ.
- **Diff:** in `faq/page.tsx:251-256`, remove the `faq={content.faq}` prop (or the whole `GamePageSeoContent` FAQ section) so there is one canonical FAQ.

## 6. `vocabulary-games-classroom` HowTo is English-only and misdescribes the flow
- **Where:** `vocabulary-games-classroom/jsonLd.ts:17-31`
- **Problem:** The HowTo says students "log in with their free LexiClash account". The page's own copy (`content.ts:236-241`, `:171`) sells a no-account class-code path. The HowTo also does not appear in page text (rich results require visible matching content), and Google has retired HowTo rich results. FAQ on this page says "Yes, students need to sign up", which contradicts the "no account" hero.
- **Impact:** Mixed messages cost conversion, and AI summaries may quote the wrong requirement.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-      { '@type': 'HowToStep', position: 2, name: 'Students join', text: 'Students log in with their free LexiClash account on any device. ...' },
+      { '@type': 'HowToStep', position: 2, name: 'Students join', text: 'Students open the class game, enter the class code and a display name. No email or password needed.' },
```
Also fix `content.ts:171` (and the translations at `:345` etc.) to say accounts are optional.

## 7. Education page claims contradict each other (poisons citations)
- **Where:** `vocabulary-games-classroom/content.ts:197` vs `:893` vs `:134`; `:217`
- **Problem:** The live-room cap is "whole class" in en, "50" in the depth sections, and "最大30人" in ja (`:893`). The `useCases` ESL text lists "EN, HE, ES, SV, JA" and omits RU, while the page claims six languages. The hero H1 says "Free accounts" while the page says "Free to start", and the paid Teacher Pro is $9/month. Comparison tables name Quizlet, Wordwall, and Kahoot with unsourced ✗/✓ claims.
- **Impact:** LLMs prefer pages with consistent facts. Unsourced competitor claims are also a legal and trust risk.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-  { icon: '👥', text: 'ライブマルチプレイヤー — 1セッション最大30人' },
+  { icon: '👥', text: 'ライブマルチプレイヤー — 1ルーム最大50人' },
```
```diff
-desc: 'Play in students\' target language — supports EN, HE, ES, SV, JA dictionaries.'
+desc: 'Play in students\' target language — supports EN, HE, ES, SV, JA, RU dictionaries.'
```

## 8. Primary CTA sends teachers to `/daily/word-hunt`, not the no-account class path the page promises

```diff
+      primaryCta: { label: c.ctaPrimaryButtonLabel, sublabel: c.ctaSubLabel, href: '/education/classroom-game' },
```

and `landing.ts:103` likewise.

## 9. Embed widget: backlink is hidden behind a noindex iframe and the widget has no attribution fallback
- **Where:** `embed/word-of-the-day/route.ts:59`, `:77`, `embedSnippet.ts:9-16`
- **Problem:** Backlinks from iframes pass little to nothing to the host page, so the "for backlinks" goal in `embedSnippet.ts:2` is largely unmet. Also, the embed route's `VALID_LOCALES` includes `ru`, but `embedSnippet.test.ts` and the `route.test.ts` assertion `locales.length).toBe(5)` only cover five, so ru is untested.

```diff
 ></iframe>`;
+  const withLink = `${iframeSnippet}\n<p style="font-size:12px"><a href="${origin}/${locale}/word-of-the-day">Word of the Day by LexiClash</a></p>`;
```

Update the tests, which assert the string ends with `</iframe>` (`embedSnippet.test.ts:65`).

## 10. Agent reachability: nothing in this slice exposes an API, MCP server, or structured product description
- **Where:** `faq/layout.tsx:57-60`, plus the absence of any machine-readable entry point in the files shown
- **Problem:** `speakable` targets `[data-speakable="true"]`, but nothing in `faq/PageClient.tsx` sets that attribute, so the selector only matches `h1`/`h2`, which are headings, not answers. Beyond that, an agent has no documented way to start a game, fetch the daily puzzle, or read the word of the day other than scraping HTML or the embed route (which is noindex). Also, `error.tsx` sets no `noindex` or status context for crawlers, and its fallback text is only in client state.
- **Impact:** AI assistants cannot recommend or launch the product programmatically, and speakable markup is effectively dead.
- **Diff:** make speakable meaningful.
```diff
// faq/PageClient.tsx:125
-                          {item.answer}
+                          <span data-speakable="true">{item.answer}</span>
```
Then verify (outside this slice) that `/llms.txt`, `robots.txt`, and the sitemap list `/{locale}/faq`, the education pages, and a JSON endpoint for the daily word, so an agent can reach the product. ## Shard 36

# SEO/GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]`)

The files provided are the showcase page, glossary and guides, plus two private pages. I did not see `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, `generatePageMetadata`, or any API/MCP/CLI code, so I can't audit those. Findings are limited to what the code shows.

## 1. Guide content is fully localized, but its internal links and hreflang point at other locales
`fe-next/app/[locale]/guides/PageClient.tsx:53-56, 90-93, 127-130, 164-167, 201-204`

Every locale's "Further Reading" links to `/en/blog/...` and `/en/best-online-word-games`. On non-English pages this sends Hebrew, Swedish, Japanese and Spanish users, and the link equity, to English URLs with non-English anchor text. In addition, `guides/blast-strategy/page.tsx:51-75` and `glossary/page.tsx:42-55` declare hreflang for `sv/ja/es` pages whose body is the English glossary (`content.ts:103,108,113`).

Impact: Google can treat the `sv/ja/es` glossaries as duplicates of `en`, so they don't rank for localized queries. Mismatched anchor and target language weakens the topical signal.

Fix: either translate the glossary, or drop the untranslated locales from hreflang and canonicalize them to `/en`.
```diff
- sv: { title: 'LexiClash Ordlista…', terms: enTerms.map(t => ({ ...t })) },
+ // remove sv/ja/es until translated; contentByLocale[locale] || en already falls back
```
```diff
+ // glossary/page.tsx generateMetadata
+ const translated = ['en','he'].includes(locale);
+ canonical: `${SITE_URL}/${translated ? locale : 'en'}/glossary`,
+ robots: { index: translated, follow: true },
```
Keep `languages` limited to `en` and `he`.

## 2. Glossary has duplicate H1s and hidden duplicate content
`glossary/page.tsx:97-112` and `glossary/PageClient.tsx:57`

The client component renders a visible `<h1>` and the full term list. The page then adds a second `sr-only` `<h1>` and a second copy of every term. Two H1s and a hidden duplicate of visible text is cloaking-adjacent. It is unnecessary, because the client component is already SSR'd by Next.

Impact: a possible quality-guideline flag and diluted headings.

Fix: delete the block.
```diff
-      {/* Server-rendered glossary terms for crawlers … */}
-      <section className="sr-only"> … </section>
```

## 3. Showcase H1 is a visually hidden stub and the visible hero has no H1
`free-multiplayer-word-game/Showcase3DClient.tsx:258-272, 379`

The only H1 is `srOnly`. Its title is "A world of words — LexiClash", with no mention of "multiplayer word game". The visible captions are `h2` elements that sit inside a scrubbed, opacity-0 pinned canvas. The target keyword appears only in the URL.

Impact: weak relevance for this page's target query, "free multiplayer word game".

Fix:
```diff
-        title={`${t('showcase3d.heroBadge', 'A world of words')} — LexiClash`}
+        title={t('showcase3d.h1', 'LexiClash — Free Multiplayer Word Game')}
```
Add a `showcase3d.h1` key to each locale, and make sure `seo.showcase3d` title and description use the same phrase.

## 4. Hardcoded English in JSON-LD and breadcrumbs on localized pages
`glossary/page.tsx:84-85` and `guides/blast-strategy/page.tsx:118-119`

Breadcrumb names ("Home", "Glossary", "Guides") are English on he/sv/ja/es pages. The visible UI is hardcoded in English too (`glossary/PageClient.tsx:52,135`, `blast-strategy/PageClient.tsx:90,106,170`).

Impact: localized rich results and AI extraction see mixed-language signals.

Fix: route these strings through the translation dictionary, for example `name: tr.home`.

## 5. HowTo schema is mis-typed, and the guide has no Article/author schema
`blast-strategy/page.tsx:86-100`

The page is an opinion essay, not a procedure. `HowToStep.text` is `substring(0,200)`, which cuts mid-sentence. Google no longer shows HowTo rich results, so this schema earns nothing. The visible byline "Ohad Fisher" is hardcoded (`PageClient.tsx:72`), but `authorName` in `content.ts:34` is "חנון המילים" for `he`, so the on-page and data authors disagree.

Fix: replace HowTo with `Article`.
```diff
-    '@type': 'HowTo',
+    '@type': 'Article',
+    headline: content.title,
+    author: { '@type': 'Person', name: 'Ohad Fisher' },
+    dateModified: DATE_PUBLISHED,
-    step: …
```
Do the same for `classic-strategy`. Also make the author consistent across locales.

## 6. Hreflang cluster claims 24 region variants that don't exist as pages
`free-multiplayer-word-game/page.tsx:18-45`, `glossary/page.tsx:42-55`, `blast-strategy/page.tsx:51-75`

Variants like `en-JP`, `en-SE` and `es-AR` all map to the same URL as their base language. This is noise and an easy source of reciprocity errors. For example, the Russian entry on the showcase page is `ru`, but there is no `ru` landing in the cluster. The block is also copy-pasted across three files.

Fix: centralize it in one helper and keep only `x-default` plus the real languages.
```diff
-        'en-IL': …, 'he-IL': …, 'en-US': …, …
```

## 7. Private and no-value routes need explicit noindex
`friends/page.tsx:9`, `friend-challenge/[id]/page.tsx`

`friend-challenge/[id]` has no `generateMetadata`, so it has no robots directive. It redirects anonymous users with a login redirect, and per-user URLs shouldn't be in the index. `friends` relies on `generatePageMetadata`, whose robots setting I couldn't verify.

Fix:
```diff
+export const metadata = { robots: { index: false, follow: false } };
```
Add this to `friend-challenge/[id]/page.tsx`. Confirm that `seoKey: 'friends'` sets noindex.

## 8. Guides index and classic guide metadata not visible
The guides index `page.tsx` and `classic-strategy/page.tsx` weren't provided, and the `PageClient.tsx` for classic was cut off. Check that they carry `canonical`, hreflang, FAQPage and BreadcrumbJsonLd the way `blast-strategy` does. The index FAQ (`guides/PageClient.tsx:34-40`) is rendered, but I saw no FaqJsonLd for it.

## 9. Scroll-pinned hero hides content from users and agents
`Showcase3DClient.tsx:372-382`

All four captions are `absolute inset-0` and start hidden via GSAP (`autoAlpha`). Without JS, or in a render-light fetch, they overlap or don't appear. The FAQ and mode copy below are fine. The risk is that the main value proposition appears only after scroll.

Fix: add the one-sentence proposition (item 3) as a visible `<p>` under the logo at line 324-326.

## 10. Agent reachability: nothing found
In the supplied files I found no `llms.txt`, MCP, or public API. The only API reference is the private `/api/growth/async-challenge` (`FriendChallengeLandingClient.tsx:59`).

Impact: AI assistants can cite the guides and glossary but can't discover or start a game.

Fix: add `public/llms.txt` listing the showcase, `/en/guides`, `/en/glossary`, the tool pages, and the play URL, with one line on the product. This is a low-cost win; build an MCP server only after the product has paying users.

**Priority for revenue:** fix 3, 1 and 2 first (they affect the pages that can rank). Then 7 and 5, then 6.

## Shard 37

# SEO/GEO audit: LexiClash guides, Hebrew daily page, classroom page

Scope: only the files shown. I did not see `robots`, `sitemap`, `llms.txt`, the root layout, `/api`, or any MCP/CLI code, so I can't judge those. Items 9 and 10 say what to check.

## 1. Both guide pages use `force-dynamic`, so every crawl is an uncached server render
`fe-next/app/[locale]/guides/classic-strategy/page.tsx:7` and `fe-next/app/[locale]/guides/word-hunt-strategy/page.tsx:7`

- **Impact:**
  - The content is a static constant, yet each request renders on the server with no cache headers.
  - That raises TTFB for Googlebot and AI fetchers, which lowers crawl budget and risks timeouts.
  - The guides index (`guides/page.tsx:7`) already uses `revalidate = 86400`, so the pages are inconsistent.
  - These are the pages most likely to earn citations.
- **Diff (same change in both files):**
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
+export function generateStaticParams() {
+  return ['en','he','sv','ja','es','ru'].map((locale) => ({ locale }));
+}
```

## 2. hreflang lists `ru` and many region variants, but the guides have no Russian content
`classic-strategy/page.tsx:60,77`, `word-hunt-strategy/page.tsx:56,73`, `guides/page.tsx:132,141`; content is in `classic-strategy/content.ts:26` and `word-hunt-strategy/content.ts:16`.

- **Impact:**
  - `contentByLocale` has en, he, sv, ja and es only. `/ru/guides/...` falls back to English text under a `ru` hreflang and a self-canonical.
  - Google treats this as duplicate content with a wrong language signal.
  - The region variants (`en-SE`, `en-JP`, `es-AR`, and so on) all point at the same URLs, which adds bloat without benefit.
  - A locale that falls back should canonicalize to `/en/`, or be dropped from hreflang.
- **Diff (both guide pages):**
```diff
+const HAS_CONTENT = ['en','he','sv','ja','es'];
 ...
-    alternates: {
-      canonical: `${SITE_URL}/${locale}/guides/${SLUG}`,
+    alternates: {
+      canonical: `${SITE_URL}/${HAS_CONTENT.includes(locale) ? locale : 'en'}/guides/${SLUG}`,
       languages: {
...
-        ru: `${SITE_URL}/ru/guides/${SLUG}`,
...
-        'ru-RU': `${SITE_URL}/ru/guides/${SLUG}`,
```
Also delete the `en-XX` rows that duplicate `en`. Keep `en`, `he`, `sv`, `ja`, `es` and `x-default`.

## 3. Hebrew classroom FAQ says Hebrew is supported "for the fallback locale", and the page content is likely client-only; verify the render
`fe-next/app/[locale]/hebrew-classroom-vocabulary-games/content.ts:38,387`

- **Impact:**
  - `getHebrewClassroomContent` falls back to English for any unknown locale. The URL is Hebrew-specific, yet the `en` copy (line 43) targets generic "classroom word games". This is a different page intent from the URL slug.
  - The `/en/` variant of a "hebrew-…" URL competes with and confuses the `he` page.
  - The `page.tsx` for this route wasn't provided, so I can't confirm canonical, FAQPage JSON-LD or `robots` there.
- **Action:** apply the same locale-gate pattern already used in `hamila-hayomit/page.tsx:57`, and confirm FAQ JSON-LD is emitted from `faqs`.

## 4. HowTo schema is wrong for these articles and truncates mid-sentence
`classic-strategy/page.tsx:89-103`, `word-hunt-strategy/page.tsx:84-93`

- **Impact:**
  - Google retired HowTo rich results, so this gives no SERP gain.
  - `text: s.content.substring(0, 200)` cuts steps mid-word, and an LLM parsing the schema sees broken text.
  - The pages lack an `Article` entity with an author, `dateModified` and a publisher. Those are the signals that help AI citation (E-E-A-T).
- **Diff (both pages):**
```diff
-  const howToSchema = {
-    '@context': 'https://schema.org',
-    '@type': 'HowTo',
-    name: content.title,
-    description: content.subtitle,
-    url: ...,
-    datePublished: DATE_PUBLISHED,
-    inLanguage: locale,
-    step: ...
-  };
+  const articleSchema = {
+    '@context': 'https://schema.org',
+    '@type': 'Article',
+    headline: content.title,
+    description: content.subtitle,
+    mainEntityOfPage: `${SITE_URL}/${locale}/guides/${SLUG}`,
+    datePublished: DATE_PUBLISHED,
+    dateModified: DATE_PUBLISHED,
+    inLanguage: locale,
+    author: { '@type': 'Person', name: 'Ohad Fisher' },
+    publisher: { '@type': 'Organization', name: 'LexiClash', url: SITE_URL },
+  };
```
Then render `articleSchema` in place of `howToSchema` in the script tag.

## 5. The byline is hard-coded to "Ohad Fisher" and ignores the per-locale author
`word-hunt-strategy/PageClient.tsx:72`

- **Impact:**
  - The content defines `authorName` per locale (`content.ts:22,146,270`), for example "חנון המילים" in Hebrew.
  - The page ignores it, so the visible author conflicts with the structured author. The inconsistency weakens author-entity trust.
  - The Classic guide has the same pattern in its content.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-              Ohad Fisher
+              {content.authorName}
```
Better: use one real name across all locales, since `authorName` is a pseudonym in four of them. That is the stronger E-E-A-T signal.

## 6. English title for Word Hunt is under-optimised and has no brand suffix
`word-hunt-strategy/page.tsx:18`

- **Impact:**
  - It is the only locale without `| LexiClash`.
  - The core "Wordle-like" intent isn't in the title, and the page body leans on it ("If you've played Wordle…").
  - The `en` description (line 26) is also thin.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-  en: 'Word Hunt Strategy: Find Hidden Words Faster',
+  en: 'Word Hunt Strategy: Best Starting Words & Clue Tips (Wordle-Style) | LexiClash',
```

## 7. Mojibake and typos in indexable copy (missing diacritics, English fragments in other locales)
`classic-strategy/content.ts:278,301,303,311,337,345,359` (Swedish "här" written as "här"/"here" artefacts: "Jag här hittat", "De här färre kopplingar"); `word-hunt-strategy/content.ts:296-298,318,332`

- **Impact:**
  - Swedish text is broken ("Jag här hittat" should be "Jag har hittat").
  - "sex språk … ryska, ryska" is duplicated at `hebrew-classroom…/content.ts:191`.
  - The Spanish title in `classic-strategy/page.tsx:20` drops accents.
  - These look machine-mangled and lower quality signals for Swedish, Spanish and Japanese. The FAQ answers in these locales are also what gets quoted.
- **Diff (examples):**
```diff
-Jag här hittat några av mina bästa ord
+Jag har hittat några av mina bästa ord
-ryska, ryska.
+ryska.
```
Run a native proofread on the sv and es strings. Fix the metadata titles too (`Strategiguide för Klassiskt Läge`, `Guía de Estrategia Modo Clásico`).

## 8. `ItemList` on the guides index hard-codes `numberOfItems: 3` and English names in every locale
`guides/page.tsx:173-185`

- **Impact:**
  - Item names are English even on `/he/` and `/ja/`.
  - The `blast-strategy` URL is declared here, but I cannot confirm it exists. A 404 in structured data is a trust and crawl-waste issue.
  - The JSON-LD also uses two different injection styles (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` and `encodeJsonLd`, line 191). Use the safe `encodeJsonLd` for all three.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-      <script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(breadcrumbSchema) }} />
+      <script type="application/ld+json">{encodeJsonLd(breadcrumbSchema)}</script>
...
-      <script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(itemListSchema) }} />
+      <script type="application/ld+json">{encodeJsonLd(itemListSchema)}</script>
```
Also confirm `/guides/blast-strategy` returns 200.

## 9. The Hebrew daily page's `x-default` points at the Hebrew `/he/daily` and offers no English alternate
`fe-next/app/[locale]/hamila-hayomit/page.tsx:50-54`, `seo.ts:7`

- **Impact:**
  - `x-default` is `DAILY_URL` (`/he/daily`), which is a different URL from the page that declares it. Google expects `x-default` to be one of the alternates of the same page set.
  - The page falls outside the group, so Google may ignore the hreflang set.
  - Non-`he` locales are `noindex` but canonical to the Hebrew page. That mixed signal (noindex plus canonical) can drop the canonical target in some cases.
- **Diff:**
```diff
       languages: {
-        'x-default': DAILY_URL,
+        'x-default': CANONICAL,
         he: CANONICAL,
         'he-IL': CANONICAL,
       },
```
For non-he locales, prefer `notFound()` over `noindex` plus a cross-locale canonical.

## 10. Agent reachability and discovery are not visible in this slice (check these first)
No evidence in the supplied files of `llms.txt`, `robots.txt` rules for AI crawlers, or an API/MCP surface. The only machine-readable agent signal here is `data-speakable="true"` (`word-hunt-strategy/PageClient.tsx:83,128`), which needs a matching `SpeakableSpecification` in the JSON-LD to take effect.

- **Impact:**
  - Without `llms.txt` and an agent entry point, ChatGPT and Perplexity can cite the guides but cannot send users into the product.
  - Revenue relevance: the guides point users to `/singleplayer` and `/daily`, but nothing lets an agent start a daily game or fetch the day's puzzle metadata.
- **Cheapest fix (add `fe-next/public/llms.txt`):**
```diff
+# LexiClash
+> Free multiplayer word-grid game (Classic, Blast, Word Hunt, Word Wheel). Six languages. No signup.
+## Play
+- [Daily challenge](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/daily)
+- [Classroom word games (teachers)](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/hebrew-classroom-vocabulary-games)
+## Guides
+- [Classic strategy](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/guides/classic-strategy)
+- [Word Hunt strategy](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/guides/word-hunt-strategy)
```
Then check that `robots.txt` doesn't block GPTBot, ClaudeBot or PerplexityBot, and that the sitemap lists every `/{locale}/guides/*` URL. After that, consider a read-only "today's puzzle" JSON endpoint as a first MCP or API step.

## Priority order for a solo founder
1. Items 1 and 2: roughly 15 minutes of edits that fix crawl and duplicate-content problems on every guide.
2. Item 10: add `llms.txt` and check `robots.txt`.
3. Items 4 and 5: add the `Article` schema and the real byline.
4. Item 7: proofread the Swedish and Spanish copy.
5. Items 6, 8 and 9: cleanup.

# SEO / GEO audit: lexiclash.live (files provided)

I only saw the page files above. I did not see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, the layout, or any API/MCP code, so I make no claims about them. The first issue below is what I would check first.

## 1. `/how-to-play` is `force-dynamic` and emits duplicate JSON-LD
`fe-next/app/[locale]/how-to-play/page.tsx:11, 196-198`, `PageClient.tsx:143-146`

- `force-dynamic` removes caching and static HTML for a pure content page. Slow TTFB hurts crawl and citation fetches.
- `BreadcrumbJsonLd` is emitted twice, once in `page.tsx` and again in `PageClient.tsx` with different names ("LexiClash" vs "Home").
- `FAQPage` is emitted twice, from `FaqPageJsonLd` and from `GameModeJsonLd includeFaq`. The two FAQs have different content. Google flags duplicate FAQPage and breadcrumb items, which can cost the rich result.
- Fix: make the page static, then drop the duplicates.

```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
```
```diff
 // PageClient.tsx
-            <BreadcrumbJsonLd items={breadcrumbs} />
-            <GameModeJsonLd mode="classic" locale={locale} includeFaq />
+            <GameModeJsonLd mode="classic" locale={locale} />
```
If the removed `GameModeJsonLd includeFaq` is the better FAQ, keep that one instead and delete `FaqPageJsonLd`.

## 2. `/how-to-play` structured data and visible content disagree
`page.tsx:84-184, 188`, `content.ts:103-113`

- The JSON-LD FAQ in `page.tsx` gives scoring as 4 letters = 2 points and 5 letters = 4 points. The visible page (`content.ts:92-93`) says 3 and 5. AI engines see contradictory facts.
- The `ru` locale is missing from `howToPlaySeoContent`, and line 188 omits `'ru'`. Russian falls back to English content under a Russian URL.
- Fix: derive the FAQ from `getHowToPlayContent`, which is the single source of truth.

```diff
-    const content = howToPlaySeoContent[validLocale] ?? howToPlaySeoContent.en;
+    const c = getHowToPlayContent(validLocale);
+    const content = { ...(howToPlaySeoContent[validLocale] ?? howToPlaySeoContent.en), faq: c.faq.items };
```
Also fix line 188 to `['en','he','sv','ja','es','ru']`.

## 3. Hebrew classroom page canonicalizes every locale to `/he`, and `noindex`es the rest
`hebrew-classroom-vocabulary-games/page.tsx:45, 59`

- Canonical is hardcoded to `/he` for all locales, while hreflang lists en/sv/ja/es/ru as alternates. The `noindex` on non-`he` pages contradicts the hreflang cluster, because hreflang pointing at noindexed pages is ignored.
- Fix: list only the `he` page, with a self-canonical.

```diff
-      canonical: `${BASE_URL}/he${PAGE_PATH}`,
-      languages: { ...all... },
+      canonical: `${BASE_URL}/he${PAGE_PATH}`,
+      languages: { he: `${BASE_URL}/he${PAGE_PATH}`, 'x-default': `${BASE_URL}/he${PAGE_PATH}` },
```
Better still, call `notFound()` for non-he locales. Related: `x-default` points to `/en${PAGE_PATH}`, which is noindexed.

## 4. Multiplayer landing pages have hreflang and canonical conflicts
`hebrew-multiplayer-word-game/page.tsx:11-13, 41-65`, `japanese-word-game/page.tsx:15-16, 44-69`

- `canonical` is hardcoded to `/he/...` or `/ja/...` regardless of `locale`. The `[locale]` segment lets `/en/hebrew-multiplayer-word-game` render with `index: true` and a canonical pointing elsewhere. That is a duplicate-content leak.
- `ru` is missing from the hreflang map even though Russian is a shipped locale.
- Roughly 20 region variants (`en-SE`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `es-AR`…) mapped to the same URLs add noise and no signal. Collapse them to language-level entries plus `x-default`.
- Fix: use `robots: { index: locale === 'he' }`, as `japanese-word-game` computes `isTargetLocale` but never uses it.

```diff
-    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: isTargetLocale, follow: true },
```
`japanese-word-game/page.tsx:70`. Apply the same change in the Hebrew page, which also needs `const { locale } = await params`.

`japanese-word-game/page.tsx:143`, `hebrew-multiplayer-word-game/page.tsx:17`, `how-to-play/content.ts:105`

- The JA page says "6 languages (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES)", which lists five.
- Player counts conflict across pages: 50 on the Hebrew page, "20+" in the how-to-play FAQ, and "2–50" on the ES page.
- The Hebrew meta description says "10,000+ words", while the ES page uses a measured `ES_DICT_FLOOR`.
- LLMs cite whichever number they retrieve, and inconsistency lowers trust. Centralize these as shared constants.

## 6. Landing-page JSON-LD is only FAQ, with no `VideoGame` or `WebApplication` entity
`hebrew-multiplayer-word-game/page.tsx:99-115`, `japanese-word-game/page.tsx:102-118`

- These pages have no `BreadcrumbList` and no `@id` or `inLanguage` on the FAQ.
- Only the RU association page has a `VideoGame` entity (`igra-v-assotsiatsii-onlayn/page.tsx:172-196`).
- Add a product entity so engines can tie "free, no signup" to a named game.

```diff
+ {'@context':'https://schema.org','@type':'VideoGame','name':'LexiClash','url':`${BASE_URL}/${locale}`,
+  'inLanguage':'he','applicationCategory':'GameApplication','operatingSystem':'Web',
+  'offers':{'@type':'Offer','price':'0','priceCurrency':'USD'}}
```

## 7. The RU association page's JSON-LD points at a different URL, and its hreflang is incomplete
`igra-v-assotsiatsii-onlayn/page.tsx:48-55, 172-196`, `igry-v-slova-onlayn/page.tsx:38-45`

- The `VideoGame` `url` is `/ru/connections`, not the page itself.
- `priceCurrency: 'RUB'` with `price: 0` is harmless but odd.
- `x-default` points to `/en/connections` for one page and `/en` for the other, which is inconsistent.
- The `igry-v-slova-onlayn` meta says "6 modes" while the RU page elsewhere describes "8 modes" on the ES page. Reconcile.
- Fix: add `mainEntityOfPage` pointing at the page URL.

## 8. The ES landing page hides FAQ content behind client-only state
`juego-de-palabras-multijugador/components/FaqAccordion.tsx:9-10, 49-65`

- The accordion is a client component, and only item 0 is open. `AnimatePresence` unmounts closed answers, so the HTML a crawler or agent receives contains only the first answer.
- The other pages use native `<details>`, which keeps all answers in the DOM.
- Fix: render all answers and hide them with CSS, or use `<details>`.

```diff
-              <AnimatePresence initial={false}>
-                {isOpen && (
-                  <m.div ...>
+              <div id={`faq-panel-${i}`} role="region" hidden={!isOpen} className="overflow-hidden">
```
`hidden` content is still in the HTML, whereas unmounted content is not. Note that `FeatureGrid`, `ModesShowcase`, and `StepsSection` use `initial={{opacity:0}}`. That is fine for SSR text, but verify the ES page's own `FAQPage` JSON-LD exists, since I could not see its `page.tsx`.

## 9. Join pages are correctly `noindex`, but `/join` has no `canonical` or `alternates`
`join/page.tsx:21`, `join/[code]/page.tsx:82`

- This is low impact, because `noindex` is right for these pages.
- However `generateMetadata` in `join/[code]` interpolates the raw `code` into `title` and `og:url`. Arbitrary strings become page titles, which is a spam and injection surface in unfurls.
- Fix: sanitize the code.

```diff
-  const { locale, code } = await params;
+  const { locale, code: rawCode } = await params;
+  const code = rawCode.replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 8).toUpperCase();
```

The page also carries a title and an ItemList with a single "Top Player" placeholder (finding 3), but its visible content is nearly empty.

```diff
+  if (locale !== 'es') notFound();
```

# SEO/GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]/...`)

Scope: only the files shown (legal pages, three comparison pages, two tests). I cannot see `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, the root layout, `generatePageMetadata`, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. Findings below cover what is visible, plus what I could not verify, so you can check the rest yourself.

## 1. Spanish Wordle page's hreflang is not reciprocal, and it is missing from the `he`/`en` clusters
`lexiclash-contra-wordle/page.tsx:43-51`: the page declares `en`, `es`, and `he` alternates, but it omits the `es-*` region variants that the Hebrew sibling (`lexiclash-neged-wordle/page.tsx:54-62`) lists. Google ignores hreflang unless the targets point back, so any missing return link drops the cluster.
- **Impact:** the `es` page is the one with the real keyword target ("alternativa a Wordle"), so mis-clustering costs it impressions.
- **Diff** (`lexiclash-contra-wordle/page.tsx:46-50`):
```diff
         'x-default': `${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`,
         en: `${BASE_URL}/en/lexiclash-vs-wordle`,
         es: `${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`,
         he: `${BASE_URL}/he/lexiclash-neged-wordle`,
+        'es-MX': `${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`,
+        'es-ES': `${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`,
+        'es-US': `${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`,
```
- Also verify that `/en/lexiclash-vs-wordle` lists these pages back.

## 2. Locale-parameterised routes serve one language's content under every locale, with a canonical that ignores `locale`
`lexiclash-contra-wordle/page.tsx:16` builds its canonical from `locale`, while `lexiclash-neged-wordle/page.tsx:16` hard-codes `/he/`. `/en/lexiclash-neged-wordle`, `/ja/lexiclash-neged-wordle` and the like still render Hebrew with `dir="rtl"`, and only `lexiclash-vs-apalabrados` sets `robots: { index: isSpanish }` (line 48).
- **Impact:** in the Wordle pages, `isTargetLocale` is computed (`contra:15`, `neged:15,71`) but never used, so every `/{locale}/` variant is indexable. That produces duplicate content and a canonical that, on the contra page, self-points to the wrong-language URL for non-es locales.
- **Diff** (`lexiclash-contra-wordle/page.tsx:52`, and the same pattern at `lexiclash-neged-wordle/page.tsx:65`):
```diff
-    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: isTargetLocale, follow: true },
```
and `const pageUrl = \`${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle\`;` at line 16 of the contra page. Better still, call `notFound()` for non-target locales.

## 3. Legal pages render entirely client-side with `force-dynamic`
`legal/privacy/page.tsx:6`, `terms/page.tsx:6`, and `refund/page.tsx:4` all set `force-dynamic`. The body is rendered by `'use client'` components that call `useParams()` (`refund/PageClient.tsx:1,11-13`), even though the comment says the content is "server-renderable for SEO".
- **Impact:** these pages are crawlable via SSR of client components, but they are uncacheable and slow. They are also low-value for search, since a privacy policy attracts no impressions.
- **Diff** (all three `page.tsx` files):
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
```
and pass `locale` as a prop into the client component instead of `useParams`.

## 4. Duplicate and conflicting metadata and JSON-LD on the legal pages
`privacy/layout.tsx:11-80` and `privacy/page.tsx:8-11` both define `generateMetadata`. The page-level one wins, so the layout's hreflang map (23 entries including odd ones like `en-JP`, `en-SE`, `en-MX`) is dead code. Both also emit a BreadcrumbList: the layout at line 93 and `BreadcrumbJsonLd` at `page.tsx:24`, so two competing BreadcrumbLists appear per page. The layout's list links to `/legal`, which may not exist.
- **Impact:** duplicate structured data triggers validation warnings, and the layout's breadcrumb can point at a 404.
- **Diff:** delete `generateMetadata` and the `<script>` block from `privacy/layout.tsx` (lines 11-80, 92-123) and the same in `terms/layout.tsx`.

## 5. `inLanguage`-less FAQPage JSON-LD and no Organization, WebSite or VideoGame schema in the files shown
Only FAQPage appears (`contra:81`, `neged:115`, `apalabrados:87`). The visible pages emit no `Organization`, `WebApplication`/`Game`, or `Offer` (the Pro subscription).
- **Impact:** AI engines use entity markup to cite who and what a product is, and Google restricts FAQ rich results to authoritative government and health sites, so FAQPage on these pages mainly serves LLM extraction.
- **Diff** (root layout, which I cannot see):
```diff
+{ '@context':'https://schema.org','@type':'WebApplication','name':'LexiClash','url':'https://www.lexiclash.live','applicationCategory':'GameApplication','operatingSystem':'Web','offers':{'@type':'Offer','price':'0','priceCurrency':'USD'} }
```
Also add `inLanguage: 'es'` and `'he'` to the contra and neged FAQs (the apalabrados page has it at line 90).

## 6. Apalabrados page emits JSON-LD without `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`
`lexiclash-vs-apalabrados/page.tsx:104`: `<script type="application/ld+json">{faqJsonLd}</script>`. React escapes JSX text children (quotes become `&quot;`), so the JSON is often unparsable.
- **Impact:** the structured data on this page is likely invalid, and it is your highest-intent comparison page.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-<script type="application/ld+json">{faqJsonLd}</script>
+<script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: faqJsonLd }} />
```

## 7. Apalabrados page is `force-dynamic` and has a wrong-language OG image
`lexiclash-vs-apalabrados/page.tsx:5` uses `force-dynamic`, while the Wordle pages use `revalidate = 86400`. The page is static content, so it hurts TTFB and crawl efficiency. Line 28 uses `og-image-en.webp` for a Spanish page, whereas `og-image-es.webp` is used elsewhere.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
...
-og-image-en.webp
+og-image-es.webp
```
(lines 28 and 34).

## 8. Factual claims on comparison pages that an AI engine will quote, and that contradict each other
`contra:160` says "5 (ES, EN, HE, SV, JA)", while `contra:64` lists six languages including Russian, and `privacy/content.ts:26` ships `ru`. `apalabrados:154` repeats the five-language list. `contra:156` says "sin anuncios molestos", and `apalabrados:75` says ads are shown. The terms and privacy pages say you are ad-supported.
- **Impact:** inconsistent facts reduce LLM citation trust, and the unsupported ad claims create a consumer-claims risk.
- **Diff** (`contra:160`, `apalabrados:154`): replace `5 (…)` with `6 (EN, ES, HE, SV, JA, RU)`.

## 9. Internal links with hard-coded or unverified targets
`contra:223` links to `/es/juego-de-palabras-multijugador`, and `neged:290` links to `/he/hebrew-multiplayer-word-game`. Neither is locale-templated, and I cannot confirm that they, or `/{locale}/lexiclash-vs-wordle`, `/lexiclash-vs-scrabble`, `/lexiclash-vs-cabanagrams` (apalabrados:250-258), `best-online-word-games`, or `hamila-hayomit` exist.
- **Impact:** broken internal links waste crawl budget, and the apalabrados page's links under a non-`es` locale resolve to a page that may not exist.
- **Fix:** check each target against the app router, and use `/es/...` explicitly in the apalabrados cross-links, since the page is Spanish-only.

## 10. Not verifiable here: sitemap, robots, llms.txt, and agent access
No `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, API docs, or MCP/CLI files were provided.
- **Impact:** if these are absent, AI crawlers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot) have no map of the site, and agents cannot reach a product that is a browser game with WebSocket rooms.
- **Check:**
  - The sitemap must include the locale-specific comparison pages with `alternates.languages`, and must exclude the `index: false` variants from items 1 and 2.
  - `robots.txt` should not block AI user agents.
  - Add `/llms.txt` listing the comparison pages, the daily challenge, and the education page.
  - For revenue, the Pro and classroom tiers need a public, crawlable pricing page with `Offer` schema (item 5).

`__tests__/freeTierHonesty.test.ts` and `gapsSetHonesty.test.ts` assert on source-string wiring, not rendered output. They would not catch the invalid-JSON-LD problem in item 6. Add one test that parses each page's `ld+json` payload.

# SEO / GEO audit: LexiClash comparison pages

Scope note: the files supplied are the `lexiclash-vs-*` comparison pages. I couldn't see `sitemap`, `robots`, `llms.txt`, the root layout, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. Findings on those are inferred from the pages or marked as unverified.

## 1. Locale pages are noindexed but still listed as the only hreflang target
`fe-next/app/[locale]/lexiclash-vs-blooket/page.tsx:47` (same pattern in cabanagrams `:44`, classpoint `:56`, flocabulary `:45`, freerice `:49`, kahoot `:46`, mentimeter `:56`, kahoot-gimkit-vocabulary `page.tsx:57`)

- **Impact:** Every non-`en` URL returns `noindex` while its canonical points at `/en`. That sends contradictory signals and wastes crawl budget. It also means the HE/SV/JA/ES/RU copy that was written for the kahoot-gimkit-vocabulary page can never rank or be cited.
- **Fix:** Pick one approach. For English-only pages, the cleanest option is to return 404 or redirect for other locales (freerice already does this via `permanentRedirect`) rather than serving noindex duplicates. Use the same helper everywhere:
```diff
-export default async function Page({ params }: PageProps) {
-  const { locale } = await params;
+export default async function Page({ params }: PageProps) {
+  const { locale } = await params;
+  const redirect = englishComparisonRedirect(locale, 'lexiclash-vs-blooket');
+  if (redirect) permanentRedirect(redirect);
```
(add the imports from freerice `:3,5`). Also make sure the sitemap lists only the `/en` URLs for these pages.

## 2. kahoot-gimkit-vocabulary: translated content that is invisible to search
`fe-next/app/[locale]/lexiclash-vs-kahoot-gimkit-vocabulary/page.tsx:50-57` and `content.ts:140-598`

- **Impact:** About 450 lines of translated copy (sv/ja/es/he/ru) are built for pages that are noindexed. In effect none of that translation work earns impressions.
- **Fix (recommended):** Follow the gimkit page (`lexiclash-vs-gimkit/page.tsx:21-23,50-54,54`) and make each locale self-canonical, with full hreflang and `index: true`:
```diff
-  const pageUrl = `${BASE_URL}/en${PAGE_PATH}`;
+  const pageUrl = `${BASE_URL}/${locale}${PAGE_PATH}`;
+  const languages: Record<string, string> = { 'x-default': `${BASE_URL}/en${PAGE_PATH}` };
+  for (const l of Object.keys(OG_LOCALE)) languages[l] = `${BASE_URL}/${l}${PAGE_PATH}`;
 ...
-      languages: { 'x-default': pageUrl, en: pageUrl },
+      languages,
 ...
-    robots: { index: isEnglish, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
```
The same applies to the JSON-LD `@id` and `item` URLs at `:68,76-78`. Those use `/en` for `@id` but `/${locale}` for breadcrumb items.

## 3. Translated content contradicts itself (Gimkit "5 students")
`content.ts:150,157,168,190,194,235,282,286,426,466,470,511,558,562` against the EN version at `:51,58,65`

- **Impact:** The EN copy says Gimkit Basic is free and unlimited on featured modes. The sv/ja/es/he/ru copy still claims a "5-student" cap. Once the translations are indexed (see #2) this is a factual error that AI engines will quote. It also conflicts with `lexiclash-vs-gimkit`, which cites the 5-player cap for Pro-Exclusive modes only.
- **Fix:** Replace the "5 students" claims in the non-EN rows, intro, FAQ and footer with the EN wording, for example:
```diff
-'✗ 5-elevbegränsning i gratisversion'
+'Basic gratis, Pro-lägen kräver Gimkit Pro'
```
Do the same for the other four locales.

## 4. Inconsistent language and class-size facts across pages
`lexiclash-vs-blooket/page.tsx:69,166`, `lexiclash-vs-cabanagrams/page.tsx:137,168`, `lexiclash-vs-flocabulary/page.tsx:63`, `lexiclash-vs-kahoot/page.tsx:68`

- **Impact:** The same site says "6 languages" while the tables list only 5 (EN/HE/SV/JA/ES). It says "up to 50" in the table and "up to 30 students" at blooket `:166` and flocabulary `:63`. It also says "3 classes × 50" in one place and "no cap" in another (content.ts `:65`). Contradictory facts lower citation confidence and trust.
- **Fix:** Centralize these constants (`LANG_COUNT`, `CLASS_CAP`) and replace the hardcoded strings. At minimum:
```diff
-'✓ EN/HE/SV/JA/ES'
+'✓ EN/HE/SV/JA/ES/RU'
-up to 30 students.
+up to 50 students.
```
Also fix `content.ts:65` ("No cap") to match "3 classes × 50".

## 5. Internal issue numbers and test jargon in indexable copy and titles
`lexiclash-vs-classpoint/page.tsx:22,63,67,85,179-181`, `lexiclash-vs-mentimeter/page.tsx:22,67,176-177`, `lexiclash-vs-blooket/page.tsx:58,71,168`

- **Impact:** Titles like "25 seats / 5 Q Honesty" and text such as "Skip open #1187 (CTR titles)", "foil" and "#1190" are internal tracker notes. They hurt click-through rate, they would be quoted verbatim by AI answers, and they undermine trust. The "lastChecked September 30, 2026" claim at classpoint `:63` is also a stated date that can't be verified.
- **Fix (title and description):**
```diff
-title: 'LexiClash vs ClassPoint Basic Free — 25 seats / 5 Q Honesty (2026) | LexiClash',
+title: 'ClassPoint Free Alternative for Vocabulary: LexiClash vs ClassPoint | LexiClash',
```

## 6. Pages with no BreadcrumbList or `@id`
`lexiclash-vs-classpoint/page.tsx:99-107`, `lexiclash-vs-mentimeter/page.tsx:97-105`, `lexiclash-vs-gimkit/page.tsx:76-84`, `lexiclash-vs-cabanagrams/page.tsx:77-85`

- **Impact:** Weaker entity signals and no breadcrumb display in search results, while sibling pages already have both.
- **Fix:** Copy the `breadcrumbJsonLd` block from kahoot (`:88-96`) into these pages and add a second `<script type="application/ld+json">`. Add `'@id'` to each FAQPage.

## 7. Product and organization structured data is missing from these pages
All files in scope.

- **Impact:** Only FAQPage and BreadcrumbList are emitted. There is no `SoftwareApplication` or `WebApplication` with `offers`, and no `Article` with `datePublished`/`dateModified`. Comparison queries and AI summaries lean on both. (Google has restricted FAQ rich results to a few sites, so FAQ markup is mostly a plain-text citation aid now.)
- **Fix (add to a shared helper and render it on each page):**
```ts
const appJsonLd = {
  '@context':'https://schema.org','@type':'WebApplication',
  name:'LexiClash', url:BASE_URL, applicationCategory:'EducationalApplication',
  operatingSystem:'Web', offers:[{'@type':'Offer',price:'0',priceCurrency:'USD'},
  {'@type':'Offer',name:'Teacher Pro',price:'9',priceCurrency:'USD'}],
};
```

## 8. `revalidate` and `force-dynamic` inconsistencies
`lexiclash-vs-freerice/page.tsx:10` (`force-dynamic`) against `revalidate = 86400` on the others (e.g. blooket `:8`)

- **Impact:** The freerice page is server-rendered on every request, which slows the response for crawlers and costs more. The redirect is also needed per request, so keep it, but you can still cache the English case.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
```
`loadTranslation` is only used for locale-specific rows, and non-English locales redirect first.

## 9. Competitor-only comparison pages with no first-party "what is LexiClash" summary for agents
The pages have no definitional lead (for example "LexiClash is a free multiplayer word game for classrooms…") near the top. They open with a taunt H1 such as blooket `:105` and flocabulary `:102`. Also, none of the pages show the date they were last verified, such as "Pricing checked <date>", which AI engines reward.

- **Impact:** AI engines have nothing to extract as a neutral entity definition, so citations tend to favor the competitor's own site.
- **Fix:** Add a one-sentence definition above the H1 paragraph and a visible "Last verified" line:
```diff
+<p className="sr-only">LexiClash is a free multiplayer word game for classrooms: Boggle-style grids, anagrams and word wheels, no student signup, six languages.</p>
```
Also add `dateModified` to the Article/WebApplication JSON-LD from #7.

## 10. Agent reachability and crawlability (unverified from supplied files)
The supplied files contain no `llms.txt`, MCP server, API docs link, or CLI, and no link from these pages to such a surface. Join-by-code flows and Teacher Pro checkout (`TeacherProCompareCheckoutStrip`) are client components whose content I couldn't inspect.

- **Impact:** If `/llms.txt` is absent, agents and answer engines have no curated index of the comparison pages. Without a machine-readable path (OpenAPI or MCP) an agent cannot create a classroom game or start checkout for a teacher.
- **Fix:** Add `public/llms.txt` listing the `/en` comparison pages and the education hub. Also confirm `app/sitemap.ts` emits only `/en` URLs for English-only pages (see #1) and that `robots.txt` doesn't block `/_next/` assets:
```
# LexiClash
> Free multiplayer word game for classrooms (Boggle-style, anagrams, word wheels). 6 languages, no student signup.
## Comparisons
- [vs Blooket](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/lexiclash-vs-blooket)
- [vs Kahoot](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/lexiclash-vs-kahoot)
- [vs Gimkit/Vocabulary.com](https://www.lexiclash.live/en/lexiclash-vs-kahoot-gimkit-vocabulary)
```

## Priority for revenue
Fix #1 and #2 first, because locale handling decides whether the pages rank at all. Fix #3 and #4 next to stop publishing contradictions, then #5 to lift click-through on the buyer-intent "alternative" queries that feed Teacher Pro.

# SEO/GEO audit: what the supplied files show

I could see only the comparison/landing pages, `llms.txt` content, and the multiplayer client files. I did not see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, the root layout, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. Issues about those are marked "unverified" and left out of the ranked list.

## Ranked issues

### 1. Swedish Wordfeud page: `robots` and hreflang contradict the page
`fe-next/app/[locale]/lexiclash-vs-wordfeud/page.tsx:43`, `:15`, `:37-41`

- Canonical is hardcoded to `/sv/...` and the body is Swedish only. `index: isSwedish` noindexes every other locale, which is fine.
- `en/he/ja/es/ru` URLs still resolve and render the same Swedish body, linking out to `/${locale}/…`. That is thin duplicate content that gets noindexed, but they still receive internal link equity.
- `locale: 'sv_SE'` and `og-image-en.webp` are used, so social previews show an English image on a Swedish page.
- Highest-impact fix: redirect non-sv locales, as the Wordle page already does.

```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+import { permanentRedirect } from 'next/navigation';
@@ export default async function LexiClashVsWordfeudPage({ params }: PageProps) {
     const { locale } = await params;
+    if (locale !== 'sv') permanentRedirect('/sv/lexiclash-vs-wordfeud');
```

Also drop `force-dynamic` (see #2) and use `og-image-sv.webp` if it exists.

### 2. `force-dynamic` on static comparison pages
`lexiclash-vs-wordfeud/page.tsx:4`, `lexiclash-vs-wordle/page.tsx:8`, `lexiclash-vs-wordwall/page.tsx:5`

- These pages have no per-request data. `force-dynamic` removes static caching, so TTFB rises and crawl budget and Core Web Vitals suffer.
- The fix is to delete the line and add `generateStaticParams` returning the locales, or `export const revalidate = 86400`.

```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
```

### 3. Wordle page: hreflang points to localized slugs that the page redirects away
`lexiclash-vs-wordle/page.tsx:43-48`, `:93-94`

- `he` and `es` alternates point to `/he/lexiclash-neged-wordle` and `/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`.
- The comment says the body is English-only, and `englishComparisonRedirect` permanently redirects non-en locales.
- If those slugs don't exist or redirect, Google ignores or flags the hreflang cluster as invalid. Alternates must be self-referencing and return 200 (the same pattern the code applies in `multiplayer-word-game-online`).

```diff
       languages: {
         'x-default': `${BASE_URL}/en/lexiclash-vs-wordle`,
         en: `${BASE_URL}/en/lexiclash-vs-wordle`,
-        he: `${BASE_URL}/he/lexiclash-neged-wordle`,
-        es: `${BASE_URL}/es/lexiclash-contra-wordle`,
       },
```

If real localized pages exist, keep them and verify they return 200.

### 4. Misleading title and metadata on `multiplayer-word-game-online`
`multiplayer-word-game-online/page.tsx:18`, `:44-49`, `:51`

- The title is "Scrabble Online Free — Multiplayer Word Game". It targets a trademark term, while the H1 (`:104`) says "Real-Time Boggle & Word Battles".
- Per the comment at `:44`, the route 308-redirects to `/en/free-multiplayer-word-game`. Yet it declares `canonical` to itself and `index: true`. Search engines will see a redirecting URL declared as canonical, so its metadata is dead weight.
- `:57` `validLocale = locale === 'en' ? 'en' : 'en'` is a no-op, and the component is served for all locales with an English-only body.

```diff
-    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: false, follow: true },
```

Or delete the file if the redirect is permanent. Repoint the internal links (`/en/words-with-friends-alternative` at `:199`, plus `/es/…` and `/sv/…`) to the canonical page.

### 5. Factual drift in FAQ JSON-LD, which AI engines quote
`multiplayer-word-game-online/page.tsx:74`, `:127`; `lexiclash-vs-wordwall/page.tsx:157`

- The FAQ says "English, Hebrew, Swedish, and Japanese". The feature list says "6 languages (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES)", which lists only five. The Wordwall page says "EN/HE/ES/SV/JA" under a "6 native-dictionary languages" heading. `llms.txt` says 6 including Russian.
- The Wordwall FAQ claims "up to 50 students", while `:54` and `:156` say 30 students per session.
- Contradictory claims reduce citation trust and invite hallucinated answers.

```diff
-'Play in 6 languages (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES)',
+'Play in 6 languages (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES, RU)',
```

Make the same fix in the FAQ at `:74`, and pick one student cap per page.

### 6. Hebrew comparison page: JSON-LD gaps and an English-body gap
`lexiclash-vs-wordwall-kahoot-quizlet/page.tsx:92-108`, `:45`

- Breadcrumb `item` URLs use `${locale}`. Under noindex locales they point to the non-canonical page.
- Use `he` explicitly, as the FAQ `@id` already does.

```diff
-item: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}${PAGE_PATH}`
+item: `${BASE_URL}/he${PAGE_PATH}`
```

Apply the same change to the other two breadcrumb items (`:104`, `:105`).

- This is the only comparison page with `dir="rtl"`. There is no `lang` attribute on `<main>`, so add `lang="he"`.
- There is no `force-static` or `generateStaticParams`, so the page is dynamic.

### 7. Missing structured data beyond FAQ
All the comparison pages (`wordfeud:82-99`, `wordle:80-106`, `multiplayer-word-game-online:84-100`)

- Only FAQPage is emitted. Google restricted FAQ rich results to authoritative sites, so impressions gain little.
- `wordfeud` and `wordle` lack BreadcrumbList. The other pages have it.
- No `SoftwareApplication` or `WebApplication` (free, `offers.price: 0`, `applicationCategory: Game`) on the product pages. This is the structured data most useful for AI and product citations.

```diff
+const appJsonLd = JSON.stringify({
+  '@context': 'https://schema.org',
+  '@type': 'WebApplication',
+  name: 'LexiClash',
+  url: BASE_URL,
+  applicationCategory: 'GameApplication',
+  operatingSystem: 'Any (browser)',
+  offers: { '@type': 'Offer', price: '0', priceCurrency: 'USD' },
+});
```

Render it in the home page layout or the multiplayer page and add `<script type="application/ld+json">`. Also, the Wordfeud page uses a plain `<script>` with JSON as a child (`:99`), which React escapes. Use `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` as the other pages do.

### 8. `/multiplayer` product route: no crawlable body without JS interaction
`multiplayer/MpPhaseRouter.tsx:34-42`, `:54-57`

- `EntryScreen` is SSR'd, which is good. `PageClient` is `'use client'` and `useLegacyClassroomRedirect` (`PageClient.tsx:72-73`) returns an empty div while a redirect is pending.
- I could not see `page.tsx` or its `generateMetadata`. I could not confirm that the H1, text, and JSON-LD are emitted server-side.
- Unverified. Check the raw HTML with `curl -s https://www.lexiclash.live/en/multiplayer | grep -i '<h1'`.

### 9. `llms.txt` gaps for agents
`llms.txt/content.ts:43-93`, `:388-399`

- The English variant has no comparison pages (`/lexiclash-vs-wordle`, `/lexiclash-vs-wordwall`) or pricing: Teacher Pro at $9/mo is not mentioned. These are the pages AI engines cite.
- Locale files point to `/llms-full.txt`, but `/llms.txt` at the root (the standard path) is not in these files. Locale paths like `/en/llms.txt` are non-standard, so unverified.
- Hebrew says "pending human translator", with English meta-prose. Fine.
- Nothing describes how an agent can use the product. There is no API, MCP, or CLI mention anywhere, and no evidence that one exists.

```diff
+## Comparisons
+- LexiClash vs Wordle: ${BASE}/en/lexiclash-vs-wordle
+- LexiClash vs Wordwall (teachers): ${BASE}/en/lexiclash-vs-wordwall
+
+## Pricing
+- Free for all play modes. Teacher Pro: $9/month (analytics, printable reports).
```

### 10. Agent reachability: no visible API, MCP, or CLI
Nothing in the supplied files exposes a programmatic entry point.

- Gameplay runs over Socket.IO (`MpPhaseRouter` and `useMultiplayerSocket`). There is no documented REST or MCP endpoint.
- The cheapest win for first revenue (Teacher Pro) is a public read-only endpoint, such as `GET /api/daily` for the daily puzzle. Add an `/.well-known/` or `llms.txt` pointer so agents can cite and link the daily puzzle with a deep link (`?room=` and `?mode=` already work, per `preselectMode.test.ts`).
- Unverified. I did not see any route handlers other than `llms.txt/route.ts`.

I could only see the files in this diff set. `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, the root layout's JSON-LD and any API/MCP surface were not provided, so I make no claims about them. Findings are ordered by impact on first revenue.

## 1. `/pricing` is `force-dynamic` only to pass a cookie to the client
**`fe-next/app/[locale]/pricing/page.tsx:13,35,56`**

- **Impact:** This is your only money page, and it is rendered per request with `no-store`. That means slower TTFB, no CDN caching, and crawlers hit your origin on every fetch. The only reason is `hasSession`, which the client can derive itself from `useAuth()`. The `cookies()` call is what makes the route dynamic.
- **Diff:** I haven't seen `UpgradePricingPageClient`, so confirm it can read auth from context first.

```diff
-import { cookies } from 'next/headers';
-import { hasSupabaseSessionCookie } from '@/lib/auth/hasSessionCookie';
...

+export const revalidate = 3600;
+export function generateStaticParams() {
+  return SUPPORTED_LOCALES.map((locale) => ({ locale }));
+}
...
-  const hasSession = hasSupabaseSessionCookie((await cookies()).getAll().map((c) => c.name));
...
-      <UpgradePricingPageClient hasSession={hasSession} />
+      <UpgradePricingPageClient />
```

Note that `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` is declared at line 15, so place `generateStaticParams` after it. Also, the canonical here is `/pricing` (line 20), but the Product JSON-LD offer points to `/teacher/upgrade` (comment, line 27). Two URLs with the same content and different canonical signals split ranking. Pick one and make the other `alternates.canonical` to it.

## 1. Rules page ships two conflicting HowTo schemas, and the client one is rendered with the wrong URL host

  - The page is `noindex`, yet it emits `ProfilePage` JSON-LD with a placeholder `Person` named "LexiClash Player".

- **Impact:** There is no ranking benefit. Placeholder entities could pollute the knowledge graph if the schema is ever read. It also adds markup weight for no gain.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-      <script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify([breadcrumbSchema, profilePageSchema]) }} />
       {children}
```
Remove the `breadcrumbSchema` and `profilePageSchema` definitions as well. Also remove the hreflang block from this noindex page.

## 7. Redundant `noindex` pages still emit canonical and hreflang metadata
- **Where:** `referrals/page.tsx:9-14`, `quests/page.tsx:10-15`, `profile/words/page.tsx:14-19`.
- **Problem:**
  - These spread the full alternates from `generatePageMetadata` and then override `robots`.
  - `quests` and `referrals` use `force-dynamic` and hold no public content.
- **Impact:** Minor. `hreflang` on noindexed URLs is ignored and can generate Search Console warnings. Make sure these paths are also absent from the sitemap and disallowed from crawling only if they are not noindexed. Disallowing them in robots.txt would prevent Google from ever seeing the `noindex`.
- **Diff:** Make `generatePageMetadata` omit `alternates` when `noIndex` is set, and use that flag consistently:
```diff
-  const meta = await generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'quests', path: '/quests', locale });
-  return { ...meta, robots: { index: false, follow: true, googleBot: { index: false, follow: true } } };
+  return generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'quests', path: '/quests', locale, noIndex: true });
```

## 8. Rules-page content sits behind a client-only demo, and the visible text is not in the SSR payload for key parts
- **Where:** `rules/PageClient.tsx:80` and `:138`.
- **Problem:**
  - The `InteractiveGridDemo` is loaded with `ssr: false`, so the "interactive tutorial" is absent from the server HTML.
  - The `m.*` framer-motion sections start at `opacity: 0`. They are still in the DOM, so crawlers read them, but this is at risk if the hydration fails.
  - The real text is in `GamePageSeoContent`, which is server-rendered, so crawlers are covered.
- **Impact:** Low for Google. For agents that don't run JS, the step-by-step is covered only by the server FAQ block.
- **Diff:** Add a static `<noscript>` or server-rendered step list inside `page.tsx`:
```diff
+      <ol className="sr-only">
+        {content.features.slice(0, 5).map((f) => <li key={f}>{f}</li>)}
+      </ol>
```
This is cheap, and it also gives AI crawlers the steps without JS.

## Not auditable from this diff
Please send these files for the highest-leverage fixes:
- `app/sitemap.ts` (check that `/ru` and the en-only pages are included).
- `app/robots.ts`.
- `public/llms.txt`.
- The root `layout.tsx`. This should carry the `WebSite` and `Organization` `@id`s that these schemas reference as `#website`.
- Any `/api` or MCP surface, for agent access to the product.

Description and OG tags exist, but the indexed body has no text to match queries like "solo word game". It also has a thin-content risk and no citable passage for LLMs.

`fe-next/app/[locale]/singleplayer/layout.tsx:52-75`

- **What happens:** There are 22 entries, including `en-IL`, `en-JP`, `en-SE`, `en-ES`, `en-MX` and `en-AU`. These are invented region variants that all point to `/en`. `es-US` points to `/es`, and `ru` is missing even though the layout type includes it (`:6`).

## Shard 48

# SEO / GEO audit: `/teacher/*` and `/tools` slice

This slice has almost no search or agent surface of its own. Only `teacher/upgrade` and `tools` are meant to rank, and the rest is authenticated app UI. Sitemap, robots, llms.txt, hreflang output and any API/MCP/CLI are not in the files shown. The `generatePageMetadata` and `teacherUpgradeJsonLd` internals are also not shown, so I can't verify canonical or hreflang there. Findings are limited to what is visible, highest impact first.

## 1. Pricing page is `force-dynamic` only because of a session cookie
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/teacher/upgrade/page.tsx:13` and `:30`
- **Impact:** This is the page that earns the first revenue and the one AI answers will cite for "LexiClash Teacher Pro price". `cookies()` makes it uncacheable per request, which hurts TTFB and crawl efficiency. Under load, slow responses to Googlebot and AI fetchers are the realistic risk.
- **Fix:** Make the page static per locale and detect the session on the client. The client already waits for `useAuth` and `useTeacherPro`, so `hasSession` only prevents a first-paint flash.

```diff
-import { cookies } from 'next/headers';
-import { hasSupabaseSessionCookie } from '@/lib/auth/hasSessionCookie';
...
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
+export function generateStaticParams() {
+  return SUPPORTED_LOCALES.map((locale) => ({ locale }));
+}
...
-  const hasSession = hasSupabaseSessionCookie((await cookies()).getAll().map((c) => c.name));
...
-      <UpgradePricingPageClient hasSession={hasSession} />
+      <UpgradePricingPageClient />
```
In `PageClient.tsx:34`, read the cookie client-side:
```diff
-export default function UpgradePricingPageClient({ hasSession = false }: { hasSession?: boolean } = {}) {
+export default function UpgradePricingPageClient() {
+  const [hasSession] = useState(() => typeof document !== 'undefined' && hasSupabaseSessionCookie(document.cookie.split('; ').map((c) => c.split('=')[0])));
```

## 2. Analytics page has hard-coded English metadata and is indexable
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/teacher/classroom/[id]/analytics/page.tsx:25-28`
- **Impact:** Every sibling teacher page uses `generatePageMetadata(... noIndex: true)`. This one doesn't, so it ships an English title in all six locales and no robots directive. Per-classroom UUID URLs become a crawlable, duplicate, thin-content surface. A signed-out bot only gets a redirect to `/education/access`, which wastes crawl budget and can produce soft-404-style entries.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-import { Metadata } from 'next';
+import type { Metadata } from 'next';
+import { generatePageMetadata } from '@/lib/seo/generatePageMetadata';
 import { AnalyticsPageClient } from './PageClient';
-export const metadata: Metadata = {
-  title: 'Class Analytics | LexiClash',
-  description: 'Track student progress, lesson effectiveness, and vocabulary mastery in real-time',
-};
+export async function generateMetadata({ params }: { params: Promise<{ locale: string; id: string }> }): Promise<Metadata> {
+  const { locale, id } = await params;
+  return generatePageMetadata({ seoKey: 'education', path: `/teacher/classroom/${id}/analytics`, locale, noIndex: true });
+}
```

## 3. No `noindex` safety net at the `/teacher` layout
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/teacher/layout.tsx:1-5`
- **Impact:** Finding 2 happened because noindex is opt-in per page. The next teacher route someone adds will leak into the index. `upgrade` is the only public page in this tree.
- **Fix:** Default the segment to noindex and let `upgrade` opt back in. This assumes `generatePageMetadata` emits explicit `robots` for `upgrade`.
```diff
-import { ReactNode } from 'react';
+import type { Metadata } from 'next';
+import { ReactNode } from 'react';
+export const metadata: Metadata = { robots: { index: false, follow: false } };
```

## 4. Pricing JSON-LD falls back to hard-coded English copy and price
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/teacher/upgrade/page.tsx:36-41`
- **Impact:** If `seo.teacherUpgrade.description` is missing in a locale, the Product schema ships "$9/month" in English. That conflicts with the visible localized page, and a mismatch between schema and page weakens rich results and AI price citations. The price is also duplicated by hand rather than sourced from the checkout config.
- **Fix:** Take the price from a single constant and fail loudly instead of falling back silently. `TEACHER_PRO_PRICE` below is a placeholder for whatever constant checkout uses.
```diff
-    description:
-      seo.description ||
-      'Upgrade to Teacher Pro for unlimited classrooms and unlimited students per class. $9/month.',
+    description: seo.description ?? (await loadTranslation('en')).seo?.teacherUpgrade?.description,
```
`buildTeacherProProductJsonLd` should also take its price from `TEACHER_PRO_PRICE`. The same constant should feed the `$9` seen in `planStates.test.tsx:92`.

## 5. Tools hub is fully client-rendered, with a placeholder card
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/[locale]/tools/PageClient.tsx:1`, `:12-13`, `:46-60`
- **Impact:**
  - Locale comes from `useParams` and copy from `useMemo`. It will SSR, but anything JSON-LD or metadata-related has to live in a `page.tsx` I can't see. If that file is missing, the hub has no CollectionPage/ItemList markup.
  - The "coming soon" card is a dash-only block that adds no indexable content.
  - Dead weight on the only hub linking to the word-solver (the one ranking tool) dilutes it.
- **Fix:** Pass `locale` and `content` from the server `page.tsx` as props, and drop the placeholder until a second tool exists.
```diff
-export default function ToolsHubPageClient() {
-  const params = useParams();
-  const locale = (params?.locale as string) || 'en';
-  const content = useMemo(() => getContent(locale), [locale]);
+export default function ToolsHubPageClient({ locale, content }: { locale: string; content: ReturnType<typeof getContent> }) {
```
```diff
-          {/* Coming Soon placeholder */}
-          <div className={cn('block bg-neo-navy-light/50 ... ')}> ... </div>
```
