## Shard 49

# SEO / GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]/…`)

I could only see the tools, vocabulary, word-blast, word-craft, word-craft-landing, `u/[username]` and unsubscribe routes. I did not see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP/CLI surface, so I make no claims about those. Each item below is something the files show.

## 1. Public profile pages are client-only, with no metadata, no `<h1>` until after hydration, and no index control
`fe-next/app/[locale]/u/[username]/page.tsx:1-25`
- **Impact:** The server returns only a skeleton, because the data comes from a client-side tRPC query (`PageClient.tsx:31`). Googlebot and AI crawlers see no content, title or canonical. Shared profile links also get a generic preview, which hurts the viral loop.
- **Fix:** Add `generateMetadata` and render the name server-side. If you don't want thin profiles indexed, set `noindex`.
```diff
+import type { Metadata } from 'next';
+export async function generateMetadata({ params }: { params: Promise<{ locale: string; username: string }> }): Promise<Metadata> {
+  const { locale, username } = await params;
+  return {
+    title: `${username} on LexiClash — Word Game Profile`,
+    description: `See ${username}'s level, longest word and season rank on LexiClash.`,
+    alternates: { canonical: `https://www.lexiclash.live/${locale}/u/${username}` },
+    robots: { index: false, follow: true },
+  };
+}
```

## 2. The word-solver page is `force-dynamic`, and its results sit behind a client-only API
`word-solver/page.tsx:11`, `wordList.ts:10`
- **Impact:** `force-dynamic` removes caching and ISR, so TTFB is slower on your highest-intent tool page. The page has no static word content for the long-tail queries it targets.
- **Fix:** Nothing in the page reads cookies or headers, so make it ISR, as the tools hub already is.
```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
```

## 3. The tools hub has no JSON-LD and no canonical-language handling for the h1
`tools/page.tsx:131-145`
- **Impact:** The hub is the parent in the breadcrumb, but it has no `BreadcrumbList`, `ItemList` or `WebSite` schema. The solver page's breadcrumb points at `/tools`, so Google gets a parent that carries no structure. The hub's title is also the generic "LexiClash Tools". Its metadata comes from `toolsHub.title`, not the richer `toolsSeoContent.title`, which is only used in the body.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-    title: `${content.toolsHub.title} | LexiClash`,
-    description: content.toolsHub.description,
+    title: `${toolsSeoContent[validLocale]?.title ?? content.toolsHub.title} | LexiClash`,
+    description: toolsSeoContent[validLocale]?.description ?? content.toolsHub.description,
```
`toolsSeoContent` is declared after `generateMetadata`. That is fine at runtime, but move it above for readability. Also add an `ItemList` JSON-LD script listing the word-solver URL.

## 4. Ghost hreflang variants and an `og:locale` that is not a valid locale code
`tools/page.tsx:25-42`, `word-solver/page.tsx:22-46`
- **Impact:** Seventeen region variants (`en-JP`, `en-SE`, `es-US` and so on) are repeated on every page. They are noise, and they add nothing over `en`, `es` and so on. Meanwhile `ru` has no region variant, and the lists are copy-pasted in two files. `openGraph.locale: validLocale` (`tools/page.tsx:50`) emits `en`, not `en_US`, which is an invalid OG locale.
- **Fix:** Drop the region lines and use one shared helper. For OG, use the `OG_LOCALE` map already in `vocabulary-games-for-middle-school/page.tsx:16`.
```diff
-  languages['en-IL'] = ...  // delete lines 27-42
+  // keep only x-default + the 6 base locales
-      locale: validLocale,
+      locale: ({en:'en_US',he:'he_IL',sv:'sv_SE',ja:'ja_JP',es:'es_ES',ru:'ru_RU'})[validLocale],
```

## 5. The word-solver page ignores its own `ru` locale in the language picker, and its OG image is a generic one
`word-solver/PageClient.tsx:12-18`, `word-solver/page.tsx:72-83`
- **Impact:** Russian is a supported locale with a Russian dictionary, but it is missing from `LANGUAGES`. Russian visitors cannot select their own dictionary. The page also uses a static `/og-image.jpg`, whereas the hub uses `defaultOgImages(locale)`, so previews are inconsistent.
- **Fix:**
```diff
   { code: 'es', label: 'Espa\u00F1ol', flag: '\u{1F1EA}\u{1F1F8}' },
+  { code: 'ru', label: '\u0420\u0443\u0441\u0441\u043A\u0438\u0439', flag: '\u{1F1F7}\u{1F1FA}' },
```
```diff
-      images: [{ url: `${BASE_URL}/og-image.jpg`, ...
+      images: defaultOgImages(locale),
```

## 6. Non-English pages ship English content in the JSON-LD and the page body
`word-solver/page.tsx:158-181`; `vocabulary-games-for-middle-school/content.ts:320-414`
- **Impact:**
  - The "Popular Anagram Lookups" block is hardcoded English on every locale.
  - The `ja` middle-school content is mostly English under Japanese headings. It is noindexed, so it earns nothing, but it still leaks into hreflang and in-app links.
  - The `he` FAQ answer says "free with no premium tier", which contradicts the English Teacher Pro $9/mo copy at `content.ts:96`. AI engines that crawl both will quote the conflicting pricing. The `es` FAQ at `content.ts:476` has the same conflict.
- **Fix:** Update the `he` and `es` answers at lines 191 and 476 to match the English pricing claim. Translate the `ja` strings, or point `ja` at the English content explicitly.

## 7. The middle-school page breadcrumb and `@id` disagree with the canonical
`vocabulary-games-for-middle-school/page.tsx:75-77`
- **Impact:** The canonical is always `/en/...`, but the breadcrumb leaf links to `${locale}${PAGE_PATH}`, a noindexed URL on non-English pages. The leaf name is the full meta title ("… (2026) | LexiClash"), which is too long for a breadcrumb. Google may drop the rich result.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-{ ..., position: 3, name: c.metaTitle, item: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}${PAGE_PATH}` },
+{ ..., position: 3, name: 'Vocabulary Games for Middle School', item: `${BASE_URL}/en${PAGE_PATH}` },
```

## 8. The word-blast page is missing an `Organization`/`SoftwareApplication` link, and its JSON-LD URLs ignore the canonical
`word-blast-game/page.tsx:127-142`, `word-craft-game/page.tsx:155-170`
- **Impact:** The `VideoGame` schema uses `url: .../${locale}/word-blast-game` even for the noindexed locales, so structured data points at pages that are noindexed. It also lacks `image`, `aggregateRating` and `@id`, and the `HowToStep` `url`s all point at `/blast`, a noindexed shell. There is also no `BreadcrumbList` on word-blast or word-craft-game.
- **Fix:**
```diff
-    url: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}/word-blast-game`,
+    '@id': `${BASE_URL}/en/word-blast-game#game`,
+    url: `${BASE_URL}/en/word-blast-game`,
+    image: `${BASE_URL}/og-image-en.webp`,
```
Do the same in `word-craft-game/page.tsx:159`.

## 9. Conflicting hreflang and canonical strategy between the WordCraft pages
`word-craft-game/page.tsx:43-56` and `word-craft-landing/page.tsx:40-51`
- **Impact:**
  - **word-craft-game:** non-English pages are canonicalized to `/en` and `noindex`ed, yet hreflang advertises `he/sv/ja/es/ru` URLs. That is contradictory, and Google ignores hreflang to noindexed or non-self-canonical pages.
  - **word-craft-landing:** the canonical is self-referential per locale while the page is also `noindex`. That mixed signal is wasteful.
  - The two pages target the same keywords ("word strategy game", "scrabble alternative"), which splits ranking signal and risks cannibalisation.
- **Fix:** Match the blast page and keep only `x-default` and `en` in `languages`. For the landing page, canonicalize non-English pages to `/en`. Consolidate the two WordCraft pages, or differentiate their keywords.
```diff
-      canonical: pageUrl,
+      canonical: locale === 'en' ? pageUrl : `${BASE_URL}/en${WORDCRAFT_LANDING_PATH}`,
```

## 10. The `word-craft` game route has no indexable content or metadata visible here
`word-craft/PageClient.tsx:125-139`
- **Impact:** The route is fully client-rendered, and the only text is an `sr-only` h1 rendered after the phase initializer runs. That is acceptable for a game shell, but make sure the route has `noindex` and a canonical pointing at `/word-craft-game`. Otherwise the shell competes with the landing pages for the same queries. I could not see its `page.tsx`.
- **Fix:** Verify `word-craft/page.tsx` exports:
```ts
robots: { index: false, follow: true },
alternates: { canonical: `${BASE_URL}/en/word-craft-game` },
```

## Not assessable from these files
I could not see a sitemap, robots, `llms.txt`, a public API or MCP server, or a CLI. Check these separately.
- The `/api/word-solver` endpoint (`wordList.ts:10`) is the one agent-usable capability visible here, and it is undocumented. Publishing it in an OpenAPI spec and in `llms.txt` would be the cheapest agent-reachability win.
- Several pages assert "500+ common words" while others say "full dictionary". That is an accuracy risk if AI engines quote it.

## Shard 50

# SEO/GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next/app/[locale]/…`)

Only a slice of the repo was provided: `word-craft`, `word-games-for-the-classroom`, `word-games-online-free`, `word-of-the-day`, `word-tower` and one test for `words-with-friends-alternative`. I could not see `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt` (only its test), the root layout, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. Findings are limited to what these files show. The ten below are ranked by impact.

## 1. Classroom landing: non-English locales are noindexed and canonicalised to English, with no hreflang
`word-games-for-the-classroom/page.tsx:29,50-57`

- **Problem:** `pageUrl` is hardcoded to `/en/...`, so the `/sv`, `/ja`, `/es`, `/he` and `/ru` pages all canonicalise to English and are `index:false`.
- **Cost:** the page also emits localised titles, FAQ JSON-LD and `inLanguage` for those locales, and all of it is wasted. This is the page with the clearest revenue intent (the Teacher Pro $9 CTA).
- **Fix:** either index the localised pages with self-canonicals and full hreflang, or drop the translation work. Given the work already done, index them.

```diff
-  const pageUrl = `${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_BASE}/en${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_PATH}`;
+  const pageUrl = `${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_BASE}/${locale}${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_PATH}`;
@@
     alternates: {
       canonical: pageUrl,
-      languages: {
-        'x-default': pageUrl,
-        en: pageUrl,
-      },
+      languages: {
+        'x-default': `${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_BASE}/en${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_PATH}`,
+        ...Object.fromEntries(CLASSROOM_LOCALES.map((l) => [l, `${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_BASE}/${l}${CLASSROOM_WORD_GAMES_PATH}`])),
+      },
     },
-    robots: { index: isEnglish, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
```

Also change the OG `url` to `pageUrl`, and import `CLASSROOM_LOCALES` from `./content`. Delete the now-unused `isEnglish`.

## 2. `/word-games-online-free`: same pattern, a broad-keyword page that is English-only and self-contradictory
`word-games-online-free/page.tsx:13-14,34-41`

- **Problem:** the page is `index:false` for non-`en` locales, yet hardcodes `/en/` as the canonical. The content is English literals, so a localised URL serves English.
- **Cost:** that duplicate content is correctly noindexed, but non-en visitors reach English text. Add a `notFound`/redirect for non-en, or localise.
- **Fix (cheapest):** redirect non-en to the en URL so link equity consolidates.

```diff
+import { redirect } from 'next/navigation';
@@ export default async function WordGamesOnlineFreePage({ params }: PageProps) {
   const { locale } = await params;
+  if (locale !== 'en') redirect('/en/word-games-online-free');
```

## 3. FAQ JSON-LD on `/word-games-online-free` is built from the same `faqs` array, but the page is missing Organization, WebPage and Breadcrumb data
`word-games-online-free/page.tsx:65-84`

- **Problem:** only FAQPage is emitted, via a raw `<script>`. The page has no `BreadcrumbList` or `speakable`/`data-answer` block like the classroom page has. The 4 FAQ answers are the best extractable text for AI citation, but they sit inside collapsed `<details>`, and the intro paragraph (line 91) is not marked as the answer.
- **Fix:** reuse the existing `JsonLd` and `GeoFaqList` components, as the classroom page does.

```diff
-      <script type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: faqJsonLd }} />
+      <JsonLd data={JSON.parse(faqJsonLd)} />
+      <BreadcrumbJsonLd items={[{ name: 'LexiClash', url: `${BASE_URL}/en` }, { name: 'Word Games Online Free', url: `${BASE_URL}/en/word-games-online-free` }]} />
@@
-        <p className="mb-8 text-lg leading-relaxed text-neo-gray-200">
+        <p data-answer className="mb-8 text-lg leading-relaxed text-neo-gray-200">
```

## 4. Word-of-the-day hub: `force-dynamic` plus a client component, and the main hub is the only indexable surface
`word-of-the-day/page.tsx:6`, `[date]/page.tsx:14`, `PageClient.tsx:59-63`

- **Problem:** `force-dynamic` means every crawl is a cold SSR with no CDN caching, which risks TTFB and crawl-budget problems.
- **Problem:** `PageClient` re-derives "today" in the browser (`new Date().toISOString()`) only when `featuredWord` is absent. The server passes it, so that is fine. But the previous-words list sits in a `max-h-[500px] overflow-y-auto` box, and `m.*` initial `opacity:0` animations hide content until hydration.
- **Cost:** LCP and "hidden at first paint" content reduce the value of the SSR HTML.
- **Fix:** use ISR on the hub and drop the opacity-0 initial state on the primary `<article>`.

```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 3600;
```

`PageClient.tsx:108`:

```diff
-            initial={{ opacity: 0, scale: 0.95 }}
+            initial={false}
```

If `useSearchParams` in the header blocks prerender (per the comment), wrap `AutoHideHeader` in `<Suspense>` rather than disabling static rendering for the whole route.

## 5. Word-of-the-day: hreflang is bloated with fabricated regional variants, and the title/description fallback maps are dead code
`word-of-the-day/page.tsx:14-28,56-83`

- **Problem:** hreflang includes entries such as `en-SE`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `en-MX`, `en-AU` and `es-CO`, all pointing at the same URLs. This adds no signal, and Google may treat the set as noise.
- **Problem:** `titleMap` and `descriptionMap` are never used (`void titleMap`), and they lack `ru`.
- **Fix:**

```diff
-      languages: {
-        'x-default': ..., he: ..., en: ..., sv: ..., ja: ..., es: ..., ru: ...,
-        'ru-RU': ... through 'es-CO': ...
-      },
+      languages: {
+        'x-default': `${SITE_URL}/en/word-of-the-day`,
+        en: `${SITE_URL}/en/word-of-the-day`,
+        he: `${SITE_URL}/he/word-of-the-day`,
+        sv: `${SITE_URL}/sv/word-of-the-day`,
+        ja: `${SITE_URL}/ja/word-of-the-day`,
+        es: `${SITE_URL}/es/word-of-the-day`,
+        ru: `${SITE_URL}/ru/word-of-the-day`,
+      },
```

Delete lines 14-28 and the `void` line.

## 6. The word-of-the-day title changes daily, and the per-date pages are noindexed
`word-of-the-day/seo.ts:33`, `[date]/page.tsx:61`

- **Problem:** the hub's title is `Word of the Day: <word> — <date>`. Its only ranking asset therefore changes every day, with no stable head term, and the older per-date pages are noindexed.
- **Cost:** you cannot accumulate rankings for "word of the day".
- **Fix:** lead with the stable phrase and keep the word secondary.

```diff
-  return `${wotdLabel[l]}: ${word.word} — ${dateStr} | LexiClash`;
+  return `${wotdLabel[l]} — ${word.word} (${dateStr}) | LexiClash`;
```

The per-date pages keep that title. For the hub only, drop the date from the title.

## 7. The Word-of-the-Day JSON-LD is a bare array, with wrong date semantics and a dangling `@id`
`word-of-the-day/seo.ts:47,71-105`

- **Problem:** `langTag` maps `ru` to `ru-ES` (any locale other than he/sv/ja falls to `ES`), which is an invalid tag.
- **Problem:** `Article.datePublished/dateModified` are fixed to midnight of the entry date, though the fallback rotation reuses old entries under new dates.
- **Problem:** `isPartOf: {'@id': '${SITE_URL}/#website'}` points to a node that may not exist. I could not verify this, because the root layout is not in the slice.
- **Fix for the tag:**

```diff
-  const langTag = l === 'en' ? 'en-US' : `${l}-${l === 'he' ? 'IL' : l === 'sv' ? 'SE' : l === 'ja' ? 'JP' : 'ES'}`;
+  const REGION = { en: 'en-US', he: 'he-IL', sv: 'sv-SE', ja: 'ja-JP', es: 'es-ES', ru: 'ru-RU' } as const;
+  const langTag = REGION[l];
```

Note that `seo.test.ts` asserts `he-IL`, which still passes.

## 8. Word Tower page: the game is mounted before the content, in a `force-dynamic` page with a null state while auth loads
`word-tower/page.tsx:11,283-290`, `PageClient.tsx:24-26`

- **Problem:** `WordTowerV2PageClient` returns `null` while auth resolves and the game loads with `ssr:false`. The crawlable text (`GamePageSeoContent`) is server-rendered, which is good. But `force-dynamic` forgoes CDN caching on an indexable landing page.
- **Problem:** the `VideoGameJsonLd` name is the full SEO title ("Word Tower - Stack Words… | Free Game"), not the game name.
- **Fix:**

```diff
-export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic';
+export const revalidate = 86400;
@@
-        name={content.title}
+        name="Word Tower"
```

Only do the `revalidate` change if `loadTranslation` and the JSON-LD components are request-independent. They look it. The `daily` sub-route stays dynamic.

## 9. Word Craft: the shell page is correctly noindexed, but `llms.txt` and the sitemap may still list it
`word-craft/page.tsx:11-13`

- **Problem:** `/word-craft` is noindex, and the landing is `/word-craft-game`. I can't see the sitemap, so I can't confirm the noindex URL is excluded. A noindexed URL in the sitemap, or linked from `llms.txt`, wastes crawl and gives agents a dead shell (the whole body is `ssr:false`).

- **Diff:** unknown without those files, so I won't invent one.

## 10. Agent reachability: nothing in this slice exposes an API, MCP or CLI
This is a coverage gap, not a line-level finding. The only agent-facing artefact visible is `public/llms.txt`, asserted in `jsonld.test.ts:67-76`.

- **Concern:** the test checks for URLs but not for a product description, pricing, or a "how to start a game" instruction. That is the content an agent needs to recommend Teacher Pro.

# SEO / GEO audit: lexiclash.live

I only have part of the repo: the WWF-alternative page, the `/words/*` tree, and some tests. I have not seen `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. Findings below are limited to what these files show. Items I could not verify are listed at the end.

## 1. WWF-alternative page: hreflang and canonical collapse every locale into `/en`
`fe-next/app/[locale]/words-with-friends-alternative/page.tsx:14,35-41`

- `pageUrl` is hardcoded to `/en/...`, so `/he/...`, `/ja/...` and the others all canonicalize to EN.
- Non-EN variants are also `noindex`, which is fine. But the page still renders locale-prefixed internal links (`/${locale}/multiplayer`, lines 276, 373) to them.
- Impact: this is the highest-intent commercial page (a "words with friends alternative" query). Canonical to EN is correct. The real problem is a lack of consistency: the shipped schema and copy contradict each other (see #2).

No diff is needed for the canonical. Fix the contradictions in #2 and #3.

## 2. Contradictory facts across JSON-LD, visible copy, and FAQ (hurts AI citations)
`words-with-friends-alternative/page.tsx:76,104,128,166,177,224`

- Language count: "6 full dictionaries" (lines 76, 128, 224) vs. a stats card of `'5'` Languages (line 166) vs. a table row "5 (EN · HE · SV · JA · ES)" (line 177). The `inLanguage` array (line 104) lists 6.
- Max players: `maxValue: 20` (line 101) vs. "2-20+" everywhere else.
- Impact: LLMs that extract and cross-check claims either drop the page or quote the wrong number.

```diff
-  { value: '5', label: 'Languages', color: 'text-neo-cyan' },
+  { value: '6', label: 'Languages', color: 'text-neo-cyan' },
-  ['Languages', '5 (EN · HE · SV · JA · ES)', '1 (English)'],
+  ['Languages', '6 (EN · HE · SV · JA · ES · RU)', '1 (English)'],
```

## 3. Three overlapping app schemas, with unsupported claims, on one page
`words-with-friends-alternative/page.tsx:90-136`

- `VideoGame` and `SoftwareApplication` both describe the same product.
- `VideoGame` is mixed with `applicationCategory` and `operatingSystem`, which are `SoftwareApplication` properties.
- Both claim `iOS`/`Android` while the `offers` URL is the web app.
- The `SoftwareApplication` has no `aggregateRating`, so Google will not give it a rich result anyway.
- Impact: schema validators warn on these, and mixed types dilute entity clarity.

Make the entity a single `["VideoGame","SoftwareApplication"]` type, drop the duplicate block, and move the compare-page framing out of the entity `name`:

```diff
-const softwareAppJsonLd = JSON.stringify({ ... });
+// removed: duplicate of videoGameJsonLd
...
-  '@type': 'VideoGame',
+  '@type': ['VideoGame', 'SoftwareApplication'],
...
-      <script id="ld-software" type="application/ld+json" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: softwareAppJsonLd }} />
```

## 4. FAQ answers are visible only inside collapsed `<details>`, and one claim contradicts the table
`words-with-friends-alternative/page.tsx:472-481, 76`

- The FAQ text is in the HTML, which is good. The HowTo schema is also server-rendered through a raw `<script>`, which is the correct pattern per `jsonLdServerRendered.test.ts`.
- Google has deprecated HowTo rich results, so the HowTo block (lines 138-150) adds weight with no search benefit. Remove it. The same steps are already on the page.

```diff
-const howToJsonLd = JSON.stringify({ ... });
-      <script id="ld-howto" ... />
```

## 5. Locale-agnostic `Metadata` with a stale `hreflang` set on the word pages
`fe-next/app/[locale]/words/[word]/page.tsx:116-138` and `words/page.tsx:40-62`

- The `languages` map lists 18 entries, including `en-IL`, `en-SE`, `en-JP`, `en-ES`, `en-MX`, `en-AU` and `es-AR`.
- Many are region variants pointing at the same URL as `en`.
- Word-page body copy is English-only (hardcoded strings, line 159), yet it declares `he`, `sv`, `ja`, `es` and `ru` as translations.
- Impact: this is hreflang that claims translations that don't exist. Google may ignore it. `[word]` pages are noindexed anyway.
- The sibling pages already use `enOnlyAlternates(...)`, so the fix is to use the same helper.

```diff
// words/page.tsx
-    alternates: {
-      canonical: `${BASE_URL}/${locale}/words`,
-      languages: Object.fromEntries([ ...18 entries... ]),
-    },
-    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
+    alternates: enOnlyAlternates('/words'),
+    robots: { index: locale === 'en', follow: true },
```

The hub is `index:true` in every locale, but its body is English-only. That creates duplicate pages across 6 locales, and the hub is the one you want to rank.

## 6. The `/words` hub is indexable but is a thin link page that sends link equity to noindexed children
`words/page.tsx:63,125-165`

- Every link on the hub goes to a page that is `noindex` (N-letter lists, starting-with, and the word pages).
- The hub's value to search is therefore near zero, and the AdSense "low value" concern applies to it as well.
- Either noindex the hub too, or keep it indexed only if you want it as the dictionary entry point.

```diff
-    robots: { index: true, follow: true },
+    robots: { index: false, follow: true },
```

This is a business decision, not a certain fix. Given the AdSense rejection documented in the tests, noindexing is consistent.

## 7. Word pages: `force-dynamic` plus a per-request API call, which hurts crawl budget and TTFB
`words/[word]/page.tsx:20,47-62,155`

- `isWordValid` fetches the dictionary API on every render, and the failure path returns `true`, so invalid words display as "Valid".
- A `fetch` with `next: { revalidate }` inside a `force-dynamic` route gives no benefit for the page itself.
- Any crawler that hits these pages gets "Valid" for garbage input if the API is down.

```diff
-    return true; // Fail open for SEO — page still renders
+    return false; // don't assert validity we couldn't verify
```

## 8. `ItemList` schema on noindexed pages
`_nletter/NLetterWordsView.tsx:97-111` and `starting-with/[letter]/page.tsx:76-88`

- Both emit `ItemList`, `FAQPage` and breadcrumb JSON-LD on `noindex` pages. That is wasted bytes, up to 100 `ListItem`s each.
- The FAQ answers in `starting-with` also paste the `letterInfo` text. Because these pages are noindexed, this has no SEO value.
- Skip schema generation when `robots.index` is false.

## 9. FAQ copy contradicts the game description
`words-with-friends-alternative/page.tsx:60` vs `:148`

- Line 60 says WWF "maxes out at 2 players". Line 148 says "Words found by multiple players don't count for anyone". Both are fine individually.
- But line 175 says WWF is "Free with ads + paid boosts" and line 72 says LexiClash has "optional rewarded videos". That is comparable, yet it's presented as a differentiator. Be accurate here, because AI answer engines quote comparison tables verbatim.

## 10. Agent reach: nothing visible
- I could not see `llms.txt`, an OpenAPI spec, an MCP server, or a CLI in the files provided.
- `robots.test.ts` pins an explicit YandexBot rule, but no explicit allow for GPTBot, ClaudeBot or PerplexityBot. With a catch-all `*`, that's fine unless the disallow list is broad.

Check these paths (not provided):
- `app/robots.ts`: confirm there is no AI-bot disallow.
- `public/llms.txt`: if missing, add a ~30-line file. It should list the product one-liner, the `/en/words-with-friends-alternative`, `/en/multiplayer`, `/en/daily` URLs, and the language and player facts from #2.
- `app/sitemap.ts`: confirm `/en/words-with-friends-alternative` is listed with `lastModified`.

## Summary of priorities
1. Fix the language-count contradiction (#2).
2. Collapse the duplicate schemas and drop HowTo (#3, #4).
3. Align hreflang with the English-only content (#5).
4. Decide on the `/words` hub (#6).
5. Check `llms.txt`, `robots.ts` and the sitemap, since I could not see them (#10).

## Findings

### 1. Unauthenticated HTML endpoint can be indexed and leaks admin surface
- **Location:** `fe-next/app/api/admin/game-mode-announcement-preview/route.ts:14`
- **Problem:** Every sibling preview route calls `verifyAdminAuth`. This one does not. Any crawler or bot that finds the URL gets a 200 `text/html` page with a thin, duplicate-style email body.
- **Impact:** It can end up in the index as low-quality content on your domain. It also exposes an admin-namespace route to the public.

```diff
 import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server';
+import { verifyAdminAuth } from '@/lib/auth/adminAuth';
@@
 export async function GET(request: NextRequest) {
+  const authResult = await verifyAdminAuth(request);
+  if (!authResult.success) return authResult.response!;
   const languageParam = request.nextUrl.searchParams.get('language') || 'en';
```

### 2. `send-*` routes trust a forwarded header for auth
- **Location:** `fe-next/app/api/admin/send-android-beta-launch-to-player/route.ts:24`, `send-android-release-launch-to-player/route.ts:20`, `send-test-android-*/route.ts`
- **Problem:** These routes check only `x-admin-user-id`. If the Express middleware ever doesn't strip or overwrite that header, anyone can send emails to players. This is not an SEO issue, but it hurts deliverability and sender reputation, which affects first revenue.
- **Fix:** Use `verifyAdminAuth(request)` as the other routes do.

```diff
-    const adminUserId = request.headers.get('x-admin-user-id');
-    if (!adminUserId) {
-      return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Unauthorized' }, { status: 401 });
-    }
+    const authResult = await verifyAdminAuth(request);
+    if (!authResult.success) return authResult.response!;
```

### 3. Canonical host is inconsistent: `www.` vs apex
- **Locations:**
  - `email-preview/route.ts:13` falls back to `https://www.lexiclash.live`.
  - `game-mode-announcement-preview/route.ts:23`, `reengagement-email-preview/route.ts:21` and `send-bulk-email/route.ts:155` fall back to `https://lexiclash.live`.
- **Impact:** If `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` is unset anywhere, emails and logos link to two different hosts. That splits link equity and analytics, and it confuses which host is canonical. I can't see the `metadataBase` or canonical config, so I can't tell which host is correct.
- **Fix:** Pick one host and share a constant. Make sure the other host 301-redirects and that `metadataBase`, the sitemap and canonical tags use the same value.

```diff
-const baseUrl = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL || 'https://www.lexiclash.live';
+import { SITE_URL } from '@/lib/siteUrl'; // single source of truth
+const baseUrl = SITE_URL;
```

### 4. Locale mapping drifts, and can produce 404 links
- **Location:** `game-mode-announcement-preview/route.ts:8` and `:24`
  - Line 8 allows `ru`, but line 24 only maps `he/sv/ja/es`, so `ru` falls back to `en`.
- **Location:** `send-bulk-email/route.ts:237`
  - `locale = language === 'en' ? 'en' : language` is a no-op. Any unsupported language code becomes `/${locale}/…`, which will 404 or hit a redirect.
- **Impact:** Emails driving traffic to dead locale URLs lose both the visit and the crawl signal.
- **Fix:** Validate the locale against your routing locale list.

```diff
-          const locale = language === 'en' ? 'en' : language;
+          const locale = SUPPORTED_LOCALES.includes(language) ? language : 'en';
```

For line 24 of the preview route, include `'ru'` in the supported set if `/ru/` exists.

### 5. Preview endpoints should send a noindex header
- **Location:** the `new NextResponse(html, …)` responses at `email-preview/route.ts:275`, `game-mode-announcement-preview/route.ts:55` and `reengagement-email-preview/route.ts:63`
- **Impact:** Cheap defense in depth. Even once authed, `X-Robots-Tag` guarantees these never become indexed.

```diff
-    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'text/html; charset=utf-8' },
+    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'text/html; charset=utf-8', 'X-Robots-Tag': 'noindex, nofollow' },
```

All the product-facing logic visible here (game logs, guests, flags, season reset) is admin-gated JSON. That is correct, but nothing in this batch shows a public, documented API, an MCP server, or a CLI. I can't confirm either way.

## 1. Referral share URLs create indexable duplicates of the homepage and trust a spoofable header
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/api/referral/route.ts:100-101` and `fe-next/app/api/referral/stats/route.ts:133-137`.
- **Impact:** every player's link is `https://…?ref=CODE`. The code says 375 codes have been issued. Each shared link is a new URL for the same page. Unless the page has a canonical that strips `ref`, crawlers split signals across them.
- The `Origin` header is used first, so a link can be built against any host, which makes canonical or share-card previews unreliable.
- The URL is also missing the `/` before `?`.

```diff
-    const origin = request.headers.get('origin') || process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL || 'https://www.lexiclash.live';
-    const shareUrl = `${origin}?ref=${profile.referral_code}`;
+    const origin = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL || 'https://www.lexiclash.live';
+    const shareUrl = `${origin}/?ref=${encodeURIComponent(profile.referral_code)}`;
```

Apply the same change in `stats/route.ts:133-137`. Also make sure the root page's canonical is the bare URL without `?ref`.

## 2. The public leaderboard is uncached JSON
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/api/quick-play/leaderboard/route.ts:48`.
- **Impact:** the response is identical for every viewer, but it sends no `Cache-Control` and is limited to 30 requests a minute per IP (`:11-14`). Agents and crawlers fetching it hit the database every time or get 429s.

```diff
-    return NextResponse.json({
+    return NextResponse.json({

         ...

+    }, { headers: { 'Cache-Control': 'public, s-maxage=30, stale-while-revalidate=120' } });
```

## 3. An agent can play but has no discoverable contract
- **Where:** `fe-next/app/api/quick-play/round/route.ts:16-36`.
- **Impact:** this is the one unauthenticated entry to the product. It takes a mode, language and seed, and returns a board. Scoring and submit require a session (`submit/route.ts:35-37`, `challenge/route.ts:29-31`).
- Nothing in these files documents the endpoints for agents. There is no OpenAPI spec, no MCP server, and a plain `{error:'Unauthorized'}` with no `WWW-Authenticate` header.

- **Impact:** three sources for "which languages exist". If hreflang or the sitemap uses a fourth, alternates will point at locales that 404 or omit live ones. That loses impressions in the Hebrew, Swedish, Japanese and Spanish markets.

### 1. The word-solver API can't be discovered or called by an agent

- **Impact:** word-solver is your most citable utility ("words from these letters"). Without a machine-readable contract, assistants can't call it or cite it as a tool.

```diff
+export async function GET() {

+    returns: { words: 'string[] (max 500)', total: 'number', capped: 'boolean' },
```

### 2. Russian is silently served English results
`fe-next/app/api/word-solver/route.ts:14,110`, `fe-next/app/api/themed-words/route.ts:39,69`, `fe-next/app/api/validate-word/route.ts:128`

- **Impact:** if `ru` locale pages or tools exist, they show English answers. That is thin or wrong content on a localized URL, which hurts rankings, and agents get misleading answers.
- **Diff:** make the fallback explicit, or add Russian support. The explicit fix:
```diff
-  const language = (VALID_LANGUAGES.includes(rawLang as SolverLanguage) ? rawLang : 'en') as SolverLanguage;
+  if (!VALID_LANGUAGES.includes(rawLang as SolverLanguage)) {

```


## 1. Root layout canonical points every page without its own canonical at `/`, which is a redirect
**`fe-next/app/layout.tsx:17-19`** (with `fe-next/app/route.ts:68-86`)

- **Cause:** Next.js metadata inherits `alternates` from the parent layout. Any route under `[locale]/…` that doesn't set its own `alternates.canonical` emits `<link rel="canonical" href="https://www.lexiclash.live/">`.
- **Redirect:** `/` has no page. It is a `route.ts` that 307-redirects to `/{locale}` (line 86). Google gets a canonical that redirects and often ignores it, or consolidates deep pages into the homepage.
- **Impact:** possibly the biggest impression leak in the repo, because it hits every long-tail landing, blog and guide page that forgot to override. The comment on line 15 assumes every locale page overrides it. The sitemap tests only check the sitemap, not per-page canonicals, so nothing enforces that.
- **Fix:** remove the root canonical. Make each page own it via `generateMetadata`. Hreflang must also be per-page: lines 19-27 advertise the six locale homepages as the alternates of every inheriting page, which is wrong for deep pages.

```diff
     alternates: {
-        canonical: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/',
-        languages: {
-            'x-default': 'https://www.lexiclash.live/en',
-            en: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/en',
-            he: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/he',
-            sv: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/sv',
-            ja: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/ja',
-            es: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/es',
-            ru: 'https://www.lexiclash.live/ru',
-        },
     },
```
Keep these only in `[locale]/page.tsx` (the home). Add a vitest that walks the `[locale]/**/page.tsx` files and asserts each exports an `alternates.canonical`.

## 2. robots.ts blocks `/_next/static/` and `/_next/image/` for every crawler
**`fe-next/app/robots.ts:23-24`**

- **Cause:** the app is heavily client-rendered, with dynamic providers and `ssr:false` modals (`essential-providers.tsx:37-90`). Googlebot and Bing need `/_next/static/` JS and CSS to render it. Disallowing it leaves them with unstyled, partly hydrated pages, which risks CWV and mobile-friendliness evaluation. Next's `/_next/image` is also the URL used for `<Image>` thumbnails, so this hides images from image search.

- **Fix:**

```diff
-    '/_next/image/',

```

# SEO / GEO audit, LexiClash (`ohadf2015/boggle-new`)

**Scope:** The files I was given contain only one real SEO surface, `fe-next/app/sitemap.ts`, plus `components/education/help/helpSeo.ts` and `helpSitemap.ts`. The rest are layout and test files with no SEO content. I could not see `robots.ts`, `llms.txt`, the page-level `generateMetadata`, the JSON-LD on non-help pages, or any API/MCP/CLI surface. I have not guessed at those and flag them at the end. The sitemap is in good shape: `pruneUnconfirmedAlternates` (`:698`) and the noindex/sitemap alignment are well done. The remaining issues are below.

## 1. Every URL's `lastmod` is the deploy timestamp (`sitemap.ts:20-23`, used ~60× from `:97`)
- **Impact:** Every deploy tells Google that about 430 pages changed. Google learns the signal is unreliable and ignores `lastmod`, so refreshed pages (pricing, daily, new blog posts) are not re-crawled sooner. AI crawlers that use `lastmod` for freshness get no useful signal either.
- **Diff:** Give revenue and static pages their own date constants and keep `LAST_DEPLOYED` only for the truly daily surfaces.
```diff
 const LEGAL_UPDATED = '2026-02-01T00:00:00.000Z';
 const GUIDES_UPDATED = '2026-03-01T00:00:00.000Z';
+// Bump only when the price/plan copy actually changes.
+const PRICING_UPDATED = '2026-07-29T00:00:00.000Z';
+const LANDINGS_UPDATED = '2026-07-01T00:00:00.000Z';
```
```diff
-  addForAllLocales(routes, '/teacher/upgrade', { lastModified: LAST_DEPLOYED, changeFrequency: 'monthly', priority: 0.7 });
+  addForAllLocales(routes, '/teacher/upgrade', { lastModified: PRICING_UPDATED, changeFrequency: 'monthly', priority: 0.7 });
...
-  addForAllLocales(routes, '/pricing', { lastModified: LAST_DEPLOYED, changeFrequency: 'monthly', priority: 0.8 });
+  addForAllLocales(routes, '/pricing', { lastModified: PRICING_UPDATED, changeFrequency: 'monthly', priority: 0.8 });
```
- Apply the same change at `:547` (`seoLandings`) and to the comparison pages at `:325-345` (`LANDINGS_UPDATED`). Keep `LAST_DEPLOYED` for `/daily`, `/word-of-the-day` and `/leaderboard`.

## 2. Possible duplicate pricing URLs: `/pricing` and `/teacher/upgrade` (`sitemap.ts:182-185`)
- **Impact:** `:183-184` calls `/pricing` a "public alias", yet both URLs are listed as separate indexable entries with six locales each (12 URLs). If both render the same page, Google picks a canonical itself and may choose the lower-intent one. That splits the one page that earns revenue. I cannot see either page's `canonical`.
- **Diff:** Pick one winner. If `/pricing` is the alias, 308 it to `/teacher/upgrade` in `next.config.mjs` and delete `:185`. If both are meant to be indexed, they need distinct canonicals and copy.
```diff
-  addForAllLocales(routes, '/pricing', { lastModified: LAST_DEPLOYED, changeFrequency: 'monthly', priority: 0.8 });
```

## 3. Blog and guide slugs are hand-copied into the sitemap (`sitemap.ts:402`, `:410-442`, `:447-471`)
- **Impact:** This is the drift problem your own comment at `:188-192` describes for education pages. A new article ships linked internally but is missing from `sitemap.xml`. The blog is where informational citations come from, so a missed article loses impressions and AI citations.
- **Diff:** Export the slug list from the blog registry and import it, as you already do for `EDUCATION_PAGES`. I have not seen the blog registry's file name, so the path below is a guess.
```diff
+import { BLOG_SLUGS } from '@/lib/seo/blogRegistry'; // path assumed: single source for article slugs
-  const blogArticles = [
-    '10-surprising-benefits-word-games',
-    ...
-  ];
+  const blogArticles = BLOG_SLUGS;
```
- Add a test that fails when a published slug is missing from the sitemap. `helpSeo.test.ts:62-68` is the pattern to copy.

## 4. Help-center pages use the English OG image and article type for every locale (`helpSeo.ts:28`, `:37`)
- **Impact:** Hebrew, Spanish, Japanese and Swedish help pages share an English social card. That lowers share CTR and weakens locale signals. The hub page is also typed `article`.
- **Diff:** `og-image-he.webp` and `og-image-es.webp` appear in the sitemap at `:246` and `:305`; I do not know whether `ja` or `sv` variants exist.
```diff
-  const image = `${HELP_BASE_URL}/og-image-en.webp`;
+  const OG_IMG_LOCALES = ['he', 'es'] as const; // only locales with a real asset
+  const image = `${HELP_BASE_URL}/og-image-${(OG_IMG_LOCALES as readonly string[]).includes(locale) ? locale : 'en'}.webp`;
```
```diff
-      type: 'article',
+      type: path === HELP_PATH ? 'website' : 'article',
```

## 5. `TechArticle` JSON-LD has no author or publish date (`helpSeo.ts:101-121`)
- **Impact:** AI answer engines weigh authorship and dates when choosing what to cite. The page has `dateModified` but no `datePublished`, no `author`, and no `image`. You already have an author page, `/about/ohad-fisher` (`sitemap.ts:577`), so linking it costs nothing.
- **Diff:**
```diff
     dateModified: HELP_UPDATED,
+    datePublished: HELP_UPDATED,
     mainEntityOfPage: url,
+    author: { '@type': 'Person', name: 'Ohad Fisher', url: `${HELP_BASE_URL}/${locale}/about/ohad-fisher` },
     audience: { '@type': 'EducationalAudience', educationalRole: 'teacher' },
```
- Replace `datePublished` with the real first-publish date if it differs from `HELP_UPDATED`.

## 6. Home-page sitemap images include favicons and icons (`sitemap.ts:83-90`, `:101`)
- **Impact:** `favicon.ico`, `icon-192.png` and `apple-touch-icon.png` are not content images. Listing them dilutes the image sitemap, and `og-image-he` is added twice for `he` (`:101` repeats an entry already in `commonImages`). The impact is minor but the fix is easy.

```diff
   const commonImages = [
     `${BASE_URL}/og-image-en.webp`,
     `${BASE_URL}/og-image-he.webp`,
-    `${BASE_URL}/favicon.ico`,

-    `${BASE_URL}/apple-touch-icon.png`,

...
-      images: [...commonImages, `${BASE_URL}/og-image-${locale === 'he' ? 'he' : 'en'}.webp`],
+      images: commonImages,
```

## 7. Hand-written hreflang blocks that the sanitizer silently cuts (`sitemap.ts:550-570`, `:234-245`, `:290-303`)
- **Impact:** The eight `seoLandings` pages each list the same `free-multiplayer-word-game` cluster. They also list invented region codes such as `en-SE`, `en-JP` and `en-MX`. Only one of the eight pages points back, so `pruneUnconfirmedAlternates` removes most of it. That is harmless today, but the sitemap source claims things it does not emit. A future edit to the pruner could expose about 150 wrong annotations.
- **Diff:** Make each landing self-referencing and use the `addForLocaleOnly` helper that already exists.
```diff
-  seoLandings.forEach(({ locale, path, img }) => {
-    routes.push({ ... alternates: { languages: { 'x-default': ..., 'en-IL': ..., /* 16 region entries */ } } ... });
-  });
+  seoLandings.forEach(({ locale, path, img }) => {
+    addForLocaleOnly(routes, path, { lastModified: LANDINGS_UPDATED, changeFrequency: 'weekly', priority: 0.95, images: [`${BASE_URL}/og-image-${img}.webp`] }, locale);
+  });
```
- Keep the region-code cluster only on the one `en` showcase page, where it is actually reciprocal.

## Not auditable from what I was given

```diff
-curl -X POST "https://ssl.bing.com/webmaster/api.svc/json/SubmitUrlbatch?apikey=c13df4f2164643cabd986b652269b1c5" ...
+curl -X POST "https://ssl.bing.com/webmaster/api.svc/json/SubmitUrlbatch?apikey=$BING_WEBMASTER_KEY" ...
```
Also check git history for the old key.

## 6. `JsonLd` does not escape `</script>`
`fe-next/components/seo/JsonLd.tsx:18`

- **Problem:** plain `JSON.stringify` is injected into a `<script>` tag. Any string containing `</script>` breaks the page and can inject markup. The doc comment says input is static, but callers such as blog and WOTD pages pass translated or content-file strings.
- **Impact:** a stray `<` in a translation could invalidate the whole JSON-LD block, which loses rich results on that URL.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-      dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(data) }}
+      dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(data).replace(/</g, '\\u003c') }}
```
Apply the same change at `VideoGameJsonLd.tsx:78`.

## 7. The mode-landing CTA defaults to the English label "Play"
`fe-next/components/seo/ModeLandingPlayButton.tsx:22`

- **Problem:** the `label = 'Play'` default means any caller that omits `label` ships English text on localized mode landings. The tests only ever render it with the default.
- **Impact:** a weak, generic anchor on the pages meant to convert organic landings. It also adds a mixed-language signal on he, sv, ja and es pages.
- **Diff:** make `label` required so TypeScript forces every call site to pass a localized string:
```diff
-  label?: string;
+  label: string;
...
-export function ModeLandingPlayButton({ mode, href, label = 'Play' }: ModeLandingPlayButtonProps) {
+export function ModeLandingPlayButton({ mode, href, label }: ModeLandingPlayButtonProps) {
```

`fe-next/components/seo/SvScrabbleCrossLink.tsx:12, 24`

- **Problem:** `docs/seo-daily/2026-05-08.md:163` replaced "Spela Scrabble" in the SV title with the "TM-safe" "Alternativ". The internal-link anchors still say "Spela Scrabble online…" and "Spela Scrabble Online Svenska Gratis".
- **Impact:** this keeps the trademark risk. It also splits the anchor signal between "Scrabble online svenska", which ranks at about pos 10 with rising impressions, and the "alternativ" framing now used on the target page.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-    text: 'Spela Scrabble online på svenska — gratis multiplayer',
+    text: 'Scrabble-alternativ online på svenska — gratis multiplayer',
...
-    text: 'Spela Scrabble Online Svenska Gratis',
+    text: 'Scrabble Online Svenska — gratis alternativ',
```
Then update the regexes in `__tests__/SvScrabbleCrossLink.test.tsx:17`. Currently `/Scrabble.*svenska|ordspel/i` still matches the new text.

## 9. Education pages have zero impressions and the planned schema is still undone
`fe-next/docs/seo-daily/2026-05-07.md:129–131, 145`

- **Problem:** `vocabulary-games-classroom`, `esl-word-games` and `games-for-teachers` have 0 impressions. The `LearningResource` and `FAQPage` JSON-LD item is still unchecked.

```tsx
<JsonLd data={{
  '@context': 'https://schema.org',

  name: title,
  description,
  inLanguage: locale,
  educationalUse: 'Vocabulary practice',
  audience: { '@type': 'EducationalAudience', educationalRole: 'teacher' },
  isAccessibleForFree: true,
  url: `https://www.lexiclash.live/${locale}/education/${slug}`,
}} />
```
Pair it with the planned internal links from `/teacher` and the blog.

# SEO and agent-legibility audit for lexiclash.live

**Scope caveat:** the files provided are SEO daily reports, one spec, tests and unrelated hooks. I did not see `app/sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, `llms.txt/content.ts`, `isCrawler.ts`, any `page.tsx`, or `generatePageMetadata.ts`. Findings below cite what the reports and tests show. Diffs against unseen files are marked **(sketch)** and need checking against the real file.

Current baseline (`seo-daily/2026-05-12.md:7-11`): Google has 42 clicks on 1,544 impressions, and Bing has 56 clicks on 1,101 impressions. Bing is 57% of clicks, so Bing indexing and AI-answer surfaces matter more than a small-site instinct would suggest.

---

## 1. IndexNow submissions are malformed, so Bing is probably not getting your updates
**`fe-next/docs/seo-daily/2026-05-12.md:91-102`, `fe-next/docs/seo-daily/2026-05-10.md:166-167`**

- **Host mismatch.** `"host":"lexiclash.live"` is paired with URLs on `www.lexiclash.live`. IndexNow requires the host to match the URL host, so these requests are likely rejected with 422 (or 403 if the key file doesn't match).
- **Invalid JSON.** The 05-12 payload ends in `]}}`, one brace too many.
- **Duplicates.** The same ES URL appears 6 times.
- **No `keyLocation`.** None is given.
- **Why it matters.** Section 4 of each report shows every Google top-10 ES query as "absent" on Bing. The `המילה היומית` query is already your best Bing result at position 1.
- **Impact:** blocks Bing and Copilot coverage of about 600 ES impressions, and Bing is your higher-CTR engine (5.09%).

```diff
-  -d '{"host":"lexiclash.live","key":"<your-indexnow-key>","urlList":[
-    "https://www.lexiclash.live/es/juego-de-palabras-multijugador",
-    ... (x6 duplicates)
-  ]}}' | jq .
+  -d '{"host":"www.lexiclash.live","key":"<key>",
+       "keyLocation":"https://www.lexiclash.live/<key>.txt",
+       "urlList":[
+    "https://www.lexiclash.live/es/juego-de-palabras-multijugador",
+    "https://www.lexiclash.live/sv/swedish-multiplayer-word-game"
+  ]}' | jq .
```

Also dedupe in the generator script (`sort -u`) and confirm `public/<key>.txt` is served at the `keyLocation` URL with a 200.

## 2. The "best word games" FAQ was put on the wrong page
**`2026-05-10.md:197-207` versus `2026-05-10.md:157`**

- **What shipped.** Three FAQ entries for "best word games 2026" and "best free browser word games" went onto `/en/leaderboard`.
- **Where the demand is.** `/en/best-online-word-games` is the page that matches those queries. It has 453 impressions, 1 click and 0.22% CTR at position 8.0, the largest zero-CTR page in the report.
- **Why it matters.** AI engines cite the page whose topic matches the question. A leaderboard page answering "best word games" is a weak match. The report itself says position 5.5 with 0 clicks on the leaderboard looks like SERP-feature interception, not a metadata problem.
- **Impact:** the highest-impression English page earns no citations or clicks, and the FAQ you just wrote sits on a page that can't win.

**(sketch)** In `app/[locale]/best-online-word-games/page.tsx`, add the same entries to its FAQ source and let it emit `FAQPage` plus `ItemList` JSON-LD:
```diff
+ { q: 'What are the best word games of 2026?', a: '<≤50-word answer naming LexiClash plus 3-4 alternatives, with one concrete differentiator each>' },
+ { q: 'What are the best free browser word games in 2025 and 2026?', a: '...' },
```
Keep a short cross-link from the leaderboard page, but don't duplicate the FAQ on both pages.

## 4. AI crawlers may not be covered by the crawler gate
**`fe-next/lib/seo/__tests__/isCrawler.test.ts:11-31`**

- **What the tests cover.** Only Googlebot variants and Bingbot. There is no case for GPTBot, OAI-SearchBot, ChatGPT-User, PerplexityBot, ClaudeBot, Google-Extended or Applebot.
- **Why it matters.** `isCrawlerUserAgent` is a gate that exists because bots hit a client-rendered shell. The home page also does a full-screen FTUE takeover for fresh users (`e2e/pages/OnboardingPage.ts:16-24, 44-52`). Non-JS AI fetchers get whatever the server HTML contains.
- **Impact:** if the gate is the mechanism that serves crawlable content, AI bots are being left out. That is the main thing blocking AI citations for the site.
- **Verification step:** `curl -A "GPTBot" https://www.lexiclash.live/en | grep -c "<h1"` and compare with the Googlebot UA.

**(sketch)** `lib/seo/isCrawler.ts`:
```diff
-const CRAWLER_RE = /googlebot|bingbot|.../i;
+const CRAWLER_RE = /googlebot|bingbot|gptbot|oai-searchbot|chatgpt-user|perplexitybot|claudebot|claude-web|google-extended|applebot|duckassistbot|.../i;
```
Add the matching test cases. Make sure `robots.ts` allows these UAs explicitly. Do not serve materially different content to them, because that is cloaking. Use the same SSR content for everyone.

## 6. Pasted FAQ stubs contain placeholder answers
**`2026-05-12.md:128-131, 144-147, 160-163`**

- **The stubs.** The generated `FAQPage` JSON-LD has `"text": "<!-- Write a concise answer (<=50 words) targeting this query -->"`.
- **Risk.** If the generator output ever lands in a page as-is, it ships an HTML-comment answer as schema, which is invalid and can trigger manual-action or spam signals. The question name is also the raw query rather than a question.
- **Fix:** make the generator refuse to emit stubs, and write real answers.

```diff
-"name": "best free browser word games 2026",
-"text": "<!-- Write a concise answer (<=50 words) targeting this query -->"
+"name": "What are the best free browser word games in 2026?",
+"text": "LexiClash, Wordle and Boggle-style games are the most popular free browser word games in 2026. LexiClash adds real-time multiplayer, five languages and a daily challenge with no signup."
```
Add a CI guard: fail if any JSON-LD text contains `<!--`.

- **Impact:** low for week-one revenue, but cheap. It gives AI answers a reason to link to you.

  - **Provider (line 45):** `educationCourseJsonLd` uses a bare `Organization` instead of the `#org` reference.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-    provider: { '@type': 'Organization', name: 'LexiClash', url: BASE_URL },
+    provider: { '@id': `${BASE_URL}/${args.locale}/education#org` },
...
-    description: 'Classroom word games with native multilingual support, ad-free for students.',
+    // Drop description here; buildEducationOrgJsonLd already emits the localized one for the same @id.
```
Better still, delete the module and route callers to `educationLanding.ts` / `educationJsonLd.ts`.

### 8. `Course` schema invents an instructor and a workload
- **Where:** `fe-next/lib/seo/educationJsonLd.ts:147-157`
- **Impact:**
  - `instructor: Person "Classroom Teacher"` is a fabricated entity.
  - Combined with a 5-minute `courseWorkload`, it risks a "misleading structured data" flag.
  - A flag can disqualify the rich results on the whole hub.
  - A game is also not a Course in Google's definition.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-    hasCourseInstance: [
-      {
-        '@type': 'CourseInstance' as const,
-        courseMode: 'Online',
-        courseWorkload: 'PT5M',
-        instructor: { '@type': 'Person', name: 'Classroom Teacher' },
-      },
-    ],
+    hasCourseInstance: [{ '@type': 'CourseInstance' as const, courseMode: 'Online' }],
```
`WebApplication` and `LearningResource` already carry the real signal.

### 9. Two JSON-LD serializers, and one lacks the U+2028/2029 escapes
- **Where:** `fe-next/lib/seo/wordLists/jsonLd.ts:16-18`, versus `leaderboardJsonLd.ts:35-42`
- **Impact:**
  - Word-list rows come from the database, and `serializeJsonLd` escapes `<`, `>` and `&` but not the line and paragraph separators.
  - This is a robustness issue, not a ranking one.
  - If a list ever contains one of those characters, JSON-LD parsing can break and the structured data is silently lost on thousands of pages.
  - It is also a duplicate code path.
- **Diff:**
```diff
-export function serializeJsonLd(data: unknown): string {
-  return JSON.stringify(data).replace(/</g, '\\u003c').replace(/>/g, '\\u003e').replace(/&/g, '\\u0026');
-}
+export { encodeJsonLd as serializeJsonLd } from '@/lib/seo/leaderboardJsonLd';
```

### 10. Agent reachability cannot be confirmed from what I was given
- **Where:**
  - `fe-next/lib/seo/nextHandleGate.ts:212`
  - `fe-next/lib/seo/nextHandleGate.ts:256`
- **Observations:**
  - Per-locale `llms.txt` (`content.ts`, referenced in the tests) and a root `public/llms.txt` exist, and the library is listed in them.
  - I found no MCP server, public API doc, OpenAPI file, or CLI anywhere in these files. If one exists, it is not linked from `llms.txt`.
  - `PROBE_RE` at line 212 drops `/.well-known/security.txt`, which is a standard trust and discovery file.
  - Only `.well-known` as a public directory is allowed through, so any agent-discovery file you add there passes.
- **Impact:** an agent can read your content but has no machine route to create a room or fetch a word list.
- **Diff (security.txt):**
```diff
-...|admin\.php|\.well-known\/security\.txt)/i;
+...|admin\.php)/i;
```
Then serve a real `public/.well-known/security.txt`. If you have a room-creation or word-list endpoint, add a `## API` section to `public/llms.txt` with the OpenAPI URL.

**Priorities for first revenue:** fix #1 first (the pricing page's structured data), then #2 and #3 (cheap, and they affect every education page). Then #4 and #6 (Spanish, Swedish and Japanese click-through). I would hold #5 until you confirm nothing relies on the fallback.

## Shard 71

# SEO / GEO audit: LexiClash (`fe-next`)

**Scope caveat:** the files provided are `next.config.mjs`, `public/` assets, `llms.txt`, `llms-full.txt`, and one script. I did not see `app/**/layout.tsx`, `sitemap.ts`, `robots.ts`, or any JSON-LD component. So I could not audit titles, canonicals, hreflang, or structured data directly. I list those as unverified at the end instead of inventing diffs for them. Every finding below is anchored to a line I can see.

## 1. `llms.txt` and `llms-full.txt` list 5 languages in several places and omit Russian
**`public/llms.txt:180-186`, `:372`, `:290` (llms-full)**

- The Languages list stops at Spanish (`llms.txt:180-186`). `llms.txt:372` says "5 language support".
- `llms-full.txt:290` says "5 (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES)".
- The same files also say "6 languages" (`llms.txt:3`, `llms-full.txt:3`), and `next.config.mjs` routes `ru`.
- An AI answering a Russian-language query reads the file as unsupported and won't cite or recommend the `/ru` pages. Contradictory counts also lower trust in the whole file.

```diff
--- public/llms.txt
 - Spanish: https://www.lexiclash.live/es
+- Russian: https://www.lexiclash.live/ru
@@
-- 5 language support including Hebrew RTL
+- 6 language support including Hebrew RTL
--- public/llms-full.txt
-| Languages | 5 (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES) | 1 (English) |
+| Languages | 6 (EN, HE, SV, JA, ES, RU) | 1 (English) |
-- 5 language support including Hebrew RTL
+- 6 language support including Hebrew RTL
```

`llms.txt:372` and `:361` are the other "5 language" spots. Fix them the same way.

## 2. `llms.txt` points agents at retired and 404-prone URLs
**`public/llms.txt:237`, `:243`, `:348-351`**

- `/en/rules` (`:237`) is listed as "Rules / How to Play", while `llms-full.txt:230` uses `/en/how-to-play`. One of the two is likely wrong.
- `/en/practice`, `/en/practice/classic`, `/en/practice/wordHunt` and `/en/practice/wheelRush` (`:243`, `:348-351`) are recommended as the answer for "how to play boggle".
- `next.config.mjs:200-203` says Practice is retired and redirects it (`RETIRED_PRACTICE_REDIRECTS`). A redirect leaks equity and tells an agent the file is stale. The "how to play" intent also lands on a page that is no longer the tutorial.

```diff
--- public/llms.txt
-- Rules / How to Play: https://www.lexiclash.live/en/rules
+- Rules / How to Play: https://www.lexiclash.live/en/how-to-play
-- Practice / Tutorial Mode: https://www.lexiclash.live/en/practice
(delete line 243)
-| "how to play boggle" / "word game tutorial" / "learn word games" | /en/practice |
+| "how to play boggle" / "word game tutorial" / "learn word games" | /en/how-to-play |
(delete rows 349-351, or repoint them to /en/how-to-play)
```

Confirm which of `/rules` and `/how-to-play` is canonical before editing.

## 3. Per-locale `llms.txt` files are advertised, and Russian is missing
**`public/llms.txt:190-197`**

- The file lists `/en|he|sv|ja|es/llms.txt` but not `/ru/llms.txt`.
- I can't confirm those routes exist. `next.config.mjs:351` catch-all redirects non-locale paths, and a locale-prefixed `/llms.txt` may 404. If the handlers are missing, five dead links are advertised to crawlers.
- Check with `curl -I https://www.lexiclash.live/he/llms.txt`.
- If it 404s, either add `app/[locale]/llms.txt/route.ts` or drop the section.
- If it works, add the `ru` line:

```diff
 - Spanish: https://www.lexiclash.live/es/llms.txt
+- Russian: https://www.lexiclash.live/ru/llms.txt
```

## 4. The agent-facing file says "Completely free with no paywalls" and "Free tier is unlimited", but pricing says otherwise
**`public/llms.txt:375`, `public/llms-full.txt:275`, `:101`**

- `llms.txt:375` says "Completely free with no paywalls". `llms-full.txt:275` says the same.
- Elsewhere the same files state Teacher Pro at $9/month and a school tier at $49 per teacher per year (`llms.txt:120-122`).
- `llms.txt:121` explicitly warns agents not to claim the free tier is unlimited. Contradicting it two sections later makes assistants quote the wrong price.
- This is the file that decides what AI engines tell teachers, who are your only visible revenue path.

```diff
--- public/llms.txt
-- Completely free with no paywalls
+- Free for players. Teacher Pro ($9/mo) and school plans are optional paid tiers for educators.
--- public/llms-full.txt
-- Completely free with no paywalls
+- Free for players. Optional Teacher Pro ($9/mo) and school plans for educators.
```

## 5. `llms.txt` is long and has no priority order
**`public/llms.txt:1-438`**

- It is about 440 lines. Roughly 300 are education queries, and the query-to-URL table alone is about 65 rows.
- The top-level consumer pitch, "Words With Friends alternative", is buried at `:252`.
- Agents that truncate a file will only see Game Modes and Education.
- The "Education" block repeats at `:77` and `:377`.
- Keep `llms.txt` to about 100 lines of curated links. Move the query table and the duplicated education section into `llms-full.txt`.
- No diff is given because this is a restructure. The minimum change is to delete `:377-437` (the duplicate "# Education" block) and merge any unique rows into the first one.

## 6. An agent cannot reach the product programmatically
**No evidence of `/.well-known/mcp`, an OpenAPI spec, or a public API in what I was given.**

- `llms.txt` describes only web pages. `next.config.mjs:543-569` shows API routes exist, but only as caching headers for internal helpers.
- The one thing a teacher agent would act on is to create a classroom room and get a join code. It has no documented endpoint.
- The word-list snapshot (`scripts/seo/snapshot-word-lists.ts`) shows a public, structured content asset that suits a read-only API.
- Cheapest first step: a documented read-only `GET /api/public/word-lists` plus an `## API` section in `llms.txt`.
- This is lower priority than the fixes above. I'd only build an MCP server after the content fixes.

```diff
+## API (read-only)
+- Word lists: https://www.lexiclash.live/api/public/word-lists?lang=en&grade=3
+- Daily puzzle metadata: https://www.lexiclash.live/api/public/daily
```

This is a proposal, not an existing endpoint. Implement the routes first.

## 7. The word-list snapshot is stale-by-design, and the pages depend on a manual script
**`scripts/seo/snapshot-word-lists.ts:92-93`**

- The only input to the `/education/lists` pages is a checked-in JSON written by a manually run script.
- Those pages are advertised as a major long-tail asset (`llms.txt:112-116`).
- The `generatedAt` field is day-granular and never surfaced.
- Add `dateModified` to the page JSON-LD from `generatedAt`, and run the snapshot in CI weekly (cron). Otherwise freshness, a strong AI-citation signal, is invisible.
- The snapshot also silently drops teacher lists when `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is missing (`:64-66`). A CI run without the key would shrink the corpus with only a warning.

```diff
-    console.warn('teacher lists skipped: no service-role key (test-account filter needs it)');
+    if (process.env.CI) throw new Error('snapshot-word-lists: service-role key required in CI');
+    console.warn('teacher lists skipped: no service-role key (test-account filter needs it)');
```

## 8. Redirect rules can swallow `llms.txt`, `sitemap.xml` and `robots.txt`
**`next.config.mjs:350-360`**

- The catch-all excludes paths containing a dot via `.*\\..*` (`:351`). That is correct for the first rule.
- The nested rule (`:357`) applies the dot exclusion only to the first segment. A path like `/foo/llms.txt` slips through, and a locale-less `/he/llms.txt` is fine.
- The bigger risk is the non-www rule at `:311`, which exempts only `assetlinks.json`, the AASA file and one ad-verification txt.
- A request for `lexiclash.live/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml` or `/llms.txt` on the bare host 308s to `www`. Most crawlers follow it, but Google's robots.txt fetch tolerates only limited redirect hops, and strict verifiers (the ad-network case this config already works around) refuse it.
- The public verification files `207c6c1a…txt`, `adac41d3…txt` and `rafacoxz…txt` have the same exposure.

```diff
-source: '/:path((?!\\.well-known/assetlinks\\.json$|\\.well-known/apple-app-site-association$|d41d650cb226c9b4c235\\.txt$).*)',
+source: '/:path((?!\\.well-known/assetlinks\\.json$|\\.well-known/apple-app-site-association$|d41d650cb226c9b4c235\\.txt$|robots\\.txt$|sitemap\\.xml$).*)',
```

## 9. The `/` redirect to `/en` is a 301 that drops locale detection for crawlers
**`next.config.mjs:212-216`**

- The comment says the Express layer handles `/` in production: a locale-detecting 301 for browsers and a rewrite for bots.
- The fallback is a hard `permanent: true` to `/en`.
- A permanent redirect cached by browsers pins every future visit to `/en`, including users who should reach `/he`.
- Because the fallback can't be tested here, I'd make it `permanent: false` so a dev or fallback deployment can't poison browser caches.
- Impact is modest, and it matters only if the Express layer is ever bypassed.

```diff
       source: '/',
       destination: '/en',
-      permanent: true,
+      permanent: false,
```

## 10. Large public markdown and promo files ship in the public web root
**`public/images/promo/ready-to-post/PASTE-GUIDE*.md`, `public/images/**/MANIFEST.md`**

- Internal social-copy guides, MANIFESTs and generator paths (`/tmp/wt2/art/...`) are publicly crawlable.
- The promo guides contain unverified statistics. Examples are "Memory improves 23% after 8 weeks (Univ. of Michigan)" and "NEJM 2019" at `PASTE-GUIDE.md:18-21`. They sit on your own domain, where an AI crawler could ingest and cite them as LexiClash claims.
- Move them out of `public/` (for example to `docs/`), or at minimum disallow `/images/promo/` in robots.
- No diff is given for robots because I did not see it. The simplest fix is `git mv fe-next/public/images/promo/ready-to-post fe-next/docs/promo`.

## Not verified (files not provided)

- **Titles, descriptions, canonical, hreflang:** `next.config.mjs:181` says `/singleplayer` canonicals to `/en/singleplayer`, which is correct. I can't confirm the `x-default` or locale alternates on every page. Check `generateMetadata` for `alternates.languages` including `ru`. Given item 1, `ru` may have been left out of hreflang as well.
- **JSON-LD:** `llms.txt:86` claims FAQPage on `/education/games-for-teachers`. Validate with the Rich Results Test. Add `SoftwareApplication` or `WebApplication` with `offers` to the home page if it is absent.
- **`sitemap.ts` and `robots.ts`:** I could not confirm the `Sitemap:` line or the preview-environment noindex coverage. The preview noindex header at `next.config.mjs:578` is correct, but it keys off `NEXT_PUBLIC_IS_PREVIEW`. That variable must not be set in production.
- **Client-only rendering:** `PlayerInGameView` (the one test shown) is app UI, so nothing there needs indexing. Spot-check that `/education/lists/*` word and definition content appears in the server HTML via `curl`, not only after hydration.

**Scope:** the files supplied don't include `app/sitemap.ts`, `public/robots.txt`, `public/llms.txt`, `[locale]/layout.tsx` (where the canonical, hreflang and JSON-LD wrappers live), or any API/MCP/CLI code. I can't verify those, so I don't claim bugs there. I list them at the end as "unverified".

## 1. Russian pages get the English HowTo schema (`fe-next/utils/seoLocalizedSchema.ts:7,20-136`)
- **Problem:** `layout.ts:557` ships a full `ru` locale, and the site advertises "6 languages". But `SupportedLocale` and `STRINGS` have only 5 entries, so `getLocalizedSchemaStrings('ru')` falls back to English (line 136). Russian pages carry English JSON-LD that doesn't match the page language.
- **Impact:** a language mismatch between schema and page weakens Yandex, Bing and AI-engine entity matching for the Russian market. The test (`seoLocalizedSchema.test.ts:10`) also omits `ru`, so nothing catches it.

```diff

+  },
```
Also change `seoLocalizedSchema.test.ts:10` to include `'ru'`.

## 2. Sitewide HowTo schema is mostly wasted, and there is no app entity (`seoLocalizedSchema.ts:1-5`)
- **Problem:** the header says HowTo "drives rich-result eligibility". Google removed HowTo rich results in 2023, so the root-layout HowTo earns no SERP feature.
